# **IN THE UNITED STATES BANKRUPTCY COURT FOR THE DISTRICT OF DELAWARE** 

In re: Chapter 11 CHARACTER TECHNOLOGIES, INC.,<sup>1</sup> Case No. 26-11603 (BLS) Debtor. 

**DEBTOR’S MOTION PURSUANT TO SECTIONS 105, 363 AND 365 OF THE BANKRUPTCY CODE FOR ENTRY OF (I) AN ORDER (A) APPROVING BIDDING PROCEDURES AND BID PROTECTIONS, (B) SCHEDULING CERTAIN DATES AND DEADLINES WITH RESPECT THERETO, (C) APPROVING THE FORM AND MANNER OF NOTICE THEREOF, (D) APPROVING THE DESIGNATION OF DISNEY WORLDWIDE SERVICES, INC. AS STALKING HORSE PURCHASER WITH RESPECT TO THE DISNEY TARGET ASSETS, (E) AUTHORIZING THE DEBTOR TO DESIGNATE ADDITIONAL STALKING HORSE PURCHASERS FOR THE ASSETS AND (F) ESTABLISHING NOTICE AND PROCEDURES FOR THE ASSUMPTION AND ASSIGNMENT OF CONTRACTS AND LEASES; AND (II) AN ORDER (A) AUTHORIZING AND APPROVING THE SALE OF ASSETS, (B) APPROVING THE ASSUMPTION OF THE TSA AND THE ASSUMPTION AND ASSIGNMENT OF THE ASSUMED EXECUTORY CONTRACTS** **<u>AND UNEXPIRED LEASES AND (C) GRANTING RELATED RELIEF</u>** 

Character Technologies, Inc., the debtor and debtor in possession in the above-captioned chapter 11 case (the “Debtor”), by and through its undersigned proposed counsel, hereby submits this motion (this “Motion”) for entry of an order granting the relief described below.  In support hereof, the Debtor relies on the _Declaration of Colin M. Adams in Support of the Debtor’s Chapter 11 Petition and First Day Motions_ (the “First Day Declaration”)<sup>2</sup> and the _Declaration of Adam_

# **<u>JURISDICTION AND VENUE</u>** 

1. The United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “Court”) has 

jurisdiction to consider this Motion under 28 U.S.C. §§ 157 and 1334 and the _Amended Standing Order of Reference_ from the United States District Court for the District of Delaware dated February 29, 2012.  Pursuant to Rule 9013-1(f) of the Local Rules of the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “Local Rules”), the Debtor confirms its consent to the entry of a final order by the Court in connection with this Motion to the extent that it is later determined that the Court, absent consent of the parties, cannot enter final orders or judgments in connection herewith consistent with Article III of the United States Constitution. 

2. This is a core proceeding pursuant to 28 U.S.C. § 157(b)(2).  Venue of this chapter 11 case and this Motion is proper in this district under 28 U.S.C. §§ 1408 and 1409. 

3. The statutory bases for the relief requested herein are sections 105, 363, and 365 of title 11 of the United States Code, as amended (the “Bankruptcy Code”), Rules 2002, 6004 and 6006 of the Federal Rules of Bankruptcy Procedure (the “Bankruptcy Rules”), and Local Rules 2002-1 and 6004-1. 

# **<u>RELIEF REQUESTED</u>** 

4. By this Motion, the Debtor seeks entry of: (a) an order (the “Bidding Procedures 

<u>Order”) substantially in the form attached hereto as</u> **<u>Exhibit A:</u>** 

- a. authorizing and approving certain bidding procedures (the “Bidding <u>Procedures”), substantially in the form attached to the Motion as Exhibit 1, for</u> the sale, license or other disposition (the “Sale”) of all or substantially all of the Debtor’s assets (the “Assets”);

- b. authorizing and approving certain bid protections (the “Bid Protections”), including a break-up fee and expense reimbursement of $700,000 (the “Break- <u>Up Fee”) for Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. (“Disney”) as the stalking horse</u> purchaser with respect to certain of the assets (the “Disney Target Assets”) as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement (the “Stalking Horse <u>Transaction Agreement”) attached to this Motion as</u> **<u>Exhibit B</u>** ; 

   - c. authorizing the Debtor to designate additional stalking horse purchasers (each, an “Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser” and together with Disney, the “Stalking Horse Purchasers”) and additional stalking horse agreements (each, an “Additional Stalking Horse Agreement”), with respect to all or any subset of the Assets, other than the Disney Target Assets, and to offer the Bid Protections to any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser; 

   - d. scheduling a hearing (the “Sale Hearing”) for approval of the Sale and approval of other deadlines related to the same; 

   - e. authorizing the assumption and assignment of certain executory contracts and unexpired leases (the “Assigned Contracts”) in connection therewith; 

   - f. authorizing and approving certain assumption and assignment procedures for the Assigned Contracts provided for herein (collectively, the “Assumption and <u>Assignment Procedures”); and</u> 

   - g. granting related relief. 

5. The Debtor further requests the entry of an order (the “Sale Order”): 

   - a. authorizing and approving the Sale to the Winning Bidder(s), free and clear of all Encumbrances (as defined in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, if applicable, or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any other Alternative Agreement(s) of the Winning Bidder as applicable) other than Assumed Liabilities (as defined in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable); 

   - b. authorizing and approving the assumption of the TSA (as defined below) if Disney is the Winning Bidder and the assumption and assignment of the Purchased Contracts (as defined below) to the Winning Bidder(s); and 

   - c. granting related relief. 

# **<u>BACKGROUND</u>** 

# **A. General Background** 

6. On October 12, 2026 (the “Petition Date”), the Debtor filed a voluntary petition for 

relief pursuant to chapter 11 of the Bankruptcy Code.  The Debtor is operating its business and managing its property as debtor in possession pursuant to sections 1107(a) and 1108 of the

Bankruptcy Code.  No request for the appointment of a trustee or examiner has been made in this chapter 11 case, and no official committee of unsecured creditors has been appointed in this chapter 11 case. 

7. The Debtor is a first in kind artificial intelligence (“AI”) entertainment platform that allows users to have immersive, multimodal experiences with fictional or imagined characters. What started as a platform to have one-on-one chats tailored to user preferences has evolved to become a category leader in entertainment AI. Today, the Character.AI platform generates stunning visuals that become part of a story, creates dynamic video content with AI characters, and actively augments videos and stories with rich audio experiences to enhance immersion. In its four-year lifespan as an active platform, Character has amassed a large and engaged user base. The burgeoning and evolving field of AI entertainment, however, has proven to be costly to develop and grow. These high costs, coupled with potential litigation-related liabilities and regulatory engagement have contributed to the Debtor’s inability to generate sufficient cash flows to support its business in the present form. 

8. The Debtor has commenced this case to engage in value-maximizing sale transactions for the benefit of its stakeholders.  The Debtor enters this case with the benefit of Disney (i) serving as the Stalking Horse Purchaser and setting a baseline bid for the sale of certain of its Assets and (ii) having agreed to provide transition services with the assistance of certain of the Debtor’s former employees that Disney has hired. The Debtor intends to run a postpetition marketing and sale process for the benefit of stakeholders to solicit higher or better offers. If such offers are unavailable, or if the transaction with Disney is not approved, Character’s platform will be wound down.

9. The events leading up to the Petition Date and the facts and circumstances 

supporting the relief requested herein are set forth in the First Day Declaration, filed contemporaneously herewith. 

# **B. The Sale Process** 

10. As detailed in the First Day Declaration, the Debtor is in a precarious financial situation, largely caused by costly capital expenditures but accelerated by litigation and a changing regulatory environment. As a result, over a year prior to the Petition Date, the Debtor began searching for strategic or financial acquirors or partners to provide a path to continued operations of Character.AI. 

11. In connection with these efforts, in August 2025, the Debtor engaged an investment banker (the “Prior Banker”) to pursue strategic and financing alternatives in parallel. As part of this process, the Prior Banker oversaw the marketing of the Debtor’s assets and approached approximately 28 potential parties (largely strategic and financial parties). Character and the Prior Banker received consistent feedback in this process that, although Character’s product and engaged user base were impressive, the litigation and regulatory overhang on Character was too great. The Prior Banker’s outreach resulted in only one proposal that was ultimately not deemed to be actionable. Thereafter, the strategic process continued in consultation with the Prior Banker, while the Debtor focused on engaging with strategic partners and undertaking operational changes to improve revenue and reduce costs. The Prior Banker’s engagement with the Debtor concluded in August 2026. 

12. By September 2026, the Debtor, through a special transaction committee (the “Initial Transaction Committee”), comprised solely of independent directors on the Debtor’s board of directors (the “Board”), which was delegated authority to advise the Board in connection with

entry into a strategic transaction, engaged in discussions primarily with two parties who expressed interest in strategic transactions with Character.  The Initial Transaction Committee considered proposals from both parties, including the relevant value that each would bring, the likelihood of the proposals being actionable, Character’s ability to safely operate its platform after a transaction, the path for Character after a transaction to further maximize value, and the overall benefit to Character and its stakeholders. Character engaged in rounds of negotiations with both parties and the Initial Transaction Committee discussed the same over multiple meetings.  During this time, Character faced mounting employee attrition, which posed a genuine operational risk because Character cannot operate the Character.AI platform without key personnel to monitor and ensure user safety at all times. The market for AI talent is extremely competitive, with companies offering considerable compensation and equity packages, while the equity upside at Character had become increasingly uncertain and Character’s cash position and continual cash burn left only a limited runway. This attrition would have jeopardized the Debtor’s ability to continue operating and to pursue a going-concern marketing process, and the Debtor had significant concerns that it may need to liquidate on an expedited basis absent a stabilizing solution. 

13. Ultimately, the Initial Transaction Committee determined that the proposal from Disney was a superior proposal for several reasons. First, Disney’s accommodation and agreement to structure its proposed transaction into two interrelated and integrated stages, one prepetition and one postpetition, allowed Character to receive an immediate capital infusion pursuant to that certain exclusivity and prepayment letter agreement (the “EPA”) and reduce its personnel costs promptly by transitioning certain of its employees to Disney, while also retaining access to key employees through transition services provided by Disney to assist with safely running Character’s platform for a limited period. These services were memorialized and agreed to pursuant to that

certain Transition Services Agreement (as amended, modified, or restated from time to time, the “TSA”). As part of the immediate capital infusion, Disney agreed to pay, pursuant to the EPA, $7,500,000 of the $20,000,000 cash consideration up-front, $2,000,000 of which serves as a refundable good-faith deposit. Between the up-front payment and the TSA, Disney’s accommodations facilitated a path forward to maximize value for all stakeholders through a chapter 11 filing. Given the Debtor’s diminishing cash reserve and significant litigation and regulatory risks, Disney required that the transaction be fully consummated in a chapter 11 sale process. Disney’s transaction will be subject to higher and better offers through a sale process conducted by Houlihan Lokey (“Houlihan”), which the Debtor believes has the necessary experience, reputation, and global buyer network to support such process. 

14. The Initial Transaction Committee finalized the terms with Disney in the EPA and the TSA. Thereafter, the Initial Transaction Committee recommended final approval of the EPA and the TSA to the Board and, as the initial, prepetition stage of the overall transaction, Character signed the agreements on September 18, 2026. 

15. Pursuant to the terms of the EPA, the Debtor and Disney agreed upon a term sheet that contemplates a comprehensive transaction whereby, among other things, (i) Disney agreed to hire the majority of its workforce, including the Debtor’s chief executive officer, up front (subject to certain employees remaining available to assist with the Debtor’s operations, sale process, and wind down through the TSA), (ii) the Debtor agreed to release Disney and the transferring employees from certain matters, (iii) Disney agreed to pay the Debtor a $7,500,000 up-front payment prior to the Petition Date, of which $2,000,000 is serving as Disney’s good faith refundable deposit, which represents 37.5% of the overall cash consideration , and (iv) the Debtor agreed to negotiate exclusively with Disney with respect to the subset of assets it seeks to acquire

(the “Disney Target Assets”) from September 18, 2026 through the Petition Date. The vast majority of the Debtor’s employees offered roles at Disney accepted their offers. Under the TSA, those employees continue to assist the Debtor with, among other things, operating its business, the Sale Process, and its wind down, for up to five months following September 18, 2026. To preserve a competitive process, Disney covenanted to make certain newly hired employees available to competing qualified bidders for diligence and, if Disney is not the Winning Bidder, to permit the Winning Bidder to interview and extend offers to up to 80% of such employees who wish to consider opportunities with that bidder, and to work in good faith to transition those who accept. This structure facilitated a value-maximizing Sale Process and avoided what the Debtor feared could be a precipitous and value-destructive liquidation due to sudden employee attrition. The releases in the EPA and the exclusivity period, together with the Break-Up Fee, were critical to Disney’s agreement to serve as Stalking Horse Purchaser, and the exclusivity period terminated by its terms upon the Petition Date. 

16. Thereafter, the Debtor and Disney negotiated and entered into an amended version of the TSA and a stalking horse agreement with respect to the Disney Target Assets (the “Stalking <u>Horse Transaction Agreement”), which implements the second, postpetition stage of the overall</u> transaction. The Disney Target Assets include a perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive license to certain of the Debtor’s intellectual property and related technology and the purchase of unreleased claims (if any) against the newly hired employees.  The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement is proposed to serve as a baseline bid for prospective bidders to negotiate from and will be subject to higher and otherwise better bids for the Disney Target Assets pursuant to the Bidding Procedures; prospective bidders may also seek to acquire the Debtor’s other Assets. The Stalking Horse

Transaction Agreement also includes certain case milestones related to the Sale Process (as defined below). 

17. Also in September 2026, the Debtor engaged Houlihan to serve as its investment banker to assist in canvassing the market for interested buyers of the Assets. Upon its retention, Houlihan immediately began conducting due diligence with respect to the Assets and preparing marketing materials. Given that the Debtor’s Assets were marketed for over a year prepetition— both with the assistance of the Prior Banker and also independently by the Debtor itself—and will be marketed again in this chapter 11 case by Houlihan, the Debtor determined in its business judgment that the terms of Disney’s bid maximized the value of its Assets. 

18. As noted above, the EPA contained a short period for the parties to negotiate exclusively among themselves through the filing of the chapter 11 case to negotiate certain of the definitive documents. To augment the extensive prepetition marketing process for the Assets, which resulted in a stalking horse bid for the Disney Target Assets, and contemporaneous with the filing of this chapter 11 case, Houlihan has commenced its marketing process for the Assets, including circulating a “teaser” to various prospective buyers. The teaser includes a brief description of the Assets and the process outlined for the proposed sale, license, or disposition of the Debtor’s Assets (the “Sale Process”) and was accompanied by a form non-disclosure agreement (an “NDA”). In addition, prior to the Petition Date, in preparation for the Sale Process, Houlihan populated an electronic data room (the “Data Room”) with related diligence information. This “teaser” and access to the Data Room will be made available to Potential Bidders (defined below) upon execution of an NDA. 

19. In furtherance of Houlihan’s ongoing efforts to actively market the Assets for sale, and consistent with the milestones and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, the Debtor has

filed this Motion seeking authority to proceed with a bidding and auction process to consummate a sale that the Debtor expects will generate maximum value for its Assets. To facilitate the Sale, the Debtor, in consultation with Houlihan and its other professional advisors, has developed the Bidding Procedures to preserve flexibility in the Sale Process, generate the greatest level of interest in the Assets, and obtain the highest or otherwise best value for the Assets. Among other things, these procedures, in the Debtor’s business judgment, create an appropriate timeline for the Sale Process, consistent with the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement milestones. The Bidding Procedures also authorize, but do not require, the Debtor to designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers for all or any subset of the Assets. 

# **C. The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement** 

20. The Stalking Horse Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, which the Debtor and Disney have negotiated in good faith and at arm’s length, will serve as a baseline for all prospective bidders. In light of the capital infusion provided by Disney in the form of the up-front payment, the Debtor’s cash on hand provides sufficient runway to market its Assets. However, given the exigencies of the Debtor’s financial condition, its monthly burn rate, and because the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement includes an outside date for the Sale closing on or prior to December 17, 2026, a timely sale of the Assets, including the Disney Target Assets, in accordance with the milestones set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, is the best way to preserve value. The Debtor believes that the framework of the Stalking Horse Transaction, subject to higher or otherwise better offers, will maximize the value of the Assets and afford the best opportunity for continued service to the Debtor’s users and maintenance of relationships with its vendors.

21. The Debtor believes that the Stalking Horse Transaction offered by Disney and commencing this chapter 11 case to pursue a sale of the Assets provide the best opportunity to maximize value. 

22. As discussed throughout this Motion, the Bidding Procedures and the Sale timeline were designed with the objective of generating the greatest level of interest in and best value for the Assets, while allowing the Debtor to close the Sale (or Sales) in a timely and efficient manner. The Debtor and its professional advisors are confident that the Bidding Procedures and the other relief requested herein will facilitate the Sale of the Assets for the highest or otherwise best value. Finally, the auction and Sale Process contemplated by the Bidding Procedures will also serve as a market test to ensure that the Stalking Horse Transaction and any other sale transactions will maximize value. 

23. Among other things, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement contemplates that Disney will pay $20 million in cash (the “Stalking Horse Purchase Price”) to the Debtor, $7.5 million of which was paid prepetition pursuant to the EPA (including a $2 million refundable good-faith deposit). The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement also includes various customary representations, warranties, and covenants by and from the Debtor and Disney, and certain conditions to closing and rights of termination related to the Sale and the chapter 11 case generally. The transaction contemplated by the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement and assumption of the 

TSA is subject to approval by the Court and entry of the Bidding Procedures Order and the Sale Order.

> 5 As noted below, Disney hired certain Debtor employees with the consent of the Debtor (acting through the Initial Transaction Committee). One of these newly hired employees was the Debtor’s chief executive officer who was an “insider” of the Debtor within the meaning of section 101(31) of the Bankruptcy Code. Nonetheless, this should not change the standard of review because (i) no common identity of directors or controlling equity interest holders existed or exists between Disney, on the one hand, and the Debtor, on the other hand, (ii) the Initial Transaction Committee and the Restructuring Committee, as applicable, are comprised solely of independents and have led and recommended that the Board approve the proposed transaction with Disney, (iii) Disney did not and does not control the Debtor in any respect and (iv) none of the Debtor’s former insiders has had meaningful input in substantial matters related to this chapter 11 case or the Debtor’s sale process, including the proposed transaction with Disney.  At all times in advance of the chapter 11 case, the Initial Transaction Committee and the Restructuring Committee, as applicable, have rigorously performed their obligations and maintained their independence.

# **D. Designation of One or More Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers** 

25. Based on the Debtor’s decision to pursue strategic transactions through a chapter 11 process, and on the advice and counsel of the Debtor’s advisors, the Debtor has determined, in its reasonable business judgment, that having the flexibility to designate one or more Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers for the Assets will enhance the Debtor’s ability to maximize value of the Debtor’s Assets and is in the best interests of its estate. 

26. Accordingly, as set forth in greater detail in the Bidding Procedures, the Debtor requests authority to (i) designate one or more Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers in the event that the Debtor executes one or more stalking horse transactions for the Assets by November 9, 2026, and (ii) offer any such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser the following bid protections: payment of a break-up fee and expense reimbursement in an amount collectively capped at 3.5% of the cash purchase price, as an administrative expense, in the event that the Debtor consummates one or more sale transactions for the Assets included in the applicable Additional Stalking Horse Agreement with one or more other bidders. 

27. Given the urgency of the Debtor’s need to maximize value for creditors through a timely and efficient Sale, the ability to designate one or more Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers and offer such parties the Bid Protections is justified and appropriate. 

# **E. The Bidding Procedures** 

28. The Debtor is in the process of soliciting bids for all of the Assets, or any number or combination thereof, including the Disney Target Assets in accordance with the Bidding

Procedures. The Bidding Procedures describe, among other things, (i) the Assets available for sale, (ii) the manner in which bids become “qualified,” (iii) the coordination of diligence efforts among the bidders and the Debtor, (iv) the receipt and negotiation of bids received, (v) the conduct of any Auction, and (vi) the selection and approval of the Winning Bidder and the selection of any BackUp Bidder. The Bidding Procedures reflect the Debtor’s objective of conducting the Sale Process in an orderly, fair and open manner, while ensuring that the highest or otherwise best bid is generated for the Assets. 

29. Certain of the key terms of the Bidding Procedures, which shall apply to Potential Bidders, Qualifying Bidders, the submission, receipt, and analysis of all bids relating to the Sale, and the conduct of the Sale and the Auction, are included below: 

- a. **<u>Qualification as Bidder</u>** <u>. Any interested party that wishes to participate in the</u> bidding process for the Assets (each, a “Potential Bidder”) must first become a “Qualifying Bidder.” To become a Qualifying Bidder, and thus be able to conduct due diligence and gain access to the Debtor’s confidential electronic data room concerning the assets (the “Data Room”), a Potential Bidder must submit to the Debtor and its advisors: 

   - i. documentation identifying the Potential Bidder, its principals, and the representatives thereof who are authorized to appear and act on their behalf for all purposes regarding the contemplated bidding and transaction process; 

   - ii. an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance satisfactory to the Debtor; and 

   - iii. sufficient information, as reasonably determined by the Debtor, to allow the Debtor to determine, in its reasonable business judgment, that the interested party (i) has the financial wherewithal to consummate a Sale, and (ii) intends to access the Data Room for a _bona fide_ purpose consistent with these Bidding Procedures. 

- b. Each Potential Bidder shall comply with all reasonable requests for information and due diligence access by the Debtor or its advisors regarding the ability of such Potential Bidder to consummate its contemplated transaction. 

- c. **<u>Due Diligence</u>** <u>. The Debtor will provide any Qualifying Bidder (including, for</u> the avoidance of doubt, Disney) with reasonable access to the Data Room and any other additional information that the Debtor believes to be reasonable and

appropriate under the circumstances. All additional due diligence requests shall be directed to (x) the proposed investment banker for the Debtor, Houlihan Lokey, Attn: Daniel Cullins  (dcullins@HL.com) and (y) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., Attn: Heather Lambert (hlambert@wsgr.com). The due diligence period shall extend through and including the Bid Deadline. The Debtor may, but shall not be obligated to, in its business judgment, furnish any due diligence information after the Bid Deadline. The Debtor reserves the right, in its reasonable discretion, to withhold or limit access to any due diligence information that the Debtor determines is business-sensitive or otherwise not appropriate for disclosure to a Qualifying Bidder. Notwithstanding any prepetition limitations, including, without limitation, any non-disclosure, confidentiality or similar provisions relating to due diligence information, the Debtor and its estate shall be authorized to provide due diligence information to Qualifying Bidders provided that such Qualifying Bidders have delivered an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance acceptable to the Debtor. 

- d. **<u>Bid Requirements</u>** <u>.</u> _Qualifying Bid._ Other than in the case of Disney and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser and its Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, which shall be considered a Qualifying Bidder and a Qualifying Bid, respectively, for all purposes under the Bidding Procedures, without regard to any of the requirements or conditions set forth herein and without any other or further action by the Stalking Horse Purchaser, to be deemed a “Qualifying Bid,” a bid must be received from a Qualifying Bidder on or before the Bid Deadline and satisfy each of the following requirements (each, a “Bid Requirement”): 

   - i. be in writing; 

   - ii. fully disclose the identity of the Qualifying Bidder (and to the extent that the Qualifying Bidder is a newly formed acquisition entity or the like, the identity of the Qualifying Bidder’s parent company or sponsor), and provide the contact information of the specific person(s) whom the Debtor or its advisors should contact in the event that the Debtor has any questions or wishes to discuss the bid submitted by the Qualifying Bidder; 

   - iii. set forth the purchase price to be paid by such Qualifying Bidder; 

   - iv. not propose payment in any form other than cash; 

   - v. state the liabilities proposed to be paid or assumed by such Qualifying Bidder; 

   - vi. specify the Assets that are included in the bid and state that such Qualifying Bidder offers to (a) purchase or license the Assets, or a number or combination of the Assets, and (b) assume liabilities, upon substantially the same terms as, or terms more favorable to the Debtor

and its estate than, the terms set forth in, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- vii. be accompanied by an alternative asset purchase agreement (an “Alternative Agreement”) that reflects any variations from, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- viii. state that such Qualifying Bidder’s offer is formal, binding, and unconditional, and is irrevocable until two (2) business days after the closing of the Sale; 

- ix. to the extent that a Bid is not accompanied by evidence of the Qualifying Bidder’s capacity to consummate the sale, license or other disposition set forth in its Bid with cash on hand, each Bid must include unconditional committed financing from a reputable financing institution, documented to the satisfaction of the Debtor, that demonstrates that the Qualifying Bidder has: (i) received sufficient debt and/or equity funding commitments to satisfy the Qualifying Bidder’s purchase price and other obligations under its Bid; and (ii) adequate working capital financing or resources to finance going concern operations for the Assets and the proposed transactions. Such funding commitments or other financing must be unconditional and must not be subject to any internal approvals, syndication requirements, diligence, or credit committee approvals, and shall have covenants and conditions reasonably acceptable to the Debtor; 

- x. contain such financial and other information to allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to the Qualifying Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement, including, without limitation, such financial and other information supporting the Qualifying Bidder’s ability to comply with the requirements of adequate assurance of future performance under section 365(f)(2)(B) and, if applicable, section 365(b)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code, including the Qualifying Bidder’s financial wherewithal and willingness to perform under any contracts and leases that are assumed and assigned to the Qualifying Bidder, in a form that allows the Debtor to serve such information on any counterparties to any contracts or leases potentially being assumed and assigned in connection with the Sale within one (1) business day after the Debtor’s receipt of such information; 

- xi. (A) identify with particularity each and every executory contract and unexpired lease the assumption and assignment of which is a condition

to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement; (B) provide for the payment of cure costs related to such executory contracts and unexpired leases, and (C) demonstrate that the Qualifying Bidder can provide adequate assurance of future performance under all such executory contracts and unexpired leases; 

- xii. set forth the Qualifying Bidder’s intentions with respect to (i) hiring any of the Debtor’s current or former employees, and (ii) providing transition services to the Debtor; 

- xiii. include a commitment to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement by no later than November 30, 2026; 

- xiv. not request or entitle such Qualifying Bidder to any break-up fee, termination fee, expense reimbursement, or similar type of fee or payment unless such Qualifying Bidder is seeking to be designated as an Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser with respect to the Assets in its bid; 

- xv. if the Alternative Agreement contemplates the license or purchase of the Disney Target Assets or the purchase of Assets subject to any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, the aggregate consideration proposed by the Qualifying Bidder must equal or exceed the sum of the amount of (1) the value offered by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement with respect to the Disney Target Assets or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement with respect to any Assets included in the bid, plus (2) any applicable Break-Up Fee, plus (3) $1,000,000 (the “Minimum Overbid Amount”); 

- xvi. not contain any contingencies of any kind, including, without limitation, contingencies related to financing, internal approval, or due diligence; 

- xvii. contain a written acknowledgement and representation that the Qualifying Bidder (i) has had an opportunity to conduct any and all due diligence regarding the Assets, (ii) has relied solely upon its own independent review, investigation or inspection of any documents and other information in making its Qualifying Bid, and (iii) did not rely upon any written or oral statements, representations, promises, warranties or guaranties whatsoever, whether express, implied, by operation of law or otherwise, regarding the Assets, or the completeness of any documents or other information provided in connection with the Bidding Procedures and the Sale. Such written acknowledgment and representation must include the following disclaimer: Buyer acknowledges that, should the Closing occur, Buyer shall acquire the Assets without any surviving representations or warranties, on an “as is” and “where is” basis, except as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement;

- xviii. set forth each regulatory and third-party approval required for the Qualifying Bidder to consummate the Sale, and the time within which the Qualifying Bidder expects to receive such regulatory and third-party approvals, and the Debtor, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, may consider the timing of such approvals, and any actions the Qualifying Bidder will take to ensure receipt of such approvals as promptly as possible, when considering the other Bid Requirements. Each Qualifying Bidder must further agree that its legal counsel will coordinate in good faith with counsel to the Debtor to provide pertinent information regarding the Qualifying Bidder’s operations reasonably required to analyze issues arising with respect to any applicable regulatory requirements and to discuss and explain the Qualifying Bidder’s regulatory analysis, strategy, and timeline for securing all applicable approvals as soon as reasonably practicable; 

- xix. provide for the Qualifying Bidder to serve as a backup bidder (the “Back-Up Bidder”) if the Qualifying Bidder’s bid is the next highest and best bid (the “Back-Up Bid”) after the Winning Bid, in accordance with the terms of the Alternative Agreement; 

- xx. include written evidence of authorization and approval from the Qualifying Bidder’s board of directors (or comparable governing body) with respect to the submission, execution, and delivery of the Alternative Agreement; 

- xxi. provide a good faith cash deposit (the “Deposit”) in an amount equal to ten percent (10%) of the aggregate purchase price provided for in the Alternative Agreement (or such additional amount as may be determined by the Debtor in its reasonable discretion); provided, however, that Disney is not required to make an additional deposit; and 

- xxii. provide that in the event of the Qualifying Bidder’s breach of, or failure to perform under, the Alternative Agreement, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to pursue all available legal and equitable remedies, including, without limitation, retention of the Deposit as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform. 

A bid from a Qualifying Bidder satisfying all of the above requirements, as determined by the Debtor in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below), shall constitute a Qualifying Bid.  The Debtor reserves the right to work with any Qualifying Bidder in advance of the Auction to cure any deficiencies in a bid that is not initially deemed a Qualifying Bid. 

Each Qualifying Bidder submitting a bid shall be deemed to: (a) acknowledge and represent that it is bound by all of the terms and conditions of the Bidding Procedures; and (b) have waived the right to pursue a substantial contribution claim

under section 503 of the Bankruptcy Code related in any way to the submission of its bid, the Bidding Procedures, and the Sale. 

- e. **<u>Bid Deadline</u>** _<u>.</u>_ A Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser that may be designated by the Debtor, that desires to make a bid shall deliver a written and electronic copy of its bid in both PDF and MS-WORD format to the Bidding Procedures Notice Parties so as to be received on or before **<u>November 20, 2026 at 5:00 p.m. (ET)</u>** <u>(the “Bid Deadline”);</u> _provided_ that the Debtor may extend the Bid Deadline in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below) without further order of the Court.  To the extent that the Bid Deadline is extended for all parties, the Debtor shall file a notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case indicating the same.  Any party that does not submit a bid by the Bid Deadline will not be allowed to (x) submit any offer after the Bid Deadline, or (y) participate in the Auction. 

- f. **<u>Evaluation of Qualifying Bids</u>** <u>. The Debtor shall make a determination</u> whether a timely submitted bid from a Qualifying Bidder is a Qualifying Bid. No later than **November 21, 2026** , the Debtor shall: (i) notify all Qualifying Bidders whether their respective bids have been determined to be Qualifying Bids; and (ii) determine which of the Qualifying Bids, at such time, is the highest or best bid for purposes of constituting the opening bid of the Auction (which must be at least equal to the Minimum Overbid Amount, the “Baseline <u>Bid” and the Qualifying Bidder submitting the Baseline Bid, the “Baseline Bidder”), and shall promptly notify any Stalking Horse Purchaser and all</u> Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids of the Baseline Bid. 

- g. **<u>Consequence of No Qualifying Bids</u>** <u>. If no timely Qualifying Bids other than</u> any Stalking Horse Purchaser’s Qualifying Bid are submitted on or before the Bid Deadline, the Debtor shall not hold an Auction with respect to the Assets covered by the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement and shall request at the Sale Hearing that the Court approve such Stalking Horse Agreement and the transactions contemplated thereunder. 

- h. **<u>Auction</u>** <u>.</u> If the Debtor timely receives one or more Qualifying Bids for all or any subset of the Assets, including, without limitation, the Disney Target Assets, then the Debtor shall conduct an auction (the “Auction”).  Following the Auction, the Debtor will determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, which Qualifying Bid is the highest or otherwise best bid for the Assets, which will be determined by considering, among other things, the following non-binding factors: (a) the amount and nature of the consideration, including any assumed liabilities; (b) the number, type and nature of any changes requested by the bidder in the form of Alternative Agreement as compared to any Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets subject to its bid; (c) the extent to which such modifications are likely to delay closing of the Sale and the cost to the Debtor and its estate of such modifications or delay; (d) the total consideration to be received by the Debtor

and its estate; (e) the transaction structure and execution risk, including conditions to, timing of and certainty of closing, termination provisions, availability of financing and financial wherewithal to meet all commitments, and required governmental or other approval; (f) the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate; and (g) any other qualitative or quantitative factors the Debtor may reasonably deem appropriate under the circumstances. The Auction shall be governed by the following procedures: 

- i. the Auction shall be held on **<u>November 23, 2026 at 10:00 a.m. (ET)</u>** at a location to be determined by the Debtor or virtually via video conference pursuant to information to be timely provided by the Debtor to the Auction Participants (as defined below); 

- ii. only Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser(s) designated by the Debtor, and the other Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids (together, the “Auction Bidders”) shall be entitled to make any subsequent bids at the Auction; 

- iii. the Auction Bidders shall appear at the Auction in person (or via Zoom, if permitted) in the Debtor’s sole discretion, or through a duly authorized representative; 

- iv. only the Debtor, the Consultation Parties, and the Auction Bidders, together with the professional advisors to each of the foregoing parties, may attend the Auction (collectively, the “Auction Participants”); _provided_ that any creditors or parties in interest in the chapter 11 case may request permission in writing to attend the Auction no later than Noon (ET) two (2) days prior to the Auction and the Debtor, in its sole discretion, may reasonably determine to grant or deny such permission; 

- v. the Debtor and its professional advisors shall direct and preside over the Auction, which shall be transcribed; 

- vi. prior to the start of the Auction, each of the Auction Bidders shall confirm on the record of the proceedings that they have not engaged in any collusion with respect to the Bidding Procedures, the Auction, or the Sale; 

- vii. bidding shall commence at the amount of the Baseline Bid, and the Auction Bidders may submit successive bids in increments of $500,000, _provided_ that: (i) each successive bid be a Qualifying Bid; and (ii) the Debtor shall retain the right to modify the bid increment requirements at the Auction; 

- viii. in any successive bid by Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor, the amount of the applicable BreakUp Fee shall be included in the calculation of such bid;

- ix. the Auction may include individual negotiations with any of the Auction Bidders, but all bids shall be made on the record and in the presence of all of the Auction Bidders; 

- x. all material terms of the bid that is deemed to be the highest and best bid for each round of bidding shall be fully disclosed to the Auction Bidders, and the Debtor shall use reasonable efforts to clarify any and all questions that the Auction Bidders may have regarding the Debtor’s announcement of the then-current highest and best bid; 

- xi. the Debtor and its professional advisors may employ and announce at the Auction additional procedural rules that are reasonable under the circumstances ( _e.g._ , the amount of time allotted to make subsequent bids) for conducting the Auction, provided that such rules are (i) not inconsistent with the Bankruptcy Code, the Federal Rules of Bankruptcy Procedure, the Local Rules of the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware, or any applicable order of the Court, including, without limitation, the Bidding Procedures Order, and (ii) disclosed to the Auction Bidders in advance of their application; 

- xii. each Auction Bidder shall (i) be deemed to have waived any right to a jury trial in connection with, and consented and submitted to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court over, any actions or proceedings arising from or relating to the Bidding Procedures, the Sale, the Auction and the construction and enforcement of the contemplated transaction documents of the Auction Bidders, (ii) bring any such action or proceeding in the Court, and (iii) be deemed to have consented to the Court entering a final judgment determining any such action or proceeding and that such final judgment in any such action or proceeding, including all appeals, shall be conclusive and may be enforced in other jurisdictions (including any foreign jurisdictions) by suit on the judgment or in any other manner provided by applicable law; 

- xiii. the Auction Bidders shall have the right to make additional modifications to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, in conjunction with each Qualifying Bid submitted in each round of bidding during the Auction, _provided_ that (i) any such modifications on an aggregate basis and viewed in whole, shall not, in the Debtor’s discretion, be less favorable to the Debtor and its estate than the terms of, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, and (ii) each Qualifying Bid shall constitute an irrevocable offer and shall be binding on the Auction Bidder submitting such bid until such party shall have submitted a subsequent Qualifying Bid at the Auction or the conclusion of the Sale Hearing, whichever occurs sooner, unless such bid is selected

as the Winning Bid or the Back-Up Bid, which shall remain binding as provided for herein; 

- xiv. the Debtor shall have the right to request any additional financial information that will allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to an Auction Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to consummate the transactions contemplated by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as may be amended during the Auction, and any further information that the Debtor may believe is reasonably necessary to clarify and evaluate any bid made by an Auction Bidder during the Auction; 

- xv. upon the conclusion of the Auction, the Debtor shall determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties and subject to Court approval, the offer or offers for the Assets that is or are the highest or otherwise best from among the Qualifying Bids submitted at the Auction for all or substantially all or any subset of the Assets (the “Winning Bids”).  In making this decision, the Debtor shall consider the amount of the purchase price, the assumption of liabilities, the likelihood of the bidder’s ability to close a transaction and the timing thereof, the number, type and nature of any changes to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement submitted with the Winning Bid, as applicable, requested by each bidder, and the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate.  The bidder submitting such Winning Bid at the Auction shall become a “Winning Bidder,” and shall have such rights and responsibilities of the purchaser as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement, as applicable.  The Debtor may, in its business judgment, designate a BackUp Bid (and the corresponding Back-Up Bidder) to purchase the Assets in the event that the Winning Bidder does not close the Sale; 

- xvi. if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall allow one (1) Winning Bidder to interview and make employment offers to any employees of Disney formerly employed by the Debtor who wish to consider opportunities with such Winning Bidder and shall work in good faith with such Winning Bidder to transition employees who accept an offer to the Winning Bidder; _provided_ that Disney may designate up to 20% of such employees as outside the scope of such process, and Disney need not make available or allow the Winning Bidder (or any other party) the right to interview or make employment offers to such employees; 

- xvii. if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall be entitled to the Break-Up Fee;

- xviii. as soon as reasonably practicable after completion of the Auction, the Debtor will file with the Court and serve on the Transaction Notice Parties a notice setting forth the results of the Auction (the “Notice of <u>Winning Bidder”), which will (a) identify the Winning Bidder and the</u> Backup Bidder, if applicable; (b) include a copy of the Winning Bid and the Backup Bid, if applicable, or a summary of the material terms of such bids, including any proposed assumption and assignment of Contracts contemplated thereby; and (c) set forth the Adequate Assurance Objection Deadline, the date, time, and location of the Sale Hearing and any other relevant dates or other information necessary to reasonably apprise the Transaction Notice Parties of the outcome of the Auction; 

   - xix. prior to the Sale Hearing, the Winning Bidder shall complete and execute all agreements, contracts, instruments and other documents evidencing and containing the terms and conditions upon which the Winning Bid was made; 

      - **THE WINNING BID AND ANY BACK-UP BID AND THEIR RELATED STALKING HORSE TRANSACTION AGREEMENT, ADDITIONAL STALKING HORSE AGREEMENT, OR ALTERNATIVE AGREEMENT, AS APPLICABLE, SHALL CONSTITUTE AN IRREVOCABLE OFFER AND BE BINDING ON THE WINNING BIDDER AND THE BACK-UP BIDDER, RESPECTIVELY, FROM THE TIME THE BID IS SUBMITTED UNTIL TWO (2) BUSINESS DAYS AFTER THE SALE HAS CLOSED.  EACH QUALIFYING BID THAT IS NOT THE WINNING BID OR THE BACK-UP BID SHALL BE DEEMED WITHDRAWN AND TERMINATED AT THE CONCLUSION OF THE SALE HEARING.** 

- i. **<u>Sale Hearing</u>** <u>.</u> The Winning Bid and any Back-Up Bid and, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement related to such bids will be subject to approval by the Court.  The Sale Hearing to approve the Winning Bid and any Back-Up Bid and the applicable asset purchase agreements of such bids shall take place on **<u>November 24, 2026 at a time to be established by the Court (ET)</u>** <u>.</u> 

The Sale Hearing and any additional deadlines established in connection with the Bidding Procedures may be adjourned by the Debtor, in consultation with any Consultation Parties, Disney, and any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser, or in the event Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then in consultation with the Winning Bidder(s), from time to time without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest other than by announcement of the adjournment in open court on the date scheduled for the Sale Hearing, or by filing a hearing agenda or notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case.

At the Sale Hearing, the Debtor will seek entry of an order, substantially in the form of the Sale Order that, among other things: (i) authorizes and approves the Sale to Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser or, in the event that Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then to the Winning Bidder(s), pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement or the Alternative Agreement submitted by the Winning Bidder, as applicable. 

- j. **<u>Backup Bidder</u>** . Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, in the event that the Winning Bidder fails to close the Sale on or before November 30, 2026 (or such date as may be extended by the Debtor), the Back-Up Bid will be deemed to be the Winning Bid, the Back-Up Bidder will be deemed to be the Winning Bidder, and the Debtor shall be authorized to close the Sale to the Back-Up Bidder subject to the terms of the Back-Up Bid without the need for further order of the Court and without the need for further notice to any interested parties; _provided, however,_ that Disney, if operating as the Back-Up Bidder, shall not be subject to any penalties, and Disney shall be permitted to terminate the Sale unilaterally if the Sale does not close on or prior to December 17, 2026 and the failure to close is not on account of Disney’s material breach; _provided, further,_ that any duties and related requirements for Disney to serve as the Back-Up Bidder, or milestones in connection therewith, shall be subject to the terms of the last bid Disney submitted at the Auction. 

- k. **<u>Return of Deposits</u>** <u>. All Deposits shall be returned to each bidder not selected</u> by the Debtor as the Winning Bidder no later than five (5) business days following the closing of the Sale, including that certain $2,000,000 fully refundable deposit to be paid to Disney to the extent Disney is not selected as the Winning Bidder.  The deposit of the Winning Bidder or, if the Sale is closed with the Back-Up Bidder, the deposit of the Back-Up Bidder, shall be applied to the purchase price for the Sale.  If the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) fails to consummate the Sale because of a breach or failure to perform on the part of such bidder, then, subject to the terms of any Stalking Horse Agreement or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to retain the Deposit of the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform.<sup>6</sup> 

- l. **<u>Fiduciary Out</u>** <u>. Nothing in the Bidding Procedures will require any director or</u> officer of the Debtor to take any action, or to refrain from taking any action, with respect to the Bidding Procedures, to the extent such director or officer reasonably determines in good faith, in consultation with outside counsel, that taking such action, or refraining from taking such action, as applicable, would

result in a violation of applicable law or be inconsistent with his or her fiduciary obligations. 

   - m. **<u>Reservation of Rights</u>** <u>. Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, the Debtor may,</u> and reserves the right to, modify the Bidding Procedures at or prior to the Auction, including, without limitation, to modify, extend, enlarge, revise, or otherwise alter any or all of the deadlines set forth herein or related hereto including, without limitation, with respect to the marketing process, the submission of bids, the designation of Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers, Qualifying Bidders, and Auction Participants, the Assumption and Assignment Procedures, the Auction, the Sale Hearing, and the closing of any Sale to Winning Bidders or Back-Up Bidders, as applicable, as well as to modify bidding increments, waive terms and conditions set forth herein with respect to any or all Potential Bidders (including, without limitation, the Bid Requirements), impose additional terms and conditions with respect to any or all of the Potential Bidders, adjourn or cancel the Auction at or prior to the Auction, and adjourn the Sale Hearing. Nothing in the Bidding Procedures shall obligate the Debtor to consummate or pursue any transaction with a Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney, subject to, and in accordance with the terms of the Bidding Procedures and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement.
