# **1.** **<u>The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement</u>** 

On October __, 2026, the Debtor entered into the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement with Disney.  As set forth more fully in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, the price to be paid by Disney consists of $20 million in cash (the “Stalking Horse Purchase Price”). Disney is deemed to be a Qualifying Bidder (as defined below), and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement is deemed to be a Qualifying Bid (as defined below). 

> 3 The Sale Objection Deadline and Contract Objection Deadlines apply to all objections to the Sale of the Assets and the assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts (including adequate assurance of future performance by any Stalking Horse Purchaser) with the exception of objections related to adequate assurance of future performance by a Winning Bidder _other than_ any Stalking Horse Purchaser, which shall be due at or prior to the Sale Hearing.

# **2.** **<u>Authorization to Designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers</u>** 

These Bidding Procedures authorize but do not require the Debtor to designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers with respect to all or any subset of the Assets, other than the Assets proposed to be acquired by Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement entered into with such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser shall be deemed to be a Qualifying Bid (as defined below) and such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser shall be deemed to be a Qualifying Bidder (as defined below) for all purposes under these Bidding Procedures. 

# **3.** **<u>Assets for Sale</u>** 

The Debtor shall offer for Sale all or substantially all of the Assets or any subset thereof, including the Disney Target Assets, _provided_ that the Debtor determines that the aggregate consideration offered by any bid, or combination of bids, for the Assets, satisfies the requirements set forth in these Bidding Procedures.  Potential Bidders may bid on all or any number or combination of the Assets. 

# **4.** **<u>Participation Requirements</u>** 

Any interested party that wishes to participate in the bidding process for the Assets (each, a “Potential Bidder”) must first become a “Qualifying Bidder.” To become a Qualifying Bidder, and thus be able to conduct due diligence and gain access to the Debtor’s confidential electronic data room concerning the assets (the “Data Room”), a Potential Bidder must submit to the Debtor and its advisors: 

- (a) documentation identifying the Potential Bidder, its principals, and the representatives thereof who are authorized to appear and act on their behalf for all purposes regarding the contemplated bidding and transaction process; 

- (b) an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance satisfactory to the Debtor; and 

- (c) sufficient information, as reasonably determined by the Debtor, to allow the Debtor to determine, in its reasonable business judgment, that the interested party (i) has the financial wherewithal to consummate a Sale, and (ii) intends to access the Data Room for a _bona fide_ purpose consistent with these Bidding Procedures; 

Each Potential Bidder shall comply with all reasonable requests for information and due diligence access by the Debtor or its advisors regarding the ability of such Potential Bidder to consummate its contemplated transaction. 

# **5.** **<u>Bankruptcy Court Jurisdiction</u>** 

Any Potential Bidders and Qualifying Bidders shall: (a) be deemed to have waived any right to a jury trial in connection with, and consented and submitted to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court over, any actions or proceedings arising from or relating the Bidding Procedures, the Sale, the Auction and the construction and enforcement of the contemplated transaction documents

of such parties; (b) bring any such action or proceeding in the Court; and (c) be deemed to have consented to the Court entering a final judgment determining any such action or proceeding and that such final judgment in any such action or proceeding, including all appeals, shall be conclusive and may be enforced in other jurisdictions (including any foreign jurisdictions) by suit on the judgment or in any other manner provided by applicable law. 

# **6.** **<u>Form of Agreement for Sale of the Assets; Including the Transferred Property</u>** 

Potential Bidders should reference the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement. As set forth below, Potential Bidders intending to submit bids that include the Disney Target Assets, or any combination of Assets must include with their bids: 

- (a) a statement that such Potential Bidder offers to (i) purchase or license the Disney Target Assets, or a number or combination of the Assets, and (ii) assume liabilities, upon substantially the same terms as, or terms more favorable to the Debtor and its estate than, as applicable, the terms set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; and 

- (b) a clean and duly executed transaction agreement (an “Alternative Agreement”) and a marked copy of such agreement showing variations from the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement. 

# **7.** **<u>Due Diligence</u>** 

The Debtor will provide any Qualifying Bidder (including, for the avoidance of doubt), Disney with reasonable access to the Data Room and any other additional information that the Debtor believes to be reasonable and appropriate under the circumstances.  All additional due diligence requests shall be directed to: (a) proposed investment banker for the Debtor, Houlihan Lokey, Attn: Daniel Cullins (214-220-8473); (dcullins@hl.com) and (b) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., Attn: Heather Lambert (hlambert@wsgr.com). 

The due diligence period shall extend through and including the Bid Deadline.  The Debtor, in its business judgment, may, but shall not be obligated to, furnish any due diligence information after the Bid Deadline. 

The Debtor reserves the right, in its reasonable discretion, to withhold or limit access to any due diligence information that the Debtor determines is business-sensitive or otherwise not appropriate for disclosure to a Qualifying Bidder. Notwithstanding any prepetition limitations, including, without limitation, any non-disclosure, confidentiality or similar provisions relating to any due diligence information, the Debtor and its estate shall be authorized to provide due diligence information to Qualifying Bidders _provided_ that such Qualifying Bidders have delivered an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance acceptable to the Debtor.

# **8.** **<u>Bid Requirements</u>** 

Other than in the case of Disney and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser and its Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, which shall be considered a Qualifying Bidder and a Qualifying Bid, respectively, for all purposes under these Bidding Procedures, without regard to any of the requirements or conditions set forth herein and without any other or further action by the Stalking Horse Purchaser, to be deemed a “Qualifying <u>Bid,” a bid must be received from a Qualifying Bidder on or before the Bid Deadline and satisfy</u> each of the following requirements (each, a “Bid Requirement”): 

- a. be in writing; 

- b. fully disclose the identity of the Qualifying Bidder (and to the extent that the Qualifying Bidder is a newly formed acquisition entity or the like, the identity of the Qualifying Bidder’s parent company or sponsor), and provide the contact information of the specific person(s) whom the Debtor or its advisors should contact in the event that the Debtor has any questions or wishes to discuss the bid submitted by the Qualifying Bidder; 

- c. set forth the purchase price to be paid by such Qualifying Bidder; 

- d. not propose payment in any form other than cash; 

- e. state the liabilities proposed to be paid or assumed by such Qualifying Bidder; 

- f. specify the Assets that are included in the bid and state that such Qualifying Bidder offers to (i) purchase or license the Assets, or a number or combination of the Assets, and (ii) assume liabilities, upon substantially the same terms as, or terms more favorable to the Debtor and its estate than, the terms set forth in, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- g. be accompanied by an alternative asset purchase agreement (an “Alternative Agreement”) that reflects any variations from, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any other Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- h. state that such Qualifying Bidder’s offer is formal, binding, and unconditional, and is irrevocable until two (2) business days after the closing of the Sale; 

- i. to the extent that a Bid is not accompanied by evidence of the Qualifying Bidder’s capacity to consummate the sale, license or

other disposition set forth in its Bid with cash on hand, each Bid must include unconditional committed financing from a reputable financing institution, documented to the satisfaction of the Debtor, that demonstrates that the Qualifying Bidder has: (i) received sufficient debt and/or equity funding commitments to satisfy the Qualifying Bidder’s purchase price and other obligations under its Bid; and (ii) adequate working capital financing or resources to finance going concern operations for the Assets and the proposed transactions. Such funding commitments or other financing must be unconditional and must not be subject to any internal approvals, syndication requirements, diligence, or credit committee approvals, and shall have covenants and conditions reasonably acceptable to the Debtor; 

j. contain such financial and other information to allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to the Qualifying Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement, including, without limitation, such financial and other information supporting the Qualifying Bidder’s ability to comply with the requirements of adequate assurance of future performance under section 365(f)(2)(B) and, if applicable, section 365(b)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code, including the Qualifying Bidder’s financial wherewithal and willingness to perform under any contracts and leases that are assumed and assigned to the Qualifying Bidder, in a form that allows the Debtor to serve such information on any counterparties to any contracts or leases potentially being assumed and assigned in connection with the Sale within one (1) business day after the Debtor’s receipt of such information; 

- k. (A) identify with particularity each and every executory contract and unexpired lease the assumption and assignment of which is a condition to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement; (B) provide for the payment of cure costs related to such executory contracts and unexpired leases, and (C) demonstrate that the Qualifying Bidder can provide adequate assurance of future performance under all such executory contracts and unexpired leases; 

- l. set forth the Qualifying Bidder’s intentions with respect to (i) hiring any of the Debtor’s current or former employees, and (ii) providing transition services to the Debtor; 

- m. include a commitment to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement by no later than December 17, 2026;

- n. not request or entitle such Qualifying Bidder to any break-up fee, termination fee, expense reimbursement, or similar type of fee or payment unless such Qualifying Bidder is seeking to be designated as an Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser with respect to the Assets in its bid; 

- o. if the Alternative Agreement contemplates the exclusive license or purchase of the Disney Target Assets, or the purchase of Assets subject to any other Stalking Horse Agreement, the aggregate consideration proposed by the Qualifying Bidder must equal or exceed the sum of the amount of (1) the value offered by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement with respect to the Disney Target Assets or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement with respect to any Assets included in the bid, plus (2) any applicable Break-Up Fee, plus (3) $1,000,000 (collectively, the “Minimum Overbid Amount”); 

- p. not contain any contingencies of any kind, including, without limitation, contingencies related to financing, internal approval, or due diligence; 

- q. contain a written acknowledgement and representation that the Qualifying Bidder (i) has had an opportunity to conduct any and all due diligence regarding the Assets, (ii) has relied solely upon its own independent review, investigation or inspection of any documents and other information in making its Qualifying Bid, and (iii) did not rely upon any written or oral statements, representations, promises, warranties or guaranties whatsoever, whether express, implied, by operation of law or otherwise, regarding the Assets, or the completeness of any documents or other information provided in connection with the Bidding Procedures and the Sale. Such written acknowledgment and representation must include the following disclaimer: Buyer acknowledges that, should the Closing occur, Buyer shall acquire the Assets without any surviving representations or warranties, on an “as is” and “where is” basis, except as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement; 

- r. set forth each regulatory and third-party approval required for the Qualified Bidder to consummate the Sale, and the time within which the Qualified Bidder expects to receive such regulatory and thirdparty approvals, and the Debtor, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, may consider the timing of such approvals, and any actions the Qualified Bidder will take to ensure receipt of such approvals as promptly as possible, when considering the other Bid Requirements. Each Qualified Bidder must further agree that its legal counsel will coordinate in good faith with counsel to the

Debtor to provide pertinent information regarding the Qualified Bidder’s operations reasonably required to analyze issues arising with respect to any applicable regulatory requirements and to discuss and explain the Qualified Bidder’s regulatory analysis, strategy, and timeline for securing all applicable approvals as soon as reasonably practicable; 

- s. provide for the Qualifying Bidder to serve as a backup bidder (the “Back-Up Bidder”) if the Qualifying Bidder’s bid is the next highest and best bid (the “Back-Up Bid”) after the Winning Bid, in accordance with the terms of the Alternative Agreement; 

- t. include written evidence of authorization and approval from the Qualifying Bidder’s board of directors (or comparable governing body) with respect to the submission, execution, and delivery of the Alternative Agreement; 

- u. provide a good faith cash deposit (the “Deposit”) in an amount equal to ten percent (10%) of the aggregate purchase price provided for in the Alternative Agreement (or such additional amount as may be determined by the Debtor in its reasonable discretion); and 

- v. provide that in the event of the Qualifying Bidder’s breach of, or failure to perform under, the Alternative Agreement, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to pursue all available legal and equitable remedies, including, without limitation, retention of the Deposit as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform. 

A bid from a Qualifying Bidder satisfying all of the above requirements, as determined by the Debtor in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below), shall constitute a Qualifying Bid.  The Debtor reserves the right to work with any Qualifying Bidder in advance of the Auction to cure any deficiencies in a bid that is not initially deemed a Qualifying Bid. 

Each Qualifying Bidder submitting a bid shall be deemed to: (a) acknowledge and represent that it is bound by all of the terms and conditions of the Bidding Procedures; and (b) have waived the right to pursue a substantial contribution claim under section 503 of the Bankruptcy Code related in any way to the submission of its bid, the Bidding Procedures, and the Sale. 

# **9.** **<u>Bid Deadline</u>** 

A Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser that may be designated by the Debtor, that desires to make a bid shall deliver a written and electronic copy of its bid in both PDF and MS-WORD format to the Bidding Procedures Notice Parties so as to be received on or before **<u>November 20, 2026 at 5:00 p.m. (ET)</u>** (the “Bid Deadline”); _provided_ that the Debtor may extend the Bid Deadline in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below) without further order of the Court.  To the extent that the Bid Deadline is extended for all

parties, the Debtor shall file a notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case indicating the same. **Any party that does not submit a bid by the Bid Deadline will not be allowed to (a) submit any offer after the Bid Deadline, or (b) participate in the Auction.** 

# **10.** **<u>Evaluation of Qualifying Bids</u>** 

The Debtor shall make a determination regarding whether a timely submitted bid from a Qualifying Bidder is a Qualifying Bid. 

No later than **November 21, 2026** , the Debtor shall: (i) notify all Qualifying Bidders whether their respective bids have been determined to be Qualifying Bids; and (ii) determine which of the Qualifying Bids, at such time, is the highest or best bid for purposes of constituting the opening bid of the Auction (which must be at least equal to the Minimum Overbid Amount, the “Baseline Bid” and the Qualifying Bidder submitting the Baseline Bid, the “Baseline Bidder”), and shall promptly notify any Stalking Horse Purchaser and all Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids of the Baseline Bid. 

# **11.** **<u>No Qualifying Bids</u>** 

If no timely Qualifying Bids other than any Stalking Horse Purchaser’s Qualifying Bid are submitted on or before the Bid Deadline, the Debtor shall not hold an Auction with respect to the Assets covered by the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement and shall request at the Sale Hearing that the Court approve such Stalking Horse Agreement and the transactions contemplated thereunder. 

# **12.** **<u>Auction</u>** 

If the Debtor timely receives one or more Qualifying Bids for all or any subset of the Assets, including, without limitation, the Disney Target Assets, then the Debtor shall conduct an auction (the “Auction”).  Following the Auction, the Debtor will determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, which Qualifying Bid is the highest or otherwise best bid for the Assets, which will be determined by considering, among other things, the following non-binding factors: (a) the amount and nature of the consideration, including any assumed liabilities; (b) the number, type and nature of any changes requested by the bidder in the form of Alternative Agreement as compared to any Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets subject to its bid; (c) the extent to which such modifications are likely to delay closing of the Sale and the cost to the Debtor and its estate of such modifications or delay; (d) the total consideration to be received by the Debtor and its estate; (e) the transaction structure and execution risk, including conditions to, timing of and certainty of closing, termination provisions, availability of financing and financial wherewithal to meet all commitments, and required governmental or other approval; (f) the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate; and (g) any other qualitative or quantitative factors the Debtor may reasonably deem reasonably appropriate under the circumstances. 

The Auction shall be governed by the following procedures: 

- (a) the Auction shall be held on **<u>November 23, 2026 at 10:00 a.m. (ET)</u>** at a location to be determined by the Debtor or virtually via video conference pursuant to

information to be timely provided by the Debtor to the Auction Participants (as defined below); 

- (b) only Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser(s) designated by the Debtor, and the other Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids (together, the “Auction <u>Bidders”) shall be entitled to make any subsequent bids at the Auction;</u> 

- (c) the Auction Bidders shall appear at the Auction in-person (or via Zoom, if permitted) in the Debtor’s sole discretion, or through a duly authorized representative; 

- (d) only the Debtor, the Consultation Parties, and the Auction Bidders, together with the professional advisors to each of the foregoing parties, may attend the Auction (collectively, the “Auction Participants”); _provided_ that any creditors or parties in interest in the chapter 11 case may request permission in writing to attend the Auction no later than Noon (ET) two (2) days prior to the Auction and the Debtor, in its sole discretion, may reasonably determine to grant or deny such permission; 

- (e) the Debtor and its professional advisors shall direct and preside over the Auction, which shall be transcribed; 

- (f) prior to start of the Auction, each of the Auction Bidders shall confirm on the record of the proceedings that they have not engaged in any collusion with respect to the Bidding Procedures, the Auction, or the Sale; 

- (g) bidding shall commence at the amount of the Baseline Bid, and the Auction Bidders may submit successive bids in increments of $500,000, _provided_ that: (i) each such successive bid must be a Qualifying Bid; and (ii) the Debtor shall retain the right to modify the bid increment requirements at the Auction; 

- (h) in any successive bid by Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor, the amount of the applicable Break-Up Fee shall be included in the calculation of such bid; 

- (i) the Auction may include individual negotiations with any of the Auction Bidders, but all bids shall be made on the record and in the presence of all of the Auction Bidders; 

- (j) all material terms of the bid that is deemed to be the highest and best bid for each round of bidding shall be fully disclosed to the Auction Bidders, and the Debtor shall use reasonable efforts to clarify any and all questions that the Auction Bidders may have regarding the Debtor’s announcement of the then-current highest and best bid; 

- (k) the Debtor and its professional advisors may employ and announce at the Auction additional procedural rules that are reasonable under the circumstances ( _e.g._ , the amount of time allotted to make subsequent bids) for conducting the Auction, provided that such rules are (i) not inconsistent with the Bankruptcy Code, the

Federal Rules of Bankruptcy Procedure, the Local Rules of the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware, or any applicable order of the Court, including, without limitation, the Bidding Procedures Order, and (ii) disclosed to the Auction Bidders in advance of their application; 

- (l) each Auction Bidder shall (i) be deemed to have waived any right to a jury trial in connection with, and consented and submitted to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court over, any actions or proceedings arising from or relating the Bidding Procedures, the Sale, the Auction and the construction and enforcement of the contemplated transaction documents of the Auction Bidders, (ii) bring any such action or proceeding in the Court, and (iii) be deemed to have consented to the Court entering a final judgment determining any such action or proceeding and that such final judgment in any such action or proceeding, including all appeals, shall be conclusive and may be enforced in other jurisdictions (including any foreign jurisdictions) by suit on the judgment or in any other manner provided by applicable law; 

- (m) the Auction Bidders shall have the right to make additional modifications to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, in conjunction with each Qualifying Bid submitted in each round of bidding during the Auction, _provided_ that (i) any such modifications on an aggregate basis and viewed in whole, shall not, in the Debtor’s discretion be less favorable to the Debtor and its estate than the terms of, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, and (ii) each Qualifying Bid shall constitute an irrevocable offer and shall be binding on the Auction Bidder submitting such bid until such party shall have submitted a subsequent Qualifying Bid at the Auction or the conclusion of the Sale Hearing, whichever occurs sooner, unless such bid is selected as the Winning Bid or the Back-Up Bid, which shall remain binding as provided for herein; 

- (n) the Debtor shall have the right to request any additional financial information that will allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to an Auction Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to consummate the transactions contemplated by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as may be amended during the Auction, and any further information that the Debtor may believe is reasonably necessary to clarify and evaluate any bid made by an Auction Bidder during the Auction; 

- (o) upon the conclusion of the Auction, the Debtor shall determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties and subject to Court approval, the offer or offers for the Assets that is or are the highest or otherwise best from among the Qualifying Bids submitted at the Auction for all or substantially all or any subset of the Assets (the “Winning Bids”).  In making this decision, the Debtor shall consider the amount of the purchase price, the assumption of liabilities, the likelihood of the bidder’s ability to close a transaction and the timing thereof, the number, type and

nature of any changes to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement submitted with the Winning Bid, as applicable, requested by each bidder, and the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate.  The bidder submitting such Winning Bid at the Auction shall become a “Winning Bidder,” and shall have such rights and responsibilities of the purchaser as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement, as applicable.  The Debtor may, in its business judgment, designate a Back-Up Bid (and the corresponding Back-Up Bidder) to purchase the Assets in the event that the Winning Bidder does not close the Sale; 

- (p) if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall allow one (1) Winning Bidder to interview and make employment offers to any employees of Disney formerly employed by the Debtor who wish to consider opportunities with such Winning Bidder and shall work in good faith with such Winning Bidder to transition employees who accept an offer to the Winning Bidder; _provided_ that Disney may designate up to 20% of such employees as outside the scope of such process, and Disney need not make available or allow the Winning Bidder (or any other party) the right to interview or make employment offers to such employees; 

- (q) if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall be entitled to the Break-Up Fee; 

- (r) as soon as reasonably practicable after completion of the Auction, the Debtor will file with the Court and serve on the Transaction Notice Parties a notice setting forth the results of the Auction (the “Notice of Winning Bidder”), which will (a) identify the Winning Bidder and the Back-Up Bidder, if applicable; (b) include a copy of the Winning Bid and the Back-Up Bid, if applicable, or a summary of the material terms of such bid, including any proposed assumption and assignment of Contracts contemplated thereby; and (c) set forth the Adequate Assurance Objection Deadline, the date, time, and location of the Sale Hearing and any other relevant dates or other information necessary to reasonably apprise the Transaction Notice Parties of the outcome of the Auction; and 

- (s) prior to the Sale Hearing, the Winning Bidder shall complete and execute all agreements, contracts, instruments and other documents evidencing and containing the terms and conditions upon which the Winning Bid was made; 

**THE WINNING BID AND ANY BACK-UP BID AND THEIR RELATED STALKING HORSE TRANSACTION AGREEMENT, ADDITIONAL STALKING HORSE AGREEMENT, OR ALTERNATIVE AGREEMENT, AS APPLICABLE, SHALL CONSTITUTE AN IRREVOCABLE OFFER AND BE BINDING ON THE WINNING BIDDER AND THE BACK-UP BIDDER, RESPECTIVELY, FROM THE TIME THE BID IS SUBMITTED UNTIL TWO (2) BUSINESS DAYS AFTER THE SALE HAS CLOSED. EACH QUALIFYING BID THAT IS NOT THE WINNING BID OR THE BACK-UP BID SHALL BE DEEMED WITHDRAWN AND TERMINATED AT THE CONCLUSION OF THE SALE HEARING.**

# **13.** **<u>Sale Hearing</u>** 

The Winning Bid and any Back-Up Bid and, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement related to such bids will be subject to approval by the Court.  The Sale Hearing to approve the Winning Bid and any Back-Up and the applicable asset purchase agreements of such bids shall take place on **<u>November 24, 2026] at [●] (ET)</u>** <u>.</u> 

The Sale Hearing and any additional deadlines established in connection with these Bidding Procedures may be adjourned by the Debtor, in consultation with any Consultation Parties, Disney, and any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser, or in the event Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then in consultation with the Winning Bidder(s), from time to time without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest other than by announcement of the adjournment in open court on the date scheduled for the Sale Hearing, or by filing a hearing agenda or notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case. 

At the Sale Hearing, the Debtor will seek entry of an order, substantially in the form of the Sale Order that, among other things: (i) authorizes and approves the Sale to Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser or, in the event that Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then to the Winning Bidder(s), pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement or the Alternative Agreement submitted by the Winning Bidder, as applicable. 

# **14.** **<u>Back-Up Bidder</u>** 

Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, in the event that the Winning Bidder fails to close the Sale on or before the date that is two weeks following entry of the Sale Order (or such date as may be extended by the Debtor), the Back-Up Bid will be deemed to be the Winning Bid, the Back-Up Bidder will be deemed to be the Winning Bidder, and the Debtor shall be authorized to close the Sale to the Back-Up Bidder subject to the terms of the Back-Up Bid without the need for further order of the Court and without the need for further notice to any interested parties; _provided, however,_ that Disney, if operating as the Back-Up Bidder, shall not be subject to any penalties, and, Disney shall be permitted to terminate the Sale unilaterally if the Sale does not close on or prior to December 17, 2026 and the failure to close is not on account of Disney’s material breach; _provided, further,_ that any duties and related requirements for Disney to serve as the Back-Up Bidder, or milestones in connection therewith, shall be subject to the terms of the last bid Disney submitted at the Auction. 

# **15.** **<u>Return of Deposits</u>** 

All Deposits shall be returned to each bidder not selected by the Debtor as the Winning Bidder no later than five (5) business days following the closing of the Sale, including that certain $2,000,000 fully refundable deposit to be paid to Disney to the extent Disney is not selected as the Winning Bidder.  The deposit of the Winning Bidder or, if the Sale is closed with the Back-Up Bidder, the deposit of the Back-Up Bidder, shall be applied to the purchase price for the Sale.  If the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) fails to consummate the Sale because of a breach or failure to perform on the part of such

bidder, then, subject to the terms of any Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to retain the Deposit of the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform.<sup>4</sup> 

# **16.** **<u>Bidding Procedures Notice Parties</u>** 

The term “Bidding Procedures Notice Parties” shall mean: (a) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., 222 Delaware Avenue, Suite 800, Wilmington, Delaware 19801, Attn: Erin R. Fay (efay@wsgr.com) and Shane M. Reil (sreil@wsgr.com); (b) proposed investment banker for the Debtor, Houlihan Lokey, Attn: Daniel Cullins (dcullins@hl.com); (c) counsel to Disney, Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom LLP, One Manhattan West, New York, New York 10001, Attn: Brandon Van Dyke (brandon.vandyke@skadden.com), Ron E. Meisler (ron.meisler@skadden.com), Bryan Uelk (bryan.uelk@skadden.com)_and Samuel J. Cammer (samuel.cammer@skadden.com)); and (d) counsel to any official committee of unsecured creditors that may be appointed in the chapter 11 case. 

# **17.** **<u>The Consultation Parties</u>** 

The Debtor shall consult with any official committee of unsecured creditors appointed by the Bankruptcy Court and counsel to such committee (the “Consultation Parties”) as explicitly provided for in the Bidding Procedures; provided, however, that the Debtor shall not be required to consult with any Consultation Party (and its advisors) that is a Qualified Bidder. For the avoidance of doubt, any consultation rights afforded to the Consultation Parties by these Bidding Procedures shall not limit the Debtor’s discretion in any way and shall not include the right to veto any decision made by the Debtor in the exercise of its reasonable business judgment. 

# **18.** **<u>Fiduciary Out</u>** 

Nothing in these Bidding Procedures will require any director or officer of the Debtor to take any action, or to refrain from taking any action, with respect to these Bidding Procedures, to the extent such director or officer reasonably determines in good faith, in consultation with outside counsel, that taking such action, or refraining from taking such action, as applicable, would result in a violation of applicable law or be inconsistent with his or her fiduciary obligations. 

# **19.** **<u>Reservation of Rights</u>** 

Except as otherwise provided in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or the Bidding Procedures Order, the Debtor may, and reserves the right to, modify the Bidding Procedures at or prior to the Auction, including, without limitation, to modify, extend, enlarge, revise, or otherwise alter any or all of the deadlines set forth herein or related hereto including, without limitation, with respect to the marketing process, the submission of bids, the designation of Additional Stalking 

> 4 For the avoidance of doubt, the Debtor’s retention of a Deposit shall not constitute a waiver of any of the Debtor’s legal or equitable rights relating to a Winning Bidder’s or Back-Up Bidder’s breach or failure to perform, and all such rights and remedies are reserved.

Horse Purchasers, Qualified Bidders, and Auction Participants, the Assumption and Assignment Procedures, the Auction, the Sale Hearing, and the closing of any Sale  to Winning Bidders or Back-Up Bidders, as applicable, as well as to modify bidding increments, waive terms and conditions set forth herein with respect to any or all Potential Bidders (including, without limitation, the Bid Requirements), impose additional terms and conditions with respect to any or all of the Potential Bidders, adjourn or cancel the Auction at or prior to the Auction, and adjourn the Sale Hearing. Nothing herein shall obligate the Debtors to consummate or pursue any transaction with a Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney subject to, and in accordance with the terms of these Bidding Procedures and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement.

**The Debtor will seek to assume and assign the Contracts that have been selected by the Winning Bidder(s) (each, a “Selected Assigned Contract” and collectively, the “Selected** **<u>Assigned Contracts”) at a hearing before the Honorable [●], in the United States Bankruptcy</u> Court for the District of Delaware, 824 North Market Street, Wilmington, Delaware 19801, [●], Courtroom #[●], (a “Sale Hearing”) on November 24, 2026 at [●] (ET)** , **subject to the Bankruptcy Court’s availability, or such other date as determined by the Debtor, in consultation with Disney or any other Stalking Horse Purchaser, or in the event that any Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as the Winning Bidder, then in consultation with the Winning Bidder(s)** , **in accordance with the terms of the Bidding Procedures Order** . 

To the extent that the parties are unable to consensually resolve any Contract Objection prior to the commencement of the Sale Hearing, including, without limitation, any dispute with

respect to the cure amount required to be paid to the applicable Counterparty under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code (any such dispute, a “Cure Dispute”), such Contract Objection will be adjudicated at the Sale Hearing or at such other date and time as may be determined by the Debtor, in consultation with the Winning Bidder, or fixed by the Court; provided, however, that if the Contract Objection relates solely to a Cure Dispute, the Selected Assigned Contract may be assumed by the Debtor and assigned to the Winning Bidder  for such Selected Assigned Contract, provided that the cure amount that the Counterparty asserts is required to be paid under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code (or such lower amount as agreed to by the Counterparty) is deposited in a segregated account by the Debtor or the Winning Bidder for such Selected Assigned Contract, pending the Court’s adjudication of the Cure Dispute or the parties’ consensual resolution of the Cure Dispute. 

Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, if after the Sale Hearing or the entry of the Sale Order additional executory contracts or unexpired leases of the Debtor are determined to be Assigned Contracts, as soon as practicable thereafter, the Debtor shall file with the Court and serve, by regular mail, on the Counterparties an Assumption and Assignment Notice, and such Counterparties shall be required to file any Contract Objections not later than fourteen (14) days thereafter. If no Contract Objection is timely received, the Debtor shall be authorized to assume and assign such Assigned Contracts to the applicable Winning Bidder without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest and without the need for further order of the Court, and such assumption and assignment shall be subject to the terms of the Sale Order. 

Dated: October [●], 2026 **WILSON SONSINI GOODRICH & ROSATI, P.C.** Wilmington, Delaware

# **TRANSITION SERVICES AGREEMENT** 

This TRANSITION SERVICES AGREEMENT (this “Agreement”), dated as of [●], 2026 (the “Effective Date”), is entered into by and between Disney Worldwide Services, Inc., a Florida corporation (“Disney”), and [Winning Bidder], a [●] (“[Winning Bidder]”).  Capitalized terms used, but not otherwise defined, herein shall have their respective meanings set forth in the Exclusivity Agreement. 

W I T N E S S E T H 

WHEREAS, Disney and Character Technologies, Inc. (“CAI”) are parties to that certain Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, dated September 18, 2026 (together with all exhibits and schedules thereto, the “Exclusivity Agreement”); 

WHEREAS, CAI has engaged in a competitive auction process for the disposition of some or all of its assets (the “Auction”), and, pursuant to the Exclusivity Agreement, in the event that a bidder other than Disney is the winning bidder for (i) that certain perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive license to certain of CAI’s intellectual property and related technology (the “IP <u>License”) or (ii) substantially all of CAI’s assets (including the intellectual property related to the</u> IP License), properties, businesses, or operations, including through an acquisition of an exclusive license of CAI’s intellectual property, then Disney shall provide to one such winning bidder certain transition services; 

WHEREAS, [Winning Bidder] was selected as a winning bidder, and this Agreement is being entered into by Disney and [Winning Bidder] to satisfy Disney’s obligation to provide transition services to one winning bidder pursuant to the Exclusivity Agreement; 

WHEREAS, pursuant to the Exclusivity Agreement, Disney has agreed to provide to [Winning Bidder] certain transition services, subject to the terms and conditions set forth herein, to facilitate [Winning Bidder’s] continued operation of the Purchased Assets; 

WHEREAS, pursuant to the Exclusivity Agreement, Disney has agreed to allow one winning bidder to interview and make employment offers to former CAI employees who wish to consider opportunities with such winning bidder; 

WHEREAS, Disney may provide, or cause its Group members or Sub-Contractors to provide to [Winning Bidder] such services for a limited period of time from and after the Effective Date; and 

WHEREAS, each of Disney and [Winning Bidder] desires to reflect herein the terms of their agreement with respect to certain transition services pursuant to the Exclusivity Agreement. 

NOW, THEREFORE, subject to the terms, conditions, covenants and provisions of this Agreement, for good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged by this Agreement, Disney and [Winning Bidder], for themselves, their successors and assigns, mutually covenant and agree as follows:

# **ARTICLE I** 

# **DEFINITIONS** 

1.1 <u>Definitions.  As used in this Agreement, the following terms shall have the</u> following meanings: 

“Affected Party” has the meaning ascribed thereto in Section 9.1.

As used in this Agreement, the term “control” (including, with correlative meanings, the terms “controlled by” and “under common control with” and whether or not such terms are capitalized), as used with respect to any Person, means the possession, directly or indirectly, of the power to direct or cause the direction of the management and policies of such Person, whether through ownership of voting securities, by contract or otherwise. 

“Agreement” has the meaning ascribed thereto in the preamble. 

“Applicable Termination Date” means, with respect to each Service, the termination date specified with respect to such Service in Schedule A, which in each case, shall occur no later than the lesser of (a) sixty (60) days following the Effective Date and (b) seventy-five (75) days following the conclusion of the Auction.

facilities, costs of materials purchased or furnished, costs for owned equipment, facilities and utilities of the Service Provider, insurance costs and all other costs and expenditures incurred by the Service Provider in connection with such Service (other than Third-Party Costs), plus a ten percent (10%) margin.

# **ARTICLE II** 

# **SERVICES** 

# 2.1 <u>Provision of Services.</u> 

(a) Disney shall provide, or cause the applicable Service Provider or Sub-Contractor to provide, the Services to [Winning Bidder] and the applicable members of the [Winning Bidder] Group, in each case, as Service Recipient(s), in accordance with the terms of this Agreement.  The Parties acknowledge and agree that the Services are provided for the benefit of the operation of the Purchased Assets as used in the Business that exists as of the Reference Date on a transitional basis only.  Accordingly, as promptly as practicable following the execution of this Agreement, [Winning Bidder] agrees to use commercially reasonable efforts to cease using the Services or transition each Service to [Winning Bidder]’s or its Group members’ own internal organization or to alternate service providers under other arrangements.  The Service Provider shall use commercially reasonable efforts (taking into account the express limitations set forth in this Agreement, including in Section 2.1(e)) to perform, or cause to be performed, the Services in substantially the same manner, and with substantially the same degree of care, quality and priority, as such Services (or reasonably similar services) were provided by or on behalf of CAI to the Business immediately prior to the Reference Date.  The Service Recipient agrees and

acknowledges that the Services shall be provided by the Service Provider solely using Service Recipient Systems (as defined in Schedule D).  To the extent that Service Recipient Systems are not suitable for providing the Services or the Service Provider is unable to use or access Service Recipient Systems, other than due to the fault of the Service Provider, the Service Provider shall be relieved and excused from any further obligation to provide or make available the applicable Services that could not be provided for such reason, and the Service Provider shall not be considered in breach of this Agreement for failure to provide such Services. 

(b) [Winning Bidder] shall pay, perform, discharge and satisfy, or cause the applicable members of the [Winning Bidder] Group to pay, perform, discharge and satisfy, as and when due, its and their respective obligations as Service Recipients under this Agreement in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 

(c) Each of Disney and [Winning Bidder] agrees to appoint (i) an employee representative, which may be an employee of one of its Group members (each such representative, a “Service Manager”), who shall have overall responsibility for overseeing the day-to-day operation, compliance and performance of this Agreement and implementing, managing and coordinating the Services and (ii) an executive steering leadership group (each such group, an “Executive Leadership Group”) that shall have responsibility for project oversight, including information security standards, governance and dispute escalation, in each case, pursuant to this Agreement on behalf of Disney and [Winning Bidder], respectively. Initially, the Service Managers and Executive Leadership Groups shall be the individuals set forth in Schedule C.  Either Party may change its designated Service Manager or Executive Leadership Group at any time upon written notice given to the other Party in accordance with <u>Section 9.7.  The Service Managers shall consult and coordinate with each other on a regular</u> basis during the term of this Agreement. 

(d) Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the Parties shall resolve all disputes arising under or in connection with this Agreement (each, a “Dispute”) in accordance with the following procedures (including, for the avoidance of doubt, any Dispute relating to payments with respect to the Services).  All Disputes shall be first discussed in person, by teleconference or by video conference by the Service Managers within ten (10) calendar days after receipt of written notice from either Party specifying the nature of the Dispute and requesting that the resolution process set forth in this Section 2.1(d) be initiated (a “Dispute <u>Notice”).  If any Dispute is not resolved by the Service Managers within fourteen (14) calendar</u> days after receipt of a Dispute Notice, or such longer period as the Service Managers may agree in writing, then, upon the written request of either Party, the Executive Leadership Groups shall meet in person, by teleconference or by video conference for the purpose of resolving the Dispute.  The Executive Leadership Groups shall negotiate in good faith to resolve the Dispute. If the Executive Leadership Groups do not resolve the Dispute within fourteen (14) calendar days after the date the Dispute was referred to them, or such longer period as the Executive Leadership Groups may agree in writing, the Parties may pursue any other rights, remedies or actions that may be available to them in accordance with Section 9.4. 

(e) The Service Provider shall use commercially reasonable efforts to cause the Services to be performed by the individual(s) who have historically performed such Services (or reasonably similar services) during the twelve (12) months prior to the Reference Date (each

such individual, a “Service Employee”), in each case only for so long as such Service Employee(s) remain employed by the Service Provider (or its Group member or Sub-Contractor, as applicable) and is reasonably available to perform the applicable Service.  In the event that any Service Employee(s) are not (or are no longer so) employed by the Service Provider, the Service Provider shall use commercially reasonable efforts to assign, and make available, personnel familiar with the applicable Service (solely to the extent such personnel exist) to perform such Service; provided, that, if there are no other personnel familiar with the applicable Service, then the Service Provider shall be relieved and excused from any further obligation to provide or make available the Services historically performed by such Service Employee(s), and the Service Provider shall not be considered in breach of this Agreement for failure to provide such Services.  Subject to the foregoing, the Service Provider shall determine (in its sole and absolute discretion) the personnel who shall perform the Services to be provided by the Service Provider.  For the avoidance of doubt, the Service Provider shall not be obligated to cause the Services to be performed by any individual(s), including independent contractors, other than the Service Employees and other personnel familiar with the applicable Services, including any legacy employees, independent contractors or representatives of the Service Provider.  The Service Recipient acknowledges and agrees that no such personnel shall be required to dedicate more than twenty percent (20%) of their hours of employment by or work on behalf of the Service Provider to the performance or support of the Services.  All personnel performing Services shall remain at all times, and be deemed to be, employees, independent contractors or representatives solely of the Service Provider responsible for providing such Services (or of such Service Provider’s Group member or Sub-Contractor, as the case may be) for all purposes, and shall not be, or be deemed to be, employees, independent contractors or representatives of the Service Recipient.  Service Recipient also shall not be considered a joint employer of any of the employees, independent contractors, or representatives performing the Services.  The Service Provider (or its Group member or Sub-Contractor, as the case may be) shall be solely responsible for (x) payment of (i) all compensation, (ii) all income, disability, withholding and other employment Taxes and (iii) all medical benefit premiums, vacation pay, sick pay and other employee benefits payable to or with respect to personnel who perform Services on behalf of the Service Provider, as well as (y) (i) the filing, collection, withholding, and transmission of all federal, state and local income, unemployment, payroll and employment taxes and other withholdings required by federal, state or local law or regulation payable with respect to the employees providing the Services in accordance with applicable law, and (ii) providing unemployment insurance and workers’ compensation benefits to all such employees.  In addition, as between Service Provider and Service Recipient, Service Provider shall be responsible for ensuring that such employees comply with all written policies and legal requirements applicable to the performance of the Services.  All such personnel shall be under the sole direction, control and supervision of the Service Provider (or its Group member or SubContractor, as the case may be) and the Service Provider (or its Group member or SubContractor, as the case may be) has the sole right to exercise all authority with respect to the employment, substitution, termination, assignment and compensation of such personnel. 

(f) The Service Provider may, at its option, from time to time, (i) delegate any or all of its obligations to perform Services under this Agreement to any one or more of its Group members or (ii) engage the services of other professionals, consultants or other third parties (each, a “Sub-Contractor”) in connection with the performance of the Services; provided, <u>however, that the Service Provider shall remain ultimately responsible for ensuring that its</u>

obligations hereunder are satisfied with respect to any Services provided by any such Group member or Sub-Contractor and shall be liable for any such Group member’s or Sub-Contractor’s failure to so satisfy such obligations; provided, further, that Disney’s Service Manager, and not any such Group member or Sub-Contractor, shall be the Service Recipient’s sole point of contact regarding the Services, including with respect to payment.  [Winning Bidder] shall reimburse Disney for all documented costs, fees and expenses paid by Disney or the applicable Service Provider to any third party, including Sub-Contractors, in connection with the provision of the Services (“Third-Party Costs”), including any documented third-party fees, penalties or other costs incurred in connection with third-party consents, waivers or licenses that Disney or the applicable Service Provider reasonably determines are necessary for the provision of the Services. 

(g) The Services shall be used by the applicable Service Recipient solely for the operation of [Winning Bidder]’s business for substantially the same purpose and in substantially the same manner as used by the Business during the three (3) months prior to the Reference Date. 

(h) In the event that the Service Provider reasonably deems it necessary to suspend delivery of a Service hereunder for maintenance, repair or replacement or legal, compliance or security purposes, the Service Provider shall not be obligated to deliver such Service during such periods, but shall use commercially reasonable efforts to minimize the duration and impact of the interruption.  Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement, in no event shall the Service Provider (or any of its Group members or Sub-Contractors) be required to (i) provide Services to any Person that the Service Recipient directly or indirectly acquires after the Effective Date, or in any jurisdiction in which Services were not provided as of the Effective Date, (ii) provide Services to the extent that the provision of any such Service would require any consent, waiver or license of any third party, unless and until such consent, waiver or license has been obtained, (iii) expand its facilities, incur any capital expenditures, employ additional personnel or maintain the employment of any specific persons to provide the Services hereunder or (iv) disclose to any member of the [Winning Bidder] Group any contracts by which any member of the Disney Group acquires from third parties any components or inputs to any Service. 

(i) The Service Recipient shall cooperate with the Service Provider to the extent necessary or appropriate to facilitate the performance of the Services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement.  Without limiting the generality of the foregoing, (i) each Party shall, and shall cause its Group members to, give the other Party and its Group members and, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors reasonable access to, and necessary rights to use, any information, assets, systems, materials and technologies to the extent reasonably necessary for the performance or receipt of the Services and (ii) the Service Recipient shall, and shall cause its Group members to, upon reasonable written notice, give to the Service Provider and its Group members and Sub-Contractors reasonable access, during regular business hours and at such other times as are reasonably required, to the relevant premises and personnel to the extent reasonably necessary for the performance of the Services; provided, that Disney shall only be required to grant such access and rights to (1) legacy employees of the Business and (2) such other employees of the [Winning Bidder] Group that Disney, in its sole discretion, has approved in writing.  Each Party shall (and shall cause its Group members and their personnel and, in the

case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors’ personnel, providing or receiving Services to): (A) not attempt to obtain access to, use or interfere with (1) any information technology systems of the other Party or its Group members, or (2) any confidential or competitively sensitive information owned, used or processed by the other Party, except, in each case, to the extent reasonably necessary to do so to provide or receive Services or as permitted under Section 2.1(i)(i); (B) maintain reasonable security measures to protect the systems of the other Party and its Group members to which it has access pursuant to this Agreement against access by unauthorized third parties; (C) not disable, damage or erase or disrupt or impair the normal operation of the information technology systems of the other Party or its Group members; and (D) promptly notify the other Party of any confirmed misuse, disclosure or loss of, or inability to account for, the other Party’s confidential or competitively sensitive information, and any confirmed unauthorized access to its or any member of its Group’s facilities, systems or network reasonably likely to affect the other Party and shall investigate such confirmed security incidents and reasonably cooperate with the other Party’s incident response team to mitigate and limit the impact of such a security incident. 

(j) [Winning Bidder] acknowledges that Disney is agreeing to perform, or cause to be performed, the Services in order to facilitate the release of claims set forth in Article VIII and the operation of the Purchased Assets.  The Services shall not include, and no Service Provider (or any Group member or Sub-Contractor of a Service Provider) shall be obligated to provide, any Service the provision of which to a Service Recipient from and after the Effective Date would constitute, or reasonably be expected to constitute, (i) a violation of any Law or (ii) a breach of any contractual obligation owed to a third party. 

(k) Each Party acknowledges that it may be necessary for each Party to make proprietary and/or third-party software available to the other Party in the course and for the purpose of performing or receiving, as applicable, Services.  Each Party (i) shall, and shall cause the members of its Group and, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors to, comply with the license restrictions applicable to any and all proprietary or third-party software made available to such Party by the other Party in the course of the provision of Services hereunder, (ii) acknowledges the license terms of use applicable to all proprietary or third party software in its possession as of the Effective Date and (iii) agrees that it shall be responsible for providing to the other Party a copy of the applicable license terms (or, solely with respect to open source software or other software with publicly available license terms, information sufficient to direct such other Party to a copy thereof) for any and all proprietary or third-party software first made available to such other Party from and after the Effective Date, solely to the extent such provision would not violate the providing Party’s duty of confidentiality owed to any third party.  In the event there are any additional licensing or other costs associated with the provision of third party software pursuant to this Section 2.1(k), such costs shall be borne by the Service Recipient. 

(l) Subject to Section 2.2, the Parties agree that the Services constitute all of the services to be provided to the [Winning Bidder] Group by Disney (or its Group members or SubContractors, as the case may be) from and after the Effective Date. 

(m) [Winning Bidder] and its Group members shall not be granted or have access to Disney computer systems.  If there is a need for such access, [Winning Bidder] and Disney will agree on the terms pursuant to which such access will be allowed.

(n) Each Party agrees to comply with the data processing addendum attached to and incorporated into this Agreement as set forth in Schedule D, which shall govern the Processing of Personal Data (as such terms are defined in Schedule D) in connection with the provision and receipt of the Services.  The Parties shall, as reasonably requested by the other Party, execute all further documents as are reasonably necessary to comply with applicable Data Protection Laws (as defined in Schedule D) in connection with the provision and receipt of the Services. 

(o) Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this Agreement, the Parties acknowledge and agree that, as between the Parties, any and all Service Recipient Data shall be owned by the Service Recipient or its applicable Group member, subject to any limitations imposed by third-party vendors or subcontractors with respect thereto, and any and all Service Provider Data shall be owned by the Service Provider or its applicable Group member, subject to any limitations imposed by third-party vendors or subcontractors with respect thereto. 

(p) Any work product, deliverables, documents, code, materials or other tangible or intangible output (other than Service Recipient Data and Service Provider Data, which are addressed above) created, developed or prepared by or on behalf of the Service Provider specifically for the Service Recipient in the course of performing the Services (collectively, “Deliverables”) shall be owned by the Service Recipient, and the Service Provider hereby assigns, and shall cause its Group members and Sub-Contractors to assign, to the Service Recipient all right, title and interest in and to such Deliverables, including any intellectual property rights therein or thereto. 

2.2 <u>Omitted Service Amendments.  From time to time during the thirty (30)-day</u> period commencing on the Effective Date, [Winning Bidder] may request Disney to provide, or cause the applicable Service Provider or Sub-Contractor to provide, services (other than Excluded Services) on a going-forward basis that are not identified in Schedule A that (a) were provided, directly or indirectly, by CAI (or its Subsidiaries) to the Business during the three (3) months prior to the Reference Date, (b) are reasonably necessary for the continued operation of the Purchased Assets for substantially the same purpose and in substantially the same manner as it was conducted during the three (3) months prior to the Reference Date and (c) a Service Recipient cannot otherwise secure from a third party on commercially reasonable terms (“Omitted Services”).  In the event that [Winning Bidder] desires to have Disney provide, or cause the applicable Service Provider or Sub-Contractor to provide, Omitted Services and Disney is reasonably capable of providing, or causing the applicable Service Provider or SubContractor to provide, such Omitted Services, then [Winning Bidder] and Disney shall in good faith negotiate an amendment to Schedule A, which shall describe in detail the service, project scope, term, price and payment terms applicable to such Omitted Services.  Any request for an Omitted Service shall be in writing and shall specify (x) the type and the scope of the requested service, (y) where and to whom the requested service is to be provided and (z) the proposed term for the requested service; provided, that the term of any Omitted Service shall terminate no later than the lesser of (i) sixty (60) days following the Effective Date and (ii) seventy-five (75) days following the conclusion of the Auction.  Once agreed to in writing, the amendment to <u>Schedule A shall be deemed part of this Agreement as of such date and the Omitted Services</u> shall be deemed “Services” provided hereunder, subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement.

2.3 <u>No Management Authority.  No Service Provider (or any Group member or Sub-</u> Contractor of a Service Provider) shall be authorized by, or shall have any responsibility under, this Agreement to manage the affairs of the business of any Service Recipient. 

2.4 <u>Employment Offer.  Disney shall allow [Winning Bidder] to interview and make</u> employment offers to former CAI employees who wish to consider opportunities with [Winning Bidder] and shall work in good faith with [Winning Bidder] to transition those employees who accept an offer. Disney shall make reasonably available the former CAI employees for the purpose described in the immediately preceding sentence and shall not interfere with, hinder or take any other action intended to impede the interview or offer process; provided, however, that such employees may still perform their day-to-day work obligations in the ordinary course; <u>provided, further, that Disney may designate up to 20% of the former CAI employees as outside</u> the scope of such process and need not make available or allow any party a right to interview or make employment offers to such employees. 

# **ARTICLE III** 

# **COMPENSATION** 

# 3.1 <u>Compensation for Services.</u> 

(a) As consideration for the Services received by Service Recipient in accordance with this Agreement, Service Recipient shall pay, or cause to be paid, to Service Provider the amounts calculated in accordance with the Cost of Services (such aggregate amount, the “Service <u>Fees”), in addition to the Service Recipient’s reimbursement of the Service Provider for any</u> Third-Party Costs in accordance with the procedures set forth in Section 3.2. 

(b) The amount of any sales Tax, value-added Tax, goods and services Tax or similar Tax that is required to be assessed and remitted by any Service Provider in connection with the Services provided hereunder (“Sales Taxes”) shall be promptly paid to the Service Provider by the Service Recipient in accordance with Section 3.2.  Such payment shall be in addition to the Service Fees and Third-Party Costs (unless such Sales Tax is expressly already accounted for in the applicable Service Fees and Third-Party Costs). 

(c) The Parties shall, and shall cause each of their respective Group members to, cooperate with each other in good faith and provide any information and/or documentation reasonably requested from time to time, in each case to enable the other Party to handle any audits, examinations or inquiries with respect to Sales Taxes and to enable the other Party to comply with applicable Law with respect to Sales Taxes. 

(d) All payments by the Service Recipient under this Agreement shall be made without deduction or withholding for Taxes, except as required by applicable Law.  If the Service Recipient is required by applicable Law to deduct or withhold any Taxes, other than Sales Taxes, from any payment under this Agreement (such Taxes, “Withholding Taxes”), the Service Recipient may deduct such Withholding Taxes from the applicable payment, shall timely remit them to the appropriate Governmental Authority and shall have no obligation to pay any additional amount to the Service Recipient in respect thereof.  The Service Recipient shall

provide the Service Provider original receipts or other reasonable evidence of remittance and reasonably cooperate in any related audit, examination or inquiry.  The Service Recipient and the Service Providers shall cooperate in furnishing information and documentation reasonably necessary to support any available foreign tax credit, exemption or reduced rate.  Sales Taxes shall be governed by Sections 3.1(b) and 3.1(c).  The obligations under this Section 3.1(d) shall survive expiration or termination of this Agreement with respect to payments attributable to Services provided before such expiration or termination. 

# 3.2 <u>Payment Terms.</u> 

(a) The Service Provider shall bill the Service Recipient within sixty (60) days after the end of each full calendar month following the Effective Date, or at such other interval specified with respect to a particular Service in Schedule A, an amount equal to the Service Fees and Third-Party Costs due for all Services provided in such month or other specified interval, as applicable, plus any Sales Taxes; provided, that the Service Provider may, in its sole discretion, bill the Service Recipient more frequently for Service Fees and Third-Party Costs as and when the Service Provider receives bills therefor from the applicable third parties.  Invoices shall be directed to the Service Manager appointed by [Winning Bidder] or to such other Person designated in writing from time to time by such Service Manager.  The Service Recipient shall pay such amount in full within thirty (30) days after receipt of each invoice by wire transfer of immediately available funds to the account designated by the Service Provider for this purpose or upon the Service Provider’s written request, directly to the applicable third party.  If the last day of such time period is not a Business Day, the Service Recipient shall pay such amount on or before the following Business Day.  In addition to any other remedies for non-payment, if any payment is not received by the Service Provider on or before the date such amount is due, then a late payment interest charge, calculated at a ten percent (10.0%) per annum rate, compounded monthly, shall immediately begin to accrue and any such late payment interest charges shall become immediately due and payable in addition to the amount otherwise owed under this Agreement and provided for in the applicable invoice. 

(b) If the Service Recipient objects to the amount of any invoice, the Service Recipient shall pay the full amount of the invoice, including any amounts to which the Service Recipient objects, to the Service Provider and shall promptly provide written notice to the Service Provider of any amounts billed and paid to which the Service Recipient objects, reasonably detailing the basis for the Service Recipient’s objection.  Any such objection shall be deemed to be a Dispute hereunder subject to the provisions applicable to Disputes set forth in <u>Section 2.1(d).</u> 

3.3 <u>Cash Calls.  If Disney expects that the Service Provider shall incur material Third-</u> Party Costs in connection with the provision of any Services pursuant to this Agreement, Disney may, at its option, provide a written cash call (“Cash Call”) to [Winning Bidder] of the amount of such Third-Party Costs in advance of the Service Provider incurring the same and [Winning Bidder] shall within five (5) days of receipt of such Cash Call provide such amount to the Service Provider for payment by wire transfer of immediately available funds to the account designated by Disney for this purpose.

3.4 <u>Books and Records.  Disney shall, and shall cause each Service Provider to,</u> maintain complete and accurate books of account and other documentation as reasonably necessary to support the accuracy of invoices issued pursuant to Section 3.2(a). 

# **ARTICLE IV** 

# **TERM** 

4.1 <u>Commencement.  This Agreement is effective as of the date hereof and shall</u> remain in effect with respect to a particular Service until the occurrence of the Applicable Termination Date with respect to such Service, unless earlier terminated (a) in its entirety or with respect to a particular Service, in each case in accordance with Section 4.2, or (b) by mutual consent of the Parties. 

4.2 <u>Termination.  If a Service Provider or Service Recipient materially breaches any</u> of its respective obligations under this Agreement (and the period for resolution of the Dispute relating to such breach set forth in Section 2.1(d) has expired), the non-breaching Party may terminate this Agreement with respect to the Service for which such obligations are owed, effective upon not less than thirty (30) days’ written notice of termination to the breaching Party, if the breaching Party does not cure such breach within thirty (30) days after receiving written notice thereof from the non-breaching Party.  The termination of this Agreement with respect to any Service pursuant to this Section 4.2 shall not affect the Parties’ rights or obligations under this Agreement with respect to any other Service; provided, that any other Service upon which the performance of such Service is dependent or bundled shall also be terminated. 

4.3 <u>Effect of Termination.  In the event of any termination of this Agreement in its</u> entirety or with respect to any Service, Service Provider shall have no further obligation to provide the applicable terminated Services, but each Party, Service Provider and Service Recipient shall remain liable for all of their respective obligations that accrued hereunder prior to the date of such termination, including all obligations of each Service Recipient to pay any amounts due to any Service Provider hereunder pursuant to Section 3.1, Section 3.2 and Section <u>3.3.  In addition to the surviving obligations set forth in the preceding sentence, Section 2.1(o),</u> this Section 4.3, Article V, Article VI, Article VII, Article VIII and Article IX shall each survive the termination of this Agreement.

# **ARTICLE VII** 

# **OTHER COVENANTS** 

# 7.1 <u>Confidentiality.</u> 

(a) Each Party hereby acknowledges that confidential information of such Party or its Group members may be exposed to the other Party or its Group members, or its or their respective Representatives (including, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors), as a result of the activities contemplated by this Agreement (such information, “Confidential

<u>Information”).  Each Party agrees that Confidential Information is a valuable asset of its owner,</u> and its public disclosure or use outside of the activities contemplated by this Agreement without the prior consent of the relevant Party or its Group member would cause substantial harm to such disclosing Party or other Person.  Therefore, each Party agrees to treat, and to cause its Group members and its and their respective Representatives (including, in the case of Disney, its SubContractors) to treat, all Confidential Information received by it with the amount of care that a reasonable business Person would use to protect its own valuable and proprietary Confidential Information and not to disclose any Confidential Information to any Person who does not have a contractual or fiduciary obligation with such Person to keep such Confidential Information confidential.  In addition, neither Party shall utilize or permit its Group members, or its or their respective Representatives (including, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors), to utilize Confidential Information for any purpose other than for the benefit of the [Winning Bidder] or the owner of such information, as the case may be. 

(b) Notwithstanding the foregoing, each Party may disclose Confidential Information to its Group members and its and their respective Representatives (including, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors), on a need-to-know basis; provided that such Party shall remain liable for any breach of this Section 7.1 by its Group members and its and their respective Representatives (including, in the case of Disney, its Sub-Contractors). 

(c) Except as may be required by Law or stock exchange rule, neither Party shall, without the prior written consent of the other Party, advertise, issue any press release or otherwise publicize the fact that the Parties have entered into this Agreement or disclose the terms and conditions of this Agreement. 

(d) As used herein, “Confidential Information” shall not include information (i) that has become generally available to the public through no fault of the receiving Person, (ii) either (x) is known to the receiving Person prior to being furnished by the disclosing Person, or (y) becomes available to the receiving Person on a non-confidential basis from a source other than the disclosing Person; provided that the source was not prohibited by any legal, contractual or fiduciary obligation to the disclosing Person or any third party from disclosing such information, or (iii) disclosed to the extent necessary pursuant to (1) any Law, (2) the order of a court of competent jurisdiction, (3) any rule or regulation of an administrative agency, (4) any stock exchange rule or other market or reporting system, (5) any deposition, interrogatory, oral questions, request for information or documents, subpoena, civil investigative demand or similar process, (6) any required report, statement or testimony submitted to any municipal, state or national (including foreign regulatory) bodies having or claiming to have jurisdiction, or (7) other legal, judicial, arbitration, regulatory or administrative process or proceeding; provided that, under the circumstances described in this subparagraph (iii), the Person required to make the disclosure shall, to the extent legally permissible, notify in writing the Person whose Confidential Information shall be disclosed of such event and shall cooperate with any reasonable request or efforts by such Person, at such Person’s sole expense, to take reasonable legally permissible actions to limit the scope of disclosure required in connection therewith and shall exercise reasonable efforts to obtain assurance that confidential treatment shall be accorded to such Confidential Information.

(e) The terms of this Section 7.1 shall continue to apply after the expiration or termination of this Agreement for (i) two (2) years with respect to Confidential Information concerning (A) the intellectual property rights of a Party or its Group members, (B) clients, vendors and customers of a Party or its Group members and (C) the business plans of a Party or its Group members and (ii) one (1) year with respect to all other Confidential Information. 

7.2 <u>Use of Disney Name.  [Winning Bidder] shall acquire no right under this</u> Agreement to use, and [Winning Bidder] shall not use, the name “Disney”, “ABC”, “ESPN”, “Lucasfilm”, “Marvel” or “Pixar” (either alone or in conjunction with or as a part of any other word, mark or name) or any marks, fanciful characters or designs of Disney or any of its Group members (including, for purposes of clarity and the avoidance of doubt, The Walt Disney Company) in any manner whatsoever.
