# **ARTICLE IX** 

# **MISCELLANEOUS** 

9.1 <u>Force Majeure.  In case performance of any terms or provisions hereof shall be</u> delayed or prevented, in whole or in part, because of or related to compliance with any Law or requirement of any national securities exchange, or because of riot, war, public disturbance, pandemic (including any lockdowns, quarantines, “shelter in place” or “stay at home” orders, shutdowns, closures, sequesters and other protective measures in response thereto by any Governmental Authority), strike, labor dispute, unavailability of labor, fire, explosion, storm, flood, act of God or act of terrorism that is not within the control of the performing Party, Service Provider or Service Recipient whose performance is interfered with (each, a “Performing <u>Party”) and which by the exercise of reasonable diligence such Performing Party is unable to</u> prevent, or for any other reason which is not within the control of such Performing Party whose performance is interfered with and which by the exercise of reasonable diligence such Performing Party is unable to prevent (each, a “Force Majeure Event”), then upon prompt written notice, stating the date and extent of such interference and the Force Majeure Event which is the cause thereof, by the Performing Party to the other Party, Service Recipient or Service Provider (each, an “Affected Party”), as applicable, the Performing Party shall be excused from its obligations hereunder during the period such Force Majeure Event or its effects continue, and no liability shall attach against either the Performing Party or the Affected Party on account thereof; <u>provided, however, that the Performing Party shall promptly resume the required performance</u> upon the cessation of the Force Majeure Event or its effects.  No Performing Party shall be excused from performance under this Section 9.1 if such Performing Party fails to use commercially reasonable efforts to mitigate the effects of the Force Majeure Event on such Performing Party’s ability to perform hereunder. 

9.2 <u>Relationship of Parties.  Nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed or construed</u> by the Parties or any third party as creating a relationship of principal and agent, partnership or joint venture between the Parties, between any Service Provider and any Service Recipient or with any individual providing Services, it being understood and agreed that no provision

contained herein, and no act of any Party or its Group members, shall be deemed to create any relationship between the Parties or Group members other than the relationship set forth herein. Each Party and each Service Provider shall act under this Agreement solely as an independent contractor and not as an agent or employee of the other Party or any of such Party’s Group members. 

# 9.3 <u>Counterparts; Effectiveness; Entire Agreement.</u> 

(a) This Agreement may be executed in counterparts, all of which shall be considered one and the same agreement and shall become effective when counterparts have been signed by each of the Parties and delivered to the other Party, it being understood that each Party need not sign the same counterpart.  Delivery of a copy of this Agreement bearing a Party’s signature by electronic mail in PDF format or by other similar electronic means shall have the same effect as physical delivery of the paper document bearing the original signature. 

(b) This Agreement, the other Transaction Documents and the other agreements referred to herein and therein constitute the entire agreement of the Parties with respect to the subject matter hereof and thereof and supersede all prior agreements and undertakings, both written and oral, among the Parties with respect to the subject matter hereof and thereof. 

9.4 <u>Governing Law; Dispute Resolution.  This Agreement, and all claims or causes of</u> action whether in contract or tort, that may be based on or arise out of or relate to this Agreement, shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the Laws of the State of Delaware (without regard to any applicable principles of conflicts of law).  Each party hereto (a) irrevocably submits to the personal jurisdiction of the United States District Court for the District of Delaware, or if and only if the United States District Court does not have jurisdiction, then before the Court of Chancery of the State of Delaware, or if and only if neither such court shall have jurisdiction, then before any other state or federal court sitting in Delaware having subject matter jurisdiction, and (b) agrees that any actions or proceedings arising in connection with this Agreement shall be brought, tried and determined exclusively in the courts referred to in clause (a) of this Section 9.4 and consents to service of process outside the State of Delaware pursuant to the requirements of such court in any matter subject to it.  The parties irrevocably waive, to the fullest extent permitted by law, any objection that they may have to the laying of venue of any such action or proceeding in such courts and any claim that any such action or proceeding brought in any such court has been brought in an inconvenient forum. 

9.5 <u>Assignment.  This Agreement and the rights and obligations hereunder shall not</u> be assignable by either Party without the express written consent of the other Party; provided, that, without such consent, Disney may assign its rights and obligations hereunder to an Affiliate at any time.  Any attempted assignment in violation of this Section 9.5 shall be void.  Subject to the preceding sentences, this Agreement shall be binding upon, inure to the benefit of, and be enforceable by, the Parties and their respective successors and permitted assigns.  Nothing in this <u>Section 9.5 shall affect or impair (a) Disney’s ability to designate one or more Group members to</u> provide Services as a Service Provider hereunder, or a Service Provider’s ability to delegate any or all of its obligations under this Agreement to one or more Group members or Sub-Contractors pursuant to Section 2.1(f), or (b) [Winning Bidder]’s ability to designate one or more Group members to receive Services as a Service Recipient hereunder.

# **<u>SCHEDULE D</u>** 

# **Data Processing Addendum** 

1. <u>Definitions.</u> 

   - (a) “Data Protection Law” means any Law (including any and all legislative and/or regulatory amendments or successors thereto) regarding privacy, data protection, information security, and/or the Processing of Personal Data. 

   - (b) “Personal Data” means any Service Recipient Data that relates to, or can be used to identify, contact, or precisely locate an identified or identifiable individual, and information considered to be personal data or personal information as defined under, or otherwise governed by, applicable Data Protection Law. 

   - (c) “Process,” “Processing” or “Processed” means any operation or set of operations which is performed upon Personal Data irrespective of the purposes and means applied, including access, collection, recording, organization, structuring, adaptation, alteration, retrieval, consultation, retention, storage, transfer, disclosure (including disclosure by transmission), dissemination or otherwise making available, alignment, combination, use, blocking, erasure and destruction. 

   - (d) “Security Incident” means any unlawful or unauthorized Processing, destruction, alteration, disclosure, loss, acquisition or access to Personal Data or any other compromise of Personal Data in the possession or control of Service Provider. For the avoidance of doubt, Service Recipient Systems are not in the possession, custody or control of Service Provider. 

   - (e) “Service Recipient Systems” means any system in the possession, custody or control of Service Recipient. 

   - (f) Unless otherwise defined, the capitalized terms in this Schedule D shall have the same meaning as such terms in the Agreement. 

2. <u>Data Processing.  The Parties acknowledge that Service Provider and its personnel may</u> provide limited professional services as a courtesy to Service Recipient in connection with the Services, and that such Services will be provided by Service Provider using only Service Recipient Systems.  Service Recipient remains solely responsible for determining the purposes and means of Processing, providing lawful and documented instructions, ensuring that such instructions comply with applicable Data Protection Laws, and satisfying all controller obligations with respect to Personal Data.  Service Provider will Process Personal Data only as reasonably necessary to provide the Services and only on Service Recipient’s documented instructions, except as otherwise required by Law.  To the extent Service Provider’s personnel access Personal Data, such access will be conducted directly through Service Recipient Systems using Service Recipient-managed laptops or devices, and Service Provider will not be required to receive, host, copy, retain, or maintain Personal Data outside of Service Recipient Systems unless expressly agreed in writing.

Service Provider’s obligations as processor are limited to the obligations required by Data Protection Laws and those expressly set forth in this Schedule D, and Service Provider will otherwise have no liability for Service Recipient Systems or Service Recipient’s instructions, data, notices, consents, legal bases, or other controller obligations.  Service Provider agrees that it will: 

   - (a) follow Service Recipient’s lawful, documented instructions for Processing Personal Data, provided that Service Provider shall not be responsible for determining whether such instructions comply with Data Protection Laws; 

   - (b) promptly notify Service Recipient if Service Provider determines an instruction of Service Recipient with respect to Personal Data is unlawful; 

   - (c) promptly notify Service Recipient if Service Provider determines that it can no longer comply with Service Recipient’s documented instructions or applicable processor obligations under this Schedule D; 

   - (d) ensure that Service Provider’s personnel who access Personal Data are subject to appropriate confidentiality obligations and receive privacy and security training appropriate to their role; 

   - (e) provide reasonable information in Service Provider’s possession or control necessary to demonstrate compliance with this <u>Schedule D, provided that unless otherwise</u> required by Data Protection Law, any review or audit will be limited to information reasonably necessary to assess Service Provider’s processor obligations and will not require access to Service Provider’s systems, networks, or any confidential information; and 

   - (f) provide reasonable assistance to Service Recipient to the extent such assistance is reasonably necessary for Service Recipient to comply with its controller obligations under Data Protection Laws and relates to Personal Data Processed by Service Provider under this Agreement. 

3. <u>Data Security.  Service Provider shall maintain reasonable and appropriate administrative,</u> technical, and organizational measures designed to protect Personal Data Processed by Service Provider against unauthorized Processing, access, disclosure, or loss, taking into account the nature of the limited access provided and the fact that access will occur through Service Recipient Systems on Service Recipient-managed laptops or devices. 

4. <u>Security Incident.  In the event Service Provider becomes aware of a Security Incident, it</u> shall notify Service Recipient promptly and without undue delay and in any event within forty-eight (48) hours of becoming aware of the Security Incident.  Unless otherwise required by applicable Law, Service Provider shall not notify any third party of, or make any public announcement concerning, the Security Incident where such notification or announcement expressly identifies Service Recipient without Service Recipient’s prior written consent.  Service Provider shall promptly investigate the Security Incident to the

extent possible within its reasonable capacity.  In the event of legal action brought by either Party against a third party in connection with a Security Incident, Service Provider and Service Recipient each agree that it shall provide such assistance as may be reasonably necessary to enable the other Party to successfully prosecute such legal action. 

5. <u>Data Subject Requests and Regulatory Requests.  Service Provider will provide reasonable</u> assistance to the extent Service Provider is legally required to assist and such assistance relates to Personal Data Processed by Service Provider under this Agreement.  Because access to Personal Data is expected to occur through Service Recipient Systems and Service Provider is not expected to separately host or retain Personal Data, Service Provider’s data subject and regulatory requests obligations shall apply only to Personal Data, if any, in Service Provider’s possession or control. 

6. <u>Controller Obligations.  Service Recipient will comply with all obligations of a controller</u> with respect to the Processing of Personal Data pursuant to this Agreement, including providing all required notices, obtaining all required consents or other legal bases, responding to data subject and regulatory requests, and ensuring that its instructions to Service Provider are lawful, complete, accurate, and documented. 

7. <u>Termination of Processing.  Upon termination or expiration of the Agreement, Service</u> Provider shall cease Processing Personal Data as soon as reasonably practicable, except to the extent continued Processing is required by Data Protection Law or necessary to complete wind-down activities.  Because access to Personal Data is expected to occur through Service Recipient Systems and Service Provider is not expected to separately host or retain Personal Data, Service Provider’s return or deletion obligations shall apply only to Personal Data, if any, in Service Provider’s possession or control. 

8. <u>Cross-Border Transfer.  In the event that Data Protection Laws impose restrictions on the</u> cross-border transfer of Personal Data that are not contemplated herein, the Parties agree to meet in good faith to complete any formalities and enter any documents as may be required by such Data Protection Laws. 

9. For the avoidance of doubt, the terms of this Schedule D shall not apply where both Parties agree that they act as independent controllers with regard to applicable Personal Data.  In the event that both Parties act as independent controllers with regard to any Services, the Parties shall negotiate in good faith the terms under which such Services shall be provided.

# **<u>EXHIBIT B</u>** 

# **Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement**

# **STALKING HORSE TRANSACTION AGREEMENT** 

This STALKING HORSE TRANSACTION AGREEMENT (this “ **Agreement** ”) is entered into by and between Disney Worldwide Services, Inc., a Florida corporation (“ **Licensee** ”), and Character Technologies, Inc., a Delaware corporation (“ **Licensor** ”).  Capitalized terms used, but not otherwise defined, herein shall have their respective meanings set forth in the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement. 

# W I T N E S S E T H 

WHEREAS, Licensor and Licensee are parties to that certain letter agreement, dated September 18, 2026 (the “ **Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement** ”) and that certain Transition Services Agreement, dated as of September 18, 2026 as amended on October 13, 2026 (the “ **TSA** ”); 

WHEREAS, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement contemplates that Licensor and Licensee will execute this Agreement, and this Agreement is being entered into by Licensor and Licensee to satisfy the requirements described therein; 

WHEREAS, in connection with the transactions contemplated by the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, Licensor intends to provide Licensee with the License Grant of the Licensed IP and convey to Licensee, and Licensee intends to purchase from Licensor, the Purchased Employee Claims on the terms and conditions set forth herein; 

WHEREAS, in connection with the transactions contemplated by the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, Licensee in coordination with Licensor, has made offers to and hired certain employees of Licensor, including certain of Licensor’s officers, and is providing transition services for a period of time to the Licensor pursuant to the TSA, and Licensor has provided a release of claims for the benefit of such employees, each in an effort to assist the Licensor in its efforts to maximize value and minimize employee attrition risk; 

WHEREAS, the parties acknowledge and agree that the transactions contemplated by this Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA have been and are, made at arm’s length and in good faith; 

WHEREAS, in furtherance of the foregoing, Licensor intends to commence a voluntary case (the “ **Chapter 11 Case** ”) under chapter 11 of title 11 of the United States Code, 11 U.S.C. §§ 101-1532 (the “ **Bankruptcy Code** ”), in the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “ **Bankruptcy Court** ”); 

WHEREAS, the execution and delivery of this Agreement is subject to, among other things, the entry of the 363 Free and Clear Order; and 

WHEREAS, the parties desire to consummate the proposed transaction (“ **Proposed Transaction** ”) contemplated by this Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA as promptly as practicable after the Bankruptcy Court enters the 363 Free and Clear Order.

NOW, THEREFORE, subject to the terms, conditions, covenants and provisions of this Agreement, the TSA, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the 363 Free and Clear Order (collectively, the “ **Transaction Documents** ”), for good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged by this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents (as applicable), Licensor and Licensee, for themselves, their successors and assigns, mutually covenant and agree as follows:

2. <u>Certain Capitalized Terms.  Capitalized terms used but not defined herein shall have the</u> meanings given to them in the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or the Term Sheet. 

3. <u>License Scope.</u> 

   - 3.1. <u>Grant of License.  Subject to Section 3.2 and effective as of the Closing, Licensor</u> shall grant and hereby grants to Licensee, and Licensee accepts, on the terms and conditions set forth herein, a perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive, transferable (in accordance with <u>Section 3.3), sublicensable (through multiple tiers; in</u> accordance with Section 3.4), non-terminable, irrevocable, fully paid-up, royaltyfree right and license: (a) to use, integrate, copy and reproduce, distribute, execute, display, perform, modify and make Derivative Works of, the Licensed Technology in connection with Licensee Products and Services, and (b) under the Licensed Patents to make, have made, use, have used, sell, offer for sale, import, export, distribute, develop, and otherwise commercially exploit Licensee Products and Services, and to practice any and all methods and processes claimed in the Licensed Patents in connection with the foregoing (the Licensed Technology and Licensed Patents, collectively, the “ **Licensed IP,** ” and such grant of license set forth in this Section 3.1, the “ **License Grant** ”).  As set forth more fully in <u>Section 13 below, the License Grant shall be a license of “intellectual</u> property rights” for the purposes of Section 365(n) of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code. 

   - 3.2. <u>Excluded IP.   For the avoidance of doubt, Licensee acknowledges that the</u> Licensed IP does not include: (a) any Technology or Intellectual Property Rights licensed by Licensor from a third party; (b) any trade names, logos, common law trademarks and service marks, trademark and service mark registrations or any other brand elements, and applications therefor (including intent to use

applications); (c) any domain names, uniform resource locators, other names and locators associated with the Internet, and applications or registrations therefor or social media accounts or handles and any website content; (d) any consumerfacing applications of Licensor; (e) Licensor’s end user data or databases, any personally identifiable information of Licensor end users, any input entered by Licensor end users when engaging with Licensor’s products and services, or any outputs generated for end users by Licensor’s products and services; (f) any Unprocessed Data; or (g) training data or any Finished Models, including each user-selectable conversational model or chat style (collectively, the “ **Excluded IP** ”). 

- 3.3. <u>Transferability.  The License Grant may be assigned or transferred by Licensee</u> without the prior written consent of Licensor only to: (a) any buyer or successor entity in connection with the sale, divestiture or spin-off of a business, asset or entity from Disney (i) that is using the Licensed IP at the time of closing of such sale, divestiture or spin-off or (ii) to which the Licensed IP relates or (b) Affiliates of Licensee (collectively, (a) and (b), “ **Disney Assignees** ”); in each case of the foregoing (a) and (b), with the right to sublicense through multiple tiers solely as set forth in Section 3.4.  For the avoidance of doubt, Licensee may not sublicense (other than as set forth in Section 3.4), transfer or assign (other than as set forth in this Section 3.3) the License Grant on a standalone basis to any third party. 

- 3.4. <u>Sublicensing.  The License Grant may be sublicensed (through multiple tiers) by</u> Licensee or any Disney Assignee only to (a) consumer end users of Licensee Products and Services as necessary for their receipt or use of such Licensee Products and Services, (b) service providers, subcontractors, agents, or vendors and any distributors, resellers, systems integrators or other entities in any applicable channel of distribution (i) of the Licensee Products and Services themselves and with respect to the Licensee Products and Services, (ii) of Disney in connection with the provision of services to Disney with respect to the Licensee Products and Services, or (iii) of a Disney Assignee with respect to the provision of services to such Disney Assignee in connection with the Licensee Products and Services, (c) Affiliates of Disney (but only for so long as such entity is an Affiliate of Disney) and (d) a Disney Assignee in accordance with Section 3.3, but only to conduct the acquired business as it was conducted as of such divestiture or spinoff (and any natural extensions or evolutions thereof).  Licensee shall ensure that its sublicensees comply with all of the applicable provisions of this Agreement and shall be responsible and liable to Licensor in the event that any of its sublicensees fail to comply with such provisions. 

- 3.5. <u>No Implied Restrictions.  No provision of this Agreement shall be construed to</u> require Licensee to meet minimum sales, development, or commercialization obligations, or to mark products. 

- 3.6. <u>Survival of License.  The License Grant shall survive any (a) termination of the</u> Transaction Documents or this Agreement; (b) change of control, or assignment or exclusive licensing of the Licensed IP, in whole or in part, occurring after the

Closing; and (c) bankruptcy or insolvency of Licensor (including the protections available under Section 365(n) of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code). 

   - 3.7. <u>Delivery</u> _._ On and after the date hereof and up to the Closing, Licensor and Licensee shall meet and confer on the process, information and other steps necessary to enable Licensor to make available to Licensee on the Closing the Licensed Technology pursuant to the License Grant by providing Licensee with access to the relevant GitHub and Google Drive repositories (or any other means necessary to make the Licensed Technology available to Licensee). Licensor shall implement such access for a thirty (30) day period and Licensee shall reasonably cooperate in facilitating such implementation. 

   - 3.8. <u>Prosecution.  Licensor shall have no obligation to prosecute, maintain, enforce,</u> support or update the Licensed IP. 

4. <u>Ownership.</u> 

   - 4.1. <u>Ownership of Licensed IP.  Licensor shall retain ownership of all right, title and</u> interest in the Licensed IP and may continue to use, develop, license and otherwise exploit or monetize the Licensed IP. 

   - 4.2. <u>Ownership of Derivative Works.  Licensee shall own any right, title and interest</u> in all modifications, improvements, enhancements, Derivative Works (other than the original work that is Licensed IP for such Derivative Work) and Outputs (including any models, weights and synthetic data), in each case created by or on behalf of Licensee with respect to the Licensed IP.  For the avoidance of doubt, this <u>Section 4.2 shall not be deemed an assignment of any of the foregoing</u> (including any Intellectual Property Rights therein or thereto) from Licensor to Licensee. Licensor and any successor (including a buyer of Licensor’s Intellectual Property Rights) shall not (a) challenge, through the courts, administrative governmental bodies, private organizations, or in any other manner the rights of Licensee to exploit the Outputs by any means whatsoever, or (b) thwart, hinder or subvert the intent of the grants to Licensee herein; <u>provided that, in each case of the foregoing (a) and (b), Licensor does not</u> otherwise have a reasonable basis to assert that the Outputs infringe upon Licensor’s Intellectual Property Rights that were not part of the Licensed IP and such Outputs were not generated in violation of, and do not relate to a violation of, this Agreement with respect to Licensed IP. 

   - 4.3. <u>Confidentiality.  Licensee shall treat any source code in the Licensed Technology</u> as confidential information of Licensor pursuant to the Mutual NDA and will be treated in a confidential manner until it ceases to qualify as confidential information. 

# 5. <u>Additional Covenants.</u> 

- 5.1. <u>Cash Consideration.  For the avoidance of doubt, the up-front payment in the</u> amount of $5.5 million (the “ **Up-Front Payment** ”) and the refundable deposit in

the amount of $2 million (the “ **Refundable Deposit** ”) were paid by Licensee to Licensor on the date of, and in accordance with, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the remaining $12.5 million of the Cash Consideration owed shall be paid upon the Closing (the “ **Remaining Cash Consideration** ”). The Refundable Deposit shall be promptly and fully refundable to Licensee if (i) Licensee is not selected as the winning bidder (to the extent there is an auction), (ii) the Proposed Transaction is not consummated prior to the Outside Date (as may be extended by mutual agreement) or (iii) Licensee is entitled to a refund under Section 1(b) of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement. To the extent the Refundable Deposit is payable to Licensee in accordance herewith, or in accordance with the terms of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, Licensee shall provide a notice to Licensor specifying the account to which the Licensor shall wire funds in the amount of the Refundable Deposit (the “ **Refundable Deposit Notice** ”), and the Licensor shall wire such funds to Licensee no later than three business days following the date of the Refundable Deposit Notice. 

- 5.2. <u>Conveyance of Employee Claims.  Effective as of the Closing, Licensor shall</u> transfer and convey to Licensee, and Licensee shall purchase and accept, for good and valuable consideration any unreleased claims that Licensor has against any former Licensor employees that accept offers of employment with Disney or any of its Affiliates (the “ **Purchased Employee Claims** ”). 

- 5.3. <u>Bankruptcy Court Filings.</u> 

(a) The Licensor shall consult with the Licensee concerning the Bidding Procedures Order and the 363 Free and Clear Order, as well as any other orders of the Bankruptcy Court relating to the Proposed Transaction, and the bankruptcy proceedings in connection therewith, and will provide the Licensee with copies of any material applications, pleadings, notices, proposed orders, and other documents to be filed by the Licensor in the Chapter 11 Case that relate in any way to the Proposed Transaction at least three (3) business days prior to the making of any such filing or submission to the Bankruptcy Court or as soon as reasonably practicable.  The Licensor shall provide the Licensee with prompt notice of (A) the filing of any objection to (or any threat or notice of intention by any person to file any objection to) this Agreement, the Proposed Transaction, the Bidding Procedures Motion, the Licensee or (B) the commencement of (or any threat or notice of intention of any person to commence), any action, suit, proceeding, whether at law or in equity or by or before any governmental authority or in arbitration, that relates in any way to this Agreement, the Bidding Procedures Order, or the Proposed Transaction. 

(b) Licensor shall file the Bidding Procedures Motion with the Bankruptcy Court on or prior to October 13, 2026. 

(c) Licensor shall cause the Bid Deadline to occur by November 20, 2026, and, if an auction is necessary, shall cause the auction to occur by

November 23, 2026, or such later dates as may be required by the Bankruptcy Court. 

(d) Licensor shall not take any action to cause the Chapter 11 Case to be dismissed or converted to a case under chapter 7 of the Bankruptcy Code prior to the Closing. 

   - 5.4. <u>Diligence Access.  The Licensor shall give or have given, as applicable, Licensee</u> reasonable access to diligence relevant to the Proposed Transaction consistent with the Term Sheet. 

   - 5.5. <u>Expenses.  Each party will be responsible for its own costs and expenses,</u> including, without limitation, all fees and expenses of such party’s representatives, incurred in connection with a potential transaction, including the negotiation of the term sheet and the negotiation, delivery and execution of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the definitive agreements underlying the Proposed Transaction. 

6. <u>Representations and Warranties. The following representations and warranties are made as</u> of the date of this Agreement. 

   - 6.1. <u>Proprietary Rights Warranty. 13. <u>Section 365(n) of the Bankruptcy Code.  After the Closing, the Parties agree that (i) this</u> Agreement constitutes an executory contract subject to the provisions of Section 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, and (ii) any and all licenses granted to Licensee under this Agreement, are “intellectual property” for purposes of Section 365(n) of the Bankruptcy Code.  Notwithstanding the commencement of any proceeding under the Bankruptcy Code or comparable insolvency proceeding by or against Licensor or any subsequent purchaser of all or a portion of the Licensed IP or any Intellectual Property Rights therein after the Closing, (a) all rights in the Licensed IP granted to Licensee under this Agreement shall be deemed fully retained by and vested in Licensee as protected intellectual property rights under Section 365(n), and (b) Licensee shall have all of

the rights afforded to non-debtor licensees under Section 365(n).  The Parties further agree that, after the Closing and in the event of a rejection of this Agreement by Licensor in any bankruptcy proceeding by or against Licensor under the Bankruptcy Code or rejection or repudiation by a foreign representative in a foreign bankruptcy proceeding that is subject to chapter 15 of the Bankruptcy Code, if Licensee elects to retain its rights under this Agreement pursuant to Section 365(n)(1)(B), Licensor shall not interfere with Licensee’s rights to the Licensed IP and all embodiments thereof in accordance with Section 365(n)(3)(B).  Upon the initiation of a voluntary or involuntary bankruptcy proceeding regarding Licensor under the laws of any jurisdiction, anywhere in the world, after the Closing, any rights in Licensed IP granted by Licensor to Licensee pursuant to this Agreement shall survive the bankruptcy of Licensor to the extent permitted under applicable Law.  Without limiting the foregoing, Section 365(n) shall apply to any case commenced under chapter 15 of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code and, if the foreign representative in such a case rejects this Agreement within the meaning of Section 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, after the Closing, Licensee shall be entitled to make the election and exercise the rights (and be bound by any consequent obligations) described in Section 365(n) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# 14. <u>Conditions Precedent to Closing.</u> 

14.1. <u>Mutual Conditions. The respective obligations of Licensor and Licensee to</u> consummate the Closing shall be subject to the satisfaction of, at or prior to the Closing, of each of the following conditions, any of which may, to the extent permitted by applicable law, be waived in writing by both Licensor and Licensee: 

- (a) The closing of the Initial Transaction and all of the conditions precedent thereto shall have occurred. 

- (b) The absence of laws or court orders prohibiting, enjoining or otherwise staying the License Transaction. 

- (c) Licensor’s board, acting upon the recommendation of a special committee of the board comprised solely of independent directors, will have reviewed and approved the License Transaction, including this Agreement, the transaction economics, and, as applicable, the Break-Up Fee and Expense Reimbursement, bid procedures, and the form of 363 Free and Clear Order. 

- (d) Receipt of all required approvals to complete the License Transaction, including any consents required related to the License Grant and/or the Licensed IP, if any. 

14.2. <u>Conditions to Obligations of Licensee. The obligation of Licensee to consummate</u> the Closing shall be subject to the satisfaction of, at or prior to the Closing, of each of the following conditions, any of which may, to the extent permitted by applicable law, be waived in writing by Licensee in its sole discretion: 

- (a) The Licensor shall have given Licensee reasonable access to diligence relevant to the Proposed Transaction consistent with the Term Sheet. 

- (b) Other than the 363 Free and Clear Order, the Transaction Documents shall be reasonably satisfactory to Licensee.

- (c) Licensor will have provided Licensee with access to, and copies of, the Licensed Technology pursuant to Section 3.7, which excludes the Excluded IP. 

- (d) No material adverse development shall have occurred with respect to the Licensed IP or the Key Employee group. 

- (e) The Bankruptcy Court shall have entered the 363 Free and Clear Order, which shall be a final and non-appealable order and the entered order shall be in form and substance acceptable to Licensee in its sole and absolute discretion. 

- (f) The 363 Free and Clear Order shall not have been stayed as of the Closing, stayed pending appeal, reversed or vacated. 

- (g) Licensor shall have delivered or have caused to be delivered to Licensee those deliverables set forth in Section 7.2. 

- (h) The representations and warranties of Licensor contained in this Agreement shall be true and correct in all material respects as of the date of this Agreement and at and as of the Closing with the same force and effect as if made at and as of the Closing (other than those representations and warranties that address matters only as of a particular date or only with respect to a particular period of time, which need only be true and correct in all material respects as of such or with respect to such period). 

- (i) The Licensor shall have performed or complied in all material respects with all agreements and covenants required by this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents to be performed or complied with by the Licensor on or prior to the Closing. 

14.3. <u>Conditions to Obligations of Licensor. The obligation of Licensor to consummate</u> the Closing shall be subject to the satisfaction, at or prior to the Closing, of each of the following conditions, any of which may, to the extent permitted by applicable law, be waived in writing by Licensor in its sole discretion: 

- (a) The Licensor shall have received the Remaining Cash Consideration and release of Licensee’s Refundable Deposit. 

- (b) Other than the 363 Free and Clear Order, the Transaction Documents shall be reasonably satisfactory to Licensor. 

- (c) Licensee shall have delivered or have caused to be delivered to Licensor those deliverables set forth in Section 7.3. 

- (d) The representations and warranties of Licensee contained in this Agreement shall be true and correct in all material respects as of the date of this Agreement and at and as of the Closing with the same force and effect as if made at and as of the Closing (other than those representations and warranties that address matters only as of a particular date or only with respect to a particular period of time, which need

- (e) The Licensee shall have performed or complied in all material respects with all agreements and covenants required by this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents to be performed or complied with by the Licensee on or prior to the Closing.

16. <u>Termination.</u> 

   - 16.1. <u>Termination.  This Agreement may be terminated at any time prior to the Closing:</u> 

      - (a) by mutual consent of Licensor and Licensee; 

(b) by Licensor if: (i) Licensee shall have breached or violated any of its representations, warranties or covenants set forth in this Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or the TSA or in any certificate delivered by Licensor in connection herewith in a manner that, either individually, or in the aggregate, would prevent the satisfaction of the conditions to Closing set forth herein; (ii) Licensee shall have materially breached the Bidding Procedures Order, and in each case, such breach or violation shall not have been cured within ten (10) days after written notice thereof has been given by Licensor to Licensee, which notice must specify in reasonable detail the alleged breach; _provided_ that Licensor shall not be entitled to terminate this Agreement if the failure of the Closing to be consummated by such date is caused by Licensor’s breach of any of its obligations under this Agreement; or (iii) the board of directors of Licensor determines, after consultation with, and advice from, outside counsel, that proceeding with the Proposed Transaction would be inconsistent with its fiduciary duties. 

(c) by Licensee if: (i) Licensor shall have breached or violated any of its representations, warranties or covenants set forth in this Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or the TSA, or in any certificate delivered by Licensee in connection herewith in a manner that, either individually, or in the aggregate, would prevent the satisfaction of the conditions to Closing set forth herein; (ii) Licensor fails to file the Chapter 11 Case with the Bankruptcy Court by October 12, 2026; (iii) Licensor fails to file the Bidding Procedures Motion with the Bankruptcy Court on or prior to October 13, 2026; (iv) the Bankruptcy Court fails to enter the Bidding Procedures Order, including approval of the Bid

Protections, by the 21st day following the commencement of the Chapter 11 Case or such later date as may be required by the Bankruptcy Court, or if the Bidding Procedures Order has been entered by such date but is stayed, stayed pending appeal, reversed or vacated, or if such Bidding Procedures Order has been entered by such date but has been amended, supplemented or otherwise modified in any manner not reasonably acceptable to Licensee; (v) the Bid Deadline has not occurred by November 20, 2026, or such later date as may be required by the Bankruptcy Court, and, if an auction is necessary, the auction is not held by November 23, 2026, or such later date as may be required by the Bankruptcy Court; (vi) the Bankruptcy Court has not entered the 363 Free and Clear Order by November 30, 2026; (vii) the 363 Free and Clear Order has been timely entered but it is not in form and substance acceptable to Licensee in its sole and absolute discretion; provided, however, that an order in the form of Exhibit A is acceptable to the Licensee; (viii) any of the conditions set forth in this Agreement shall not have been, or if it becomes apparent that any of such conditions will not be, fulfilled by December 17, 2026, unless such failure shall be due to the failure of Licensee to perform or comply with any of the covenants, agreements, or conditions hereof to be performed or complied with by it prior to the Closing; (ix) Licensor shall have materially breached the Bidding Procedures Order or the 363 Free and Clear Order, and in each case, such breach or violation shall not have been cured within ten (10) days after written notice thereof has been given by Licensee to Licensor; _provided_ that Licensee shall not be entitled to terminate this Agreement if the failure of the Closing to be consummated by such date is caused by Licensee’s breach of any of its obligations under this Agreement; (x) the Bankruptcy Court enters an order materially inconsistent with the Bidding Procedures Order or inconsistent with the 363 Free and Clear Order or consummation of the Proposed Transaction; (xi) prior to the Closing, any party obtains a final and unstayed order of the Bankruptcy Court granting relief from the automatic stay to foreclose on any portion of the Licensed IP or the Purchased Employee Claims; (xii) the Chapter 11 Case is dismissed or converted to a case under chapter 7 of the Bankruptcy Code prior to the Closing, or (xiii) if the Closing has not occurred on or prior to December 17, 2026; _provided_ that Licensee shall not be entitled to terminate this Agreement if the failure of the Closing to be consummated by such date is caused by Licensee’s breach of any of its obligations under this Agreement. 

# 16.2. <u>Effect of Termination.</u> 

(a) Except as otherwise provided in this <u>Section 16.2, in the event of</u> termination of this Agreement by either party hereto in accordance with <u>Section 16.1, all rights and obligations of the parties under this Agreement shall</u> terminate without any liability of any party to any other parties hereto, except for (i) Licensor’s obligation to refund promptly to the Licensee the Refundable Deposit in accordance with the terms of the Exclusivity and Prepayment

Agreement, and (ii) liability for fraud or willful breach of this Agreement prior to such termination. 

(b) In consideration for Licensee having expended considerable time and expense in connection with this Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA, and the hiring of former Licensor employees, among other things, and the negotiation thereof, and to compensate Licensee as the stalking horse bidder, and regardless of whether or not Licensee makes any matching or competing bids at the auction (if applicable), Licensor shall pay to Licensee a break-up fee and reasonable expense reimbursement collectively capped at 3.5% of the $20 million cash purchase price (the “ **Bid Protections** ”) in the event that (i) Licensor materially breaches this Agreement or any other Transaction Document, or (ii) Licensor consummates an alternative transaction involving the Transferred Assets, including if Licensee is not the successful bidder at the auction (if applicable) (unless such alternative transaction is a “back-up bid” in the event the Closing does not occur due to the breach by Licensee of any of its obligations under this Agreement).  The Bid Protections shall be due and payable simultaneously with the closing of the alternative transaction. The Bid Protections shall, subject to Bankruptcy Court approval, be treated as an administrative expense in the Chapter 11 Case under Section 503(b)(1)(A) and Section 507(a)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code.  Licensor acknowledges and agrees that: (i) the approval of the Bid Protections is an integral part of the transactions contemplated by this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents; (ii) in the absence of Licensor’s obligation to pay the Bid Protections, Licensee would not have entered into this Agreement or the other Transaction Documents; (iii) the entry of Licensee into this Agreement and the other Transaction Documents is necessary for preservation of the estate of Licensor and is beneficial to Licensor because, in Licensor’s business judgment, it will enhance Licensor’s ability to maximize value for the benefit of their creditors and other stakeholders; (iv) the Bid Protections are reasonable in relation to Licensee’s costs and efforts and to the magnitude of the transactions contemplated hereby and Licensee’s lost opportunities resulting from the time spend pursuing the transactions contemplated hereby; and (v) time is of the essence with respect to the entry of the Bidding Procedures Order. 

(c) If Licensor fails to take any action necessary to cause the delivery of the Bid Protections under circumstances in which Licensee is entitled to the Bid Protections and, in order to obtain the Bid Protections, the Licensee commences a suit with respect to the same, which results in a judgment in favor of Licensee, Licensor shall pay to Licensee, in addition to the Bid Protections, an amount in cash equal to the reasonable and documented costs and expenses (including attorney’s fees) incurred by Licensee in connection with such suit.

# **<u>Licensed Technology</u>** 

The code repositories, documentations, frameworks, tools, and any other artifacts produced for each of the following areas: 

- **Post-training stack:** The codebases, scripts, and documentation used to fine-tune and align models after their initial training phase. 

- **Training recipes:** The documented processes, configuration files, and step-by-step methodologies required to successfully train the models. 

- **Reward model:** The underlying frameworks and training code used to train, evaluate and score model outputs. 

- **Benchmarks:** The evaluation frameworks, scoring code, and technical documentation used to measure model performance and capabilities. 

- **Eval Framework:** The comprehensive architecture, technical design and any other relevant documentations, codebases, and standardized methodologies used to systematically assess model quality, safety, and performance against defined criteria. 

- **Model safety stack:** The complete set of code repositories and system architectures designed to post-train for safety, develop classifiers, filters, monitoring systems, and other relevant processes and artifacts to ensure safe model outputs. 

- **Closed source vLLM to serve with classifier heads with optimization:** The proprietary serving infrastructure, including optimization code and frameworks used to run classifier heads efficiently. 

- **Fallback classifier stack:** The secondary classification code and frameworks utilized when primary safety or routing models fail or encounter errors. 

- **RL adapters for safety:** The reinforcement learning code repositories and training scripts used to adapt base models for safety parameters. 

- **AI infrastructure stack:** The foundational codebase, automation scripts, and architectural documentation used to manage and optimize the AI hardware and software environments. 

- **Training infrastructure:** The specific codebase, frameworks, orchestration tools used to manage and execute large-scale model training runs, and any documentations and other artifacts produced. 

- **Inference stack (GPU/cluster management, model routing, deployment):** The code repositories and operational systems for managing GPU clusters, routing inference requests, and deploying model updates. 

- **SRE workflows:** The Site Reliability Engineering documentation, automated scripts, and procedural workflows used to maintain system uptime, scaling, and reliability. 

- **Lorebook and prompt management stack:** The software frameworks and code used to manage character contexts, store lore configurations, and handle prompt structuring.

- **Guardian (long horizon safety monitoring):** The code repositories and analytical frameworks for the Guardian system, used to monitor safety and context over extended conversational interactions. All documentations related to the design, implementation, running, and evaluations of the system. 

- **Age assurance (age prediction model):** The underlying architecture, training code, and frameworks for the age prediction systems. 

- **Evaluation and PT data platform:** The data processing infrastructure, code pipelines, and frameworks used to manage and format data for pre-training and evaluation. All design documentations and tools developed. 

- **Multi-modal stack (image/video generation models and post-training stack):** The underlying codebase, architectural frameworks, and documentations for processing and generating images and video, along with their post-training methodologies. The training pipelines, scripts, and evaluation processes. 

- **Chat stack (scale and model routing) and chat styles framework:** The core conversational infrastructure backend code responsible for handling massive user scale and routing messages to the appropriate model endpoints. 

- **Safety Prevalence (measurement and metrics stack):** The analytical codebases, measurement tools, all documentations, and logging frameworks used to track and report on safety metrics across the platform. 

- **Prompt optimization framework:** The specific code, algorithms, and documented methodologies used to automatically refine and optimize system and user prompts. 

- **Trust and Safety Moderation stack:** The comprehensive suite of code repositories, automated moderation frameworks, and documentation used to enforce community guidelines. 

- **AI Red teaming and adversarial testing frameworks:** The tools, codebases, scripts, and documented methodologies used to simulate attacks, test model vulnerabilities, and defend against adversarial prompts or jailbreaks. 

- **Security infrastructure and processes:** The proprietary tools, architectures, and documented workflows developed to ensure overall system security, including AppSec frameworks and analysis tools for AI products, as well as custom configurations and access control frameworks for endpoints, APIs, and administrative interfaces. 

- **Data science and agentic pipelines:** The code repositories for data processing code sources, data agents, agentic pipelines utilized for data science workflows, and any associated frameworks, tools, and documented processes supporting data science operations. 

- **Additional Technical Materials:** Any other training infrastructure, training pipelines, know-how, evaluation frameworks, safety tools, documentation, design-specifications and technical materials reasonably necessary to utilize the foregoing and develop future models and any embodiment of such intellectual property.
