# **FOUND, CONCLUDED, AND DETERMINED THAT:** 

# **<u>Jurisdiction, Venue, and Final Order</u>** 

A. This Court has jurisdiction to consider the Motion pursuant to 28 U.S.C. §§ 157(a)– (b)(1) and 1334(b) and the _Amended Standing Order of Reference_ from the United States District Court for the District of Delaware, dated February 29, 2012. This is a core proceeding under 28 U.S.C. § 157(b)(2)(A). Venue of this case and this Motion in this district is proper under 28 U.S.C. §§ 1408 and 1409. 

B. This 363 Free and Clear Order constitutes a final and appealable order within the meaning of 28 U.S.C. § 158(a). This Court may enter a final order consistent with Article III of the United States Constitution. Notwithstanding Bankruptcy Rules 6004(h) and 6006(d), and to any extent necessary under Bankruptcy Rule 9014 and Federal Rule of Civil Procedure 54(b), as made applicable by Bankruptcy Rule 7054, the Court expressly finds that there is no just reason for delay in the implementation of this 363 Free and Clear Order, and expressly directs entry of judgment as set forth herein. 

C. The findings and conclusions set forth herein constitute the Court’s findings of fact and conclusions of law pursuant to Bankruptcy Rule 7052 made applicable to this proceeding pursuant to Bankruptcy Rule 9014. To the extent any of the findings of fact set forth herein constitute conclusions of law, they are adopted as such. To the extent any of the conclusions of law set forth herein constitute findings of fact, they are adopted as such.

# **<u>Transferred Property</u>** 

D. The Debtor is the sole and lawful owner of, and has clear and marketable title to, 

(a) that certain intellectual property and related technology that is the basis for that certain perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive, transferable (subject to certain limitations), sublicensable (subject to certain limitations), non-terminable, irrevocable, fully paid-up, royalty-free right and license (the “IP License”). Pursuant to the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Debtor will grant to Buyer the IP License and the Debtor will sell and the Buyer will purchase any claims that the Debtor has against current or former Debtor employees who accept or have accepted offers of employment with the Buyer or any of its affiliates that have not previously been released by the Debtor (collectively, the “Transferred Property”). The Debtor’s right, title, and interest in and to the Transferred Property constitutes property of the Debtor’s estate and title thereto is presently, or will be, vested in the Debtor’s estate within the meaning of section 541(a) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# **<u>Notice and Opportunity to Object</u>** 

E. As evidenced by the affidavits of service and publication previously filed with this Court, proper, timely, adequate, and sufficient notice of the Motion, Sale Hearing, Stalking Horse Agreement, Bidding Procedures, Bidding Procedures Order (including, without limitation, the deadline for submitting Qualified Bids), Transaction (including the selection of the Buyer as the Successful Bidder), the Debtor’s intent to assume the TSA, and the proposed entry of this 363 Free and Clear Order has been provided to each party entitled to such notice in accordance with sections 102(1), 363, and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, Bankruptcy Rules 2002, 6004, 6006, 9007, 9008, and 9014 and Local Bankruptcy Rule 2002-1(b), and in compliance with the Bidding Procedures Order. No other or further notice of the foregoing is required. With respect to parties whose identities or addresses are not reasonably ascertained by the Debtor, publication of the Transaction

Notice in _USA Today_ on [●], 2026, and the posting of the Transaction Notice on the website of the Debtor’s claims and noticing agent, Stretto, Inc., at https://cases.stretto.com/chartech on [●], 2026 and on the Debtor’s blog and online community webpages (which are the online locations through which the Debtor regularly communicates with the public), was, and is deemed, sufficient and reasonably calculated under the circumstances to reach such parties. 

F. A reasonable opportunity to object and to be heard regarding the relief requested in the Motion and granted by this 363 Free and Clear Order, including, but not limited to, the consummation of the Transaction, the Debtor’s entry into, and performance under, the Stalking Horse Agreement, and the assumption of the TSA, was afforded to all interested persons and entities. 

G. [In accordance with the Bidding Procedures Order, the Debtor has served a notice of its intent to assume [and assign] certain Assigned Contracts and of the Cure Amounts upon each Counterparty to such Assigned Contract at Docket No. [●] (such notice an “Assumption and <u>Assignment Notice”). The service and provision of such notice[s] was good, sufficient, and</u> appropriate under the circumstances; Counterparties to the Assigned Contracts have had or will have an adequate opportunity to object to: (i) the assumption and assignment of the Counterparties’ Assigned Contracts, (ii) the Cure Amounts for the Assigned Contracts set forth in the Assumption and Assignment Notice(s), and (iii) the provision of adequate assurance of future performance on or before the Contract Objection Deadline. The initial Contract Objection Deadline occurred on November [18], 2026. All Contract Objections filed prior to the Contract Objection Deadline were either resolved prior to the commencement of the Sale Hearing, were adjudicated at the Sale Hearing, or will be adjudicated at such other date and time as may be determined by the Debtor in consultation with the Buyer. With respect to any Contract Objections related solely to a Cure

Dispute that were not resolved at or prior to the Sale Hearing, the Assigned Contract may be assumed by the Debtor and assigned to the Buyer provided that the cure amount that the Counterparty asserts is required to be paid under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code will be deposited in a segregated account by the Debtor or the Buyer for such Assigned Contract pending the Court’s adjudication of the Cure Dispute or the parties’ consensual resolution of the Cure Dispute. Additional Assumption and Assignment Notices may be served in accordance with the Bidding Procedures Order following entry of this Order and Counterparties to such additional Assigned Contracts shall be required to file any Contract Objections not later than fourteen (14) days thereafter. If no Contract Objection is timely received, the Debtor shall be authorized to assume and assign such Assigned Contracts to the Buyer without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest and without the need for further order of this Court, and such assumption and assignment shall be subject to the terms of this Order. 

H. Contract Objections must (i) be in writing, (ii) comply with the applicable provisions of the Bankruptcy Rules and the Local Rules, (iii) be filed with the Clerk of the Court on or before the applicable Contract Objection Deadline; (iv) be served upon the Objection Notice Parties identified in the Bidding Procedures Order; and (v) state with specificity the grounds for such objection, including, without limitation, the fully liquidated cure amount and the legal and factual bases for any unliquidated cure amount that the Counterparty believes is required to be paid under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code for the Assigned Contract, along with the specific nature and dates of any alleged defaults, the pecuniary losses, if any, resulting therefrom, and the conditions giving rise thereto. 

# **<u>Business Judgment</u>** 

I. The Debtor has demonstrated good, sufficient, and sound business purposes to promptly consummate the Transaction, including assumption of the TSA, and that doing so is an

appropriate exercise of the Debtor’s business judgment and is in the best interests of the Debtor, its estate, its creditors, and other parties in interest. Such business reasons for promptly consummating the Transaction include, but are not limited to, the fact that: (i) the transfer of the Transferred Property pursuant to the Transaction documents presents the best opportunity and represents the best method to maximize the value of the Debtor’s estate for the benefit of the Debtor, its estate, its creditors, and other parties in interest; (ii) the purchase price and other terms set forth in the Stalking Horse Agreement and other Transaction documents constitute the highest or otherwise best offer received for the Transferred Property; (iii) certain special committees of the Debtor’s board of directors (the “Board”), the members of which are each disinterested with respect to the Transaction (each, a “Special Committee”), led the strategic direction of the marketing process and all negotiations with the Buyer, and have recommended the Board approve the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA (including assumption thereof, and in connection with such, the assumption of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement), and (iv) unless the Transaction is consummated as provided for in this 363 Free and Clear Order, the Debtor and its estate may receive significantly less value for all stakeholders.  The business reasons for assumption of the TSA include but are not limited to:  (i) the Debtor’s ongoing need for critical transition support services from the Buyer for a period of time following the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain of the Debtor’s former employees; (ii) the need to recognize and give effect to the terms of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, which are material terms critical to the Transaction, including the Transaction’s payment structure, the hiring of certain employees, the granting of releases, and certain confidentiality concerns; and (iii) and the need to provide for consummation of the Transaction in full.

J. The Debtor had good, sufficient, and sound business purposes for entering into the 

TSA and the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the Debtor’s entry into the TSA and Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement were integral components of the Transaction. In particular, the Debtor demonstrated that to maximize the value of the Transferred Property through a marketing and sale process, it needed to minimize employee attrition risk and that the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ prepetition employment of the Debtor’s former employees and the provision of transition services for a limited period pursuant to the TSA were all done in furtherance of this purpose and as part of the Transaction, at the request and with the consent of the Debtor, acting through a Special Committee. The Debtor demonstrated that it entered into the TSA because it needed critical transition services for a limited period from the Buyer following the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain of the Debtor’s former employees. Further, the Debtor demonstrated that its agreement to enter into the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement (including the releases contained therein) was necessary to induce the Buyer and its affiliates to hire the Debtor’s former employees, provide the transition services contemplated by the TSA during and after the marketing and sale process for a limited period, and agree to the terms of the Stalking Horse Agreement or the terms of an alternative form of transition services agreement if the Buyer was not chosen as the [Winning Bidder]. 

# **<u>Marketing Process and Highest or Otherwise Best Offer</u>** 

K. The Debtor, consistent with its fiduciary duties, conducted a robust and extensive marketing process overseen by the Special Committees both before and after the commencement of this Chapter 11 Case, which was substantively and procedurally fair to all parties and all Potential Bidders, and afforded a full, fair, and reasonable opportunity for any interested party to make a higher or otherwise better offer to purchase the Transferred Property.

L. The Bidding Procedures were appropriately tailored to maximize the value obtained through any sale of the Debtor’s property for the benefit of the Debtor’s estate. The Debtor, overseen by a Special Committee, and the Debtor’s advisors, adequately marketed the Transferred Property and conducted the marketing and sale process in accordance with the Bidding Procedures and the Bidding Procedures Order, which marketing and sale process was made possible by the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain of the Debtor’s former employees and provision of transition services through the TSA for a limited period. Ultimately, the Debtor, overseen by a Special Committee and in consultation with its advisors, determined that the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, constituted the highest and best offer for the sale of the Transferred Property, and selected the Buyer as the Winning Bidder. The Debtor determined, at the recommendation of a Special Committee and in a valid and sound exercise of its business judgment, that consummation of the Transaction will provide a greater recovery for the Debtor’s estate than would be provided by any other available alternative. 

M. The Transaction process was conducted in a proper manner and was overseen by a Special Committee, wherein: (i) the Transaction process was non-collusive, duly noticed, free of unfair dealing, and provided a full, fair, reasonable, and adequate opportunity for any person or entity to make an offer, including a higher or otherwise better offer than the Buyer, to purchase the [Debtor’s property, including, without limitation, the] Transferred Property; (ii) the Debtor provided Potential Bidders with sufficient information to enable them to make an informed judgment on whether to bid on the Debtor’s property and considered any and all Qualified Bids (if any); (iii) the Debtor and the Buyer have negotiated and undertaken their roles leading to the entry into the Stalking Horse Agreement, TSA and Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement in a diligent, non-collusive, fair, reasonable, and good-faith manner; (iv) the competitive, fair, and open sale

process conducted by the Debtor, and its advisors, both prior to the commencement of this Chapter 11 Case and postpetition pursuant to the Bidding Procedures and Bidding Procedures Order resulted in the highest or otherwise best value for the Debtor’s property, was in the best interest of the Debtor, its creditors, and all parties in interest, and any other transaction would not have yielded as favorable a result. 

N. The transfer of the Transferred Property and the consideration provided therefore by the Buyer pursuant to the Stalking Horse Agreement and the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement are fair and reasonable, the highest or otherwise best offer, shall be deemed for all purposes to constitute a transfer for reasonably equivalent value and fair consideration under the Bankruptcy Code and any other applicable law, and were the result of a fair and non-collusive bidding process. Such bidding process was conducted in compliance with the Bidding Procedures and Bidding Procedures Order and was free from unfair dealing. 

# **<u>Single, Integrated Transaction</u>** 

O. Entry of this 363 Free and Clear Order authorizing and approving the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and assumption of the TSA, is a necessary condition precedent to the Buyer consummating the Transaction. The terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order, the Stalking Horse Agreement, the TSA, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain of the Debtor’s former employees are inextricably linked and technically and collectively constitute a single, integrated transaction. 

# **<u>No</u>** **_<u>Sub Rosa</u>_** **<u>Plan</u>** 

P. The consummation of the Transaction outside a plan of reorganization pursuant to Stalking Horse Agreement and the transactions contemplated thereby, [including the assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts,] neither impermissibly restructures the rights of the Debtor’s creditors nor impermissibly dictates the terms of a plan of reorganization or liquidation

for the Debtor. The Transaction does not constitute a _sub rosa_ chapter 11 plan for which approval has been sought without the protections that a disclosure statement would afford. 

# **<u>Disclosure of Connections</u>** 

Q. The Buyer disclosed to the Debtor in certain of the bid materials all information required by the Bidding Procedures concerning the identity of the Buyer and its employees, agents, and affiliates. The Buyer’s disclosures comply with the disclosure requirements of the Bidding Procedures and are sufficient to provide the Debtor and the Debtor’s advisors complete and accurate information concerning all material connections between the Buyer and the Debtor, its affiliates and their respective current or former directors and officers. The Buyer’s disclosures establish that because the Buyer or its affiliates solicited, made offers of employment to, and hired certain Debtor employees with the consent of the Debtor (acting through a Special Committee), certain employees of the Buyer were “insiders” of the Debtor within the meaning of section 101(31) of the Bankruptcy Code, insofar as certain former employees of the Debtor, including the Debtor’s former Chief Executive Officer, are presently employed by the Buyer. Although certain members of the Buyer were insiders of the Debtor, (i) no common identity of directors or controlling equity interest holders existed or exists between Buyer, on the one hand, and the Debtor, on the other hand, (ii) a Special Committee led and recommended that the Board approve the marketing process and review and approve the Transaction, and (iii) the Buyer did not and does not control the Debtor in any respect. 

# **<u>Good Faith of Buyer</u>** 

R. The Buyer has not acted in a collusive manner, and the consideration to be paid by the Buyer under the Stalking Horse Agreement, as well as that which it has already paid pursuant to the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, was not controlled by any agreement among bidders and was negotiated at arm’s length, in good faith, and without collusion pursuant to section

363(m) of the Bankruptcy Code and is fair, adequate, and reasonable under the circumstances. Specifically: (i) at all times prior to entry into the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and from and after the Petition Date, the Buyer recognized that the Debtor was free to deal with any other party interested in purchasing the Transferred Property and at all times in general the Buyer recognized that the Debtor was free to deal with any other party interested in buying any of the Debtor’s other assets; (ii) the Buyer complied in all respects with the provisions of the Bidding Procedures, the Bidding Procedures Order, the Bankruptcy Rules, and the Bankruptcy Code; (iii) the Buyer agreed to subject its bid to the competitive sale process set forth in the Bidding Procedures and Bidding Procedures Order; (iv) all payments made by the Buyer in connection with the Transaction have been disclosed; (v) no common identity of directors or controlling stockholders exists among the Buyer and the Debtor, and the sale process was overseen by a Special Committee comprised of independent directors which, after thorough consideration of the Transaction and other Qualified Bids received (if any) recommended approval of the Transaction to the Board; (vi) the negotiation and execution of the Transaction was at arm’s length and in good faith, and at all times each of the Buyer and the Debtor were represented by competent counsel of their choosing; (vii) the Buyer has not acted in a collusive manner with any person, and there was no fraud or unfair dealing in connection with the bidding process and the sale of the Transferred Property. 

S. The Debtor, the Buyer, and each of their respective affiliates, management, boards of directors, officers, directors, members, partners, principals, employees, agents, advisors, and representatives, have acted in good faith and without any collusion or fraud of any kind. The Buyer is consummating the Transaction in good faith and for reasonably equivalent value and is a “good faith purchaser” within the meaning of section 363(m) of the Bankruptcy Code. The Buyer is

therefore entitled to all of the protections afforded under section 363(m), has proceeded in good faith in all respects in connection with the Transaction, and would not have entered or agreed to consummate the Transaction if it were not granted all of the protections afforded under section 363(m) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# **<u>No Fraudulent Transfer</u>** 

T. The Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA, were not entered into, and are not being consummated, for the purpose of hindering, delaying, or defrauding the Debtor or its creditors under applicable law, and none of the parties to such documents are consummating the Transaction with any fraudulent or otherwise improper purpose. The consideration provided by the Buyer in connection with the Transaction, including pursuant to the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA, constitutes reasonably equivalent value and fair consideration under the Bankruptcy Code, the Uniform Fraudulent Conveyance Act, the Uniform Fraudulent Transfer Act, and under the laws of the United States, any state, territory, possession, or the District of Columbia. None of the Debtor, the Buyer, nor any of their respective affiliates, officers, directors, members, partners, principals, or shareholders (or equivalent) or any of their respective representatives, attorneys, successors, or assigns have engaged in any conduct that would cause or permit the consummation of the Transaction (including the various terms of the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, or the TSA) to be avoided, or costs or damages to be imposed, under section 363(n) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# **<u>Buyer Not Successor</u>** 

U. Neither the Buyer nor its past, present, and future subsidiaries, parents, divisions, 

affiliates, agents, representatives, insurers, attorneys, successors, and assigns, nor any of its nor their respective directors, managers, officers, employees, shareholders, members, agents,

representatives, attorneys, contractors, subcontractors, independent contractors, owners, insurance companies, or partners (collectively, the “Buyer Parties”) is a continuation of the Debtor or its estate, no Buyer Party is holding itself out to the public as a continuation of the Debtor or its estate, and the Transaction, including, without limitation, the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of the Debtor’s former employees, the Buyer’s provision of transition services pursuant to the TSA for a limited period, and the Debtor’s and Buyer’s performance under the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, does not amount to a consolidation, merger, or _de facto_ merger of the Buyer (or any other Buyer Party) and the Debtor. Further, no employees of the Debtor will be deemed to transfer to any Buyer Party by operation of law in connection with the Transaction. 

V. The authorization and approval of the Transaction, including the (i) approval of the Stalking Horse Agreement, (ii) approval of the terms and all actions taken under the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, (iii) assumption of the TSA, [and (iv) assignment to the Buyer of the Assigned Contracts (which shall be subject to further order of this Court except as set forth herein)] will not subject the Buyer or any of its affiliates to any liability whatsoever that arises prior to the Closing or by reason of transfer of the Transferred Property under the laws of the United States, any state, territory, or possession thereof, or the District of Columbia, based, in whole or in part, directly or indirectly, on any theory of law or equity, including, without limitation, any theory of equitable law, any theory of antitrust, successor, transferee, derivative, or vicarious liability or any similar theory, and/or applicable state or federal law or otherwise. [For the avoidance of doubt, and notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this 363 Free and Clear Order, any contract that is not a Closing Date Assigned Contract (as defined herein) shall not be an Assigned Contract or part of the Transferred Property under this 363 Free and Clear Order or the Transaction Documents until further order of this Court.]

# **<u>Validity of Transfer</u>** 

W. The Debtor, acting through a Special Committee, authorized the execution and delivery of the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA, and consummation of the Transaction. The Debtor and its affiliates (i) have full corporate power and authority to execute and deliver all documents contemplated by the Transaction, as applicable, (ii) have all of the power and authority necessary to consummate the Transaction, and (iii) have taken all action necessary to authorize and approve the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA, and to consummate the Transaction, and no further consents or approvals are required for the Debtor to consummate the Transaction. 

# **<u>Section 363(f) Is Satisfied</u>** 

X. The transfer of the Transferred Property to the Buyer [and the assumption and assignment to the Buyer of the Assigned Contracts] under the terms of the Stalking Horse Agreement and the TSA meet[s] the applicable provisions of section 363(f) of the Bankruptcy Code such that the transfer of the Transferred Property will be a legal, valid, and effective transfer of all of the legal, equitable, and beneficial rights, title, and interests in the Transferred Property free and clear of any and all liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances and will not subject any Buyer Party to any liability for any liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances whatsoever (including, without limitation, under any theory of equitable law, antitrust, or successor or transferee liability), except as expressly provided in the Stalking Horse Agreement and this 363 Free and Clear Order. All holders of liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances who did not object, or withdrew their objections, to the Transaction are deemed to have consented to the Transaction pursuant to section 363(f)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code, and all holders of liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances are adequately protected—thus satisfying section 363(e) of the Bankruptcy Code—by having their liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances, if any, attach to the proceeds of

the Transaction (in the same order of priority and with the same validity, force, and effect that such holder had prior to the Transaction, subject to any rights, claims, and defenses of the Debtor or its estate, as applicable). Those holders of claims who did object and that have an interest in the Transferred Property fall within one or more of the other subsections of section 363(f) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

Y. The Buyer has expressly relied upon the finality of this 363 Free and Clear Order and the protections afforded by section 363(f) of the Bankruptcy Code in agreeing to consummate the Transaction and pay the purchase price in connection with the Transaction. The Buyer would not have entered into the Stalking Horse Agreement, the TSA, or the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and would not agree to consummate the Transaction [, including the assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts,] (i) if the transfer of the Transferred Property were not free and clear of all interests of any kind or nature whatsoever or (ii) if the Buyer or any of its affiliates would, or in the future could, be liable for any interests, in each case, including, without limitation, rights or claims based on any successor, transferee, derivative, or vicarious liability or any similar theory and/or applicable state or federal law or otherwise. Not transferring the Transferred Property free and clear of all interests of any kind or nature whatsoever would adversely impact the Debtor’s efforts to maximize the value of its estate. 

# <u>[</u> **<u>Assumption and Assignment of the Assigned Contracts</u>** 

Z. Solely with respect to any Assigned Contract listed on an Assumption and Assignment Notice for which the Contract Objection Deadline has occurred, and for which no Contract Objection has been filed and with respect to which Buyer has requested that such Contract be treated as a [“Transferred Contract”] under the Stalking Horse Agreement (each such contract, a “Closing Date Assigned Contract”), the Cure Amounts listed on such Assumption and Assignment Notices are deemed to be the sole amounts necessary to be paid upon assumption of

such Closing Date Assigned Contracts under Bankruptcy Code sections 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) and 365(f)(2)(A). All Cure Amounts, if any, with respect to the Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall be satisfied by the Buyer in accordance with the Stalking Horse Agreement. 

AA. The assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts pursuant to the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order are integral to the Transaction, are in the best interests of the Debtor and its estate, creditors, and other parties in interest, and represent the reasonable exercise of sound and prudent business judgment by the Debtor. 

BB. The Debtor has met all requirements of section 365(b) of the Bankruptcy Code for each of the Assigned Contracts. The Buyer and/or the Debtor has provided compensation or adequate assurance of compensation to any counterparty for actual pecuniary loss to such party resulting from a default prior to the Closing under any of the Assigned Contracts and provided adequate assurance of future performance, within the meaning of sections 365(b)(1) of the Bankruptcy Code. The Buyer has provided adequate assurance of future performance within the meaning of sections 365(b)(1)(C) and 365(f)(2)(B) and in accordance with the Bidding Procedures to the extent that any such assurance is required and not waived by the counterparties to such Assigned Contracts.] 

# **<u>Prompt Consummation</u>** 

CC. For the reasons stated in the Motion, Transaction Declarations, and/or on the record at the Sale Hearing, consummation of the Transaction, including the Debtor’s entry into the Stalking Horse Agreement and assumption of the TSA, must be approved promptly to maximize the value of the Debtor’s estate and its property. Time, therefore, is of the essence in consummating the Transaction, including effectuating the Stalking Horse Agreement and assuming the TSA. Accordingly, the Debtor and the Buyer intend to enter into the Stalking Horse Agreement and close the Transaction as soon as reasonably practicable. The Debtor has demonstrated compelling

circumstances and a good, sufficient, and sound business purpose and justification for immediate approval, effectuation, and consummation of the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and assumption of the TSA.  There is no legal or equitable reason to delay approval, effectuation, and consummation of the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and assumption of the TSA.  Accordingly, there is sufficient cause to waive the stay provided in the Bankruptcy Rules 6004(h) and 6006(d). 

# **NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS ORDERED, ADJUDGED, AND DECREED THAT:** 

# **<u>General Provisions</u>** 

1. The Motion is GRANTED as set forth herein. 

2. All objections to or reservation of rights with respect to the Motion or the relief 

requested therein that have not been withdrawn or resolved are overruled[, except for any unresolved Contract Objections to or reservation of rights with respect to the proposed assumption and assignment of any Assigned Contracts, which may be consensually resolved among the parties or adjudicated at a subsequent hearing in accordance with the Bidding Procedures Order and the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order. All persons and entities who did not object or withdraw their objections to the Motion are deemed to have consented pursuant to section 363(f)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code, except as otherwise set forth herein. All objections to the entry of this 363 Free and Clear Order or to the relief granted herein that were not timely filed or raised at the Sale Hearing (if applicable) are forever barred. 

# **<u>Approval of the Transaction, the Stalking Horse Agreement, and Related Documents</u>** 

3. The Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, assumption of the TSA, 

and all other related documents or actions (if any) that are necessary or appropriate to consummate the Transaction, are hereby authorized and approved in all respects. The failure specifically to include any particular term or component of the Transaction in this 363 Free and Clear Order shall

not diminish or impair its effectiveness, it being the intent of the Court that the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and the assumption of the TSA, each be authorized and approved in full. 

4. Pursuant to sections 363 and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, entry by the Debtor into the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and any other Transaction document, is hereby authorized and approved as a valid exercise of the Debtor’s business judgment. Pursuant to sections 105, 363, and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, the Debtor and its officers, employees, and agents are authorized, empowered, and directed to (a) take any and all actions, including entry into any additional documents, necessary or appropriate to consummate the Transaction, including the transfer of the Transferred Property to the Buyer pursuant to and in accordance with the terms and conditions of the Stalking Horse Agreement and this 363 Free and Clear Order, (b) execute, deliver, and perform, as applicable, under the Stalking Horse Agreement and the TSA, and [(c) assume and assign any and all Assigned Contracts (subject to parties’ rights as set forth in the Bidding Procedures Order, this 363 Free and Clear Order, and the applicable Assumption and Assignment Notice).] [For the avoidance of doubt, and notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this 363 Free and Clear Order, any contract that is not a Closing Date Assigned Contract shall not be an Assigned Contract or part of the Transferred Property under this 363 Free and Clear Order or the Stalking Horse Agreement until further order of this Court.] 

5. The Debtor is authorized to cause to be filed with the secretary of state of any state or other applicable officials of any applicable Governmental Units (as defined in section 101(27) of the Bankruptcy Code), any and all certificates, agreements, or amendments necessary or appropriate to effectuate the Transaction and this 363 Free and Clear Order, including amended and restated certificates, articles of incorporation, and bylaws, and all such other actions, filings,

or recordings as may be required under appropriate provisions of the applicable laws of all applicable Governmental Units or as any of the officers of the Debtor may determine are necessary or appropriate. The execution of any such document or the taking of any such action shall be, and hereby is, deemed conclusive evidence of the authority of such person to so act. 

# **<u>Consummation of the Transaction</u>** 

6. Pursuant to Sections 105(a), 363(b), 363(f), 365(a), [365(b), 365(f),] and 365(n) of the Bankruptcy Code, the Debtor is authorized and directed to transfer the Transferred Property to the Buyer upon the Closing in accordance with the Transaction-related documents and this 363 Free and Clear Order, and the Buyer is acquiring only the Transferred Property, except as otherwise stated in this 363 Free and Clear Order or the executed documents related to the Transaction, as applicable. 

7. All persons and entities are prohibited and enjoined from taking any action to adversely affect or interfere with, or which would be inconsistent with, the Transaction, including the ability of (a) the Debtor to transfer the Transferred Property to the Buyer in accordance with the Transaction documents and this 363 Free and Clear Order, (b) the Buyer to provide to the Debtor the services contemplated under the TSA and for the Buyer to receive the benefits under the TSA, and (c) the Buyer to facilitate the completion of the Debtor’s Chapter 11 Case and to facilitate any potential wind down of the Debtor, as applicable, through the services available under the TSA. 

8. At Closing, all of the Debtor’s right, title, and interest in and to, and possession of, the Transferred Property shall be immediately vested in the Buyer pursuant to sections 105(a), 363(b), 363(f), and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code. Such transfer shall constitute a legal, valid, enforceable, and effective transfer of the Transferred Property. All persons or entities, presently, or at or after the Closing, in possession of some or all of the Transferred Property, are directed to

surrender possession of any and all portions of the Transferred Property to the Buyer on the Closing or at such time thereafter as the Buyer may request. 

9. Except as otherwise set forth herein, this 363 Free and Clear Order (a) shall be effective as a determination that, as of the Closing, (i) the Transferred Property shall have been transferred to the Buyer free and clear of all liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances, (ii) holders of claims who did not object (or who ultimately withdrew their objections, if any) to the Transaction are deemed to have consented to the Transaction being free and clear of their claims pursuant to section 363(f)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code and holders of claims who did object could be compelled in a legal or equitable proceeding to accept money satisfaction of such claims pursuant to section 363(f)(5) of the Bankruptcy Code or fall within one or more of the other subsections of section 363(f) of the Bankruptcy Code and are therefore adequately protected by having their claims that constitute interests in the Transferred Property, if any, attach to the proceeds of the Transaction with the same priority that existed immediately prior to the Closing, and (iii) the conveyances described herein have been effected, and (b) is and shall be binding upon and govern the acts of all entities, including, without limitation, all filing agents, filing officers, title agents, title companies, recorders of mortgages, recorders of deeds, registrars of deeds, registrars of patents, trademarks, or other intellectual property, administrative agencies, governmental departments, secretaries of state, federal and local officials, and all other persons and entities who may be required by operation of law, the duties of their office, or contract, to accept, file, register, or otherwise record or release any documents or instruments, or who may be required to report or insure any title or state of title in or to any lease; and each of the foregoing persons and entities is hereby directed to accept for filing any and all of the documents and instruments necessary and appropriate to consummate the Transaction, including the

implementation of the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, and the TSA. The Transferred Property is sold free and clear of any reclamation rights. All liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances on the Transferred Property shall attach to the proceeds of the Transaction (in the same order of priority and with the same validity, force, and effect that such liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances applied prior to the Transaction, subject to any rights, claims, and defenses of the Debtor or its estate, as applicable, or as otherwise provided herein). Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this 363 Free and Clear Order, no liens shall attach to any Transferred Property upon or after Closing that is contemplated to be transferred to the Buyer in the Transaction, whether or not such Transferred Property is transferred at Closing or thereafter, or remain in the possession or control of the estate following Closing. 

10. Except as otherwise provided in the executed documents related to the Transaction, as applicable, and this 363 Free and Clear Order, all persons and entities (and their respective successors and assigns), including, but not limited to, all debt security holders, equity security holders, affiliates, governmental, tax, and regulatory authorities, lenders, customers, vendors, employees, trade creditors, litigation claimants, and other creditors holding claims arising under or out of, in connection with, or in any way relating to, the Debtor, the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA (and, for avoidance of doubt, the Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain former Debtor employees and the corresponding provision of transition services for a limited period), the Transferred Property, and the ownership, sale, or operation of the Transferred Property prior to Closing or the transfer of the Transferred Property to the Buyer, are hereby forever barred, estopped, and permanently enjoined from asserting such claims against any Buyer Party and its property (including, without limitation, the Transferred Property).  Following the Closing, no holder of any

claim or interest shall interfere with the Buyer’s title to or use and enjoyment of the Transferred Property or the TSA based on or related to any such claim or interest, or based on any action the Debtor may take in its Chapter 11 Case. 

11. If any person or entity that has filed financing statements, mortgages, mechanic’s claims, lis pendens, or other documents or agreements evidencing claims against the Debtor or in the Transferred Property, or in any way related to the Transaction, shall not have delivered to the Debtor prior to the Closing of the Transaction, in proper form for filing and executed by the appropriate parties, termination statements, instruments of satisfaction, and/or releases of all liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances that the person or entity has with respect to the Debtor, the Transaction, or the Transferred Property or otherwise, then only with regard to the Transferred Property that is purchased by the Buyer pursuant to the Transaction, upon or after Closing, (a) the Debtor is hereby authorized and directed to execute and file such statements, instruments, releases, and other documents on behalf of the person or entity with respect to the Transferred Property; (b) the Buyer is hereby authorized to file, register, or otherwise record a certified copy of this 363 Free and Clear Order, which, once filed, registered, or otherwise recorded, shall constitute conclusive evidence of the release, discharge, and termination of all liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances against the Buyer Parties and the Transferred Property, and (c) the Buyer may seek in this Court or any other court to compel appropriate parties to execute termination statements, instruments of satisfaction, and releases of all liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances that are extinguished or otherwise released pursuant to this 363 Free and Clear Order under section 363 of the Bankruptcy Code, and any other provisions of the Bankruptcy Code, with respect to the Transferred Property. This 363 Free and Clear Order is deemed to be in recordable form sufficient to be placed in the filing or recording system of each and every federal, state, or local government

agency, department, or office. Notwithstanding the foregoing, the provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order authorizing and approving consummation of the Transaction, the Debtor’s entry into the Stalking Horse Agreement, assumption of the TSA, and transfer of the Transferred Property free and clear of liens, claims, interests, and encumbrances, shall be self-executing and neither the Debtor nor the Buyer shall be required to execute or file releases, termination statements, assignments, consents, or other instruments to effectuate, consummate, and implement the provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order. 

# **<u>No Successor or Transferee Liability</u>** 

12. To the fullest extent permitted by law, no Buyer Party shall be deemed, as a result 

of any action taken in connection with the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, TSA, and Buyer’s or its affiliates’ employment of certain of the Debtor’s former employees, or the transfer, operation, or use of the Transferred Property to (a) be a legal successor, or otherwise be deemed a successor to the Debtor, (b) have, _de facto_ or otherwise, merged with or into the Debtor, (c) be a joint employer, co-employer or single integrated enterprise with the Debtor, or (d) be an alter ego or a mere continuation or substantial continuation of the Debtor or the enterprise of the Debtor including, without limitation, within the meaning of any foreign, federal, state, or local revenue law, pension law, the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974 (“ERISA”), tax law, labor law, products liability law, employment law, environmental law, or other law, rule, or regulation (including, without limitation, filing requirements under any such laws, rules or regulations), or under any products liability law or doctrine with respect to the Debtor’s liability under such law, rule, or regulation. Further, no employee shall be deemed to transfer to any Buyer Party by operation of law in connection with the Transaction. The Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and the Exclusivity and

Prepayment Agreement, are not an acquisition of the equity of the Debtor or an acquisition of all or substantially all of the assets of the Debtor. 

13. Immediately prior to the Closing, the Buyer was not an “insider” or “affiliate” of the Debtor, as those terms are defined in the Bankruptcy Code, and no common identity of incorporators, directors, or controlling stockholders existed between the Buyer and the Debtor. 

14. No Buyer Party shall have any responsibility for (a) any liability or other obligation of the Debtor or related to the Transaction, including the Transferred Property (and, for the avoidance of doubt, the underling intellectual property and related IP License), or (b) any claims against the Debtor or any of its predecessors or affiliates. No Buyer Party shall have any liability whatsoever with respect to the Debtor (or its predecessors’ or affiliates’) respective businesses or operations or the Debtor’s (or its predecessors’ or affiliates’) obligations (as defined herein, “Successor or Transferee Liability”) based, in whole or part, directly or indirectly, on any theory of successor or vicarious liability of any kind or character, or based upon any theory of antitrust, environmental, successor, or transferee liability, _de facto_ merger or substantial continuity, labor and employment or products liability, whether known or unknown, as of the Closing, now existing or hereafter arising, asserted or unasserted, fixed or contingent, liquidated or unliquidated, including, without limitation, liabilities on account of (a) any taxes arising, accruing, or payable under, out of, in connection with, or in any way relating to the Transaction, including the Transferred Property and any assumed contracts, prior to the Closing or in respect of pre-Closing periods or (b) any plan, agreement, practice, policy, or program, whether written or unwritten, providing for pension, retirement, health, welfare, compensation, or other employee benefits which is or has been sponsored, maintained, or contributed to by the Debtor or with respect to which the Debtor has any liability, whether or not contingent, including, without limitation, any

“multiemployer plan” (as defined in Section 3(37) of ERISA) or “pension plan” (as defined in Section 3(2) of ERISA) to which the Debtor has at any time contributed, or had any obligation to contribute. No Buyer Party shall have any liability or obligation under any applicable law, including, without limitation, (i) the Worker Adjustment and Retraining Notification Act, 29 U.S.C. §§ 2101 _et seq._ , (ii) the Comprehensive Environmental Response Compensation and Liability Act, (iii) the Age Discrimination and Employment Act of 1967 (as amended), (iv) the Federal Rehabilitation Act of 1973 (as amended), (v) the National Labor Relations Act, 29 U.S.C. §§ 151 _et seq._ , or (vi) any foreign, federal, state, or local labor, employment, or environmental law, by virtue of the Buyer’s purchase of the Transferred Property or the Buyer’s or any of its affiliates’ hiring of any employees of the Debtor. Without limiting the foregoing, no Buyer Party shall have any liability or obligation with respect to any environmental liabilities of the Debtor or any environmental liabilities associated with the Transferred Property. 

15. Effective upon the Closing and subject to the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order, all persons and entities are forever prohibited and enjoined from commencing or continuing in any matter any action or other proceeding, whether in law or equity, such as any judicial, administrative, arbitral, or other proceeding, against any Buyer Party or their respective property (including, without limitation, the Transferred Property), with respect to any (a) claim in this Chapter 11 Case or in connection with or related to the Transaction or the Debtor or (b) Successor or Transferee Liability including, without limitation, the following actions with respect to each of clauses (a) and (b): (i) commencing or continuing any action or other proceeding pending or threatened; (ii) enforcing, attaching, collecting, or recovering in any manner any judgment, award, decree, or order; (iii) creating, perfecting, or enforcing any lien, claim, interest, or encumbrance; (iv) asserting any setoff (to the extent not taken prepetition) or right of subrogation or recoupment

of any kind; (v) commencing or continuing any action, in any manner or place, that does not comply with, or is inconsistent with, the provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order or other orders of this Court, or the agreements or actions contemplated or taken in respect hereof; or (vi) to the maximum extent permitted under applicable law (including to the extent provided under Section 525 of the Bankruptcy Code), revoking, terminating, failing, or refusing to renew any license, permit, or authorization to operate any business in connection with the Transferred Property or conduct any of the businesses operated with respect to such property. 

16. To the fullest extent permitted by applicable law, (a) the Buyer and its affiliates and successors and (b) their respective officers, directors, managers, employees, shareholders, agents, contractors, professionals, and assigns (together, the “TSA Exculpated Parties”) shall not have or incur, before, on, or after the Closing, any liability to any person or entity for any claims, suits, demands, penalties, fines, charges, proceedings, causes of action, damages, losses, costs, or expenses of any nature (including attorneys’ fees) arising, directly or indirectly, from (i) the administration, implementation, or performance of the TSA or (ii) the provision, management, or oversight of the transition services under the TSA; _provided_ , that nothing in this paragraph (x) releases the parties to the TSA from their obligations thereunder, or (y) exculpates any TSA Exculpated Party from liability for any act or omission determined by final order of the Court to constitute willful misconduct, bad faith, or gross negligence. 

# **<u>Good Faith of Buyer</u>** 

17. The Transaction is undertaken by the Buyer without collusion and in good faith, as that term is defined in section 363(m) of the Bankruptcy Code, and accordingly, the reversal or modification on appeal of the authorization provided herein to consummate the Transaction shall not affect the validity of the Transaction (including, without limitation, entry into the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, assumption of the TSA, [and the

assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts in accordance with this Order)], unless such authorization and consummation of such Transaction are duly and properly stayed pending such appeal. 

18. Neither the Debtor nor the Buyer have engaged in any action or inaction that would cause or permit the Transaction to be avoided or costs or damages to be imposed under section 363(n) of the Bankruptcy Code.  The consideration provided by the Buyer in connection with the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement and the TSA, including the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, is substantial, fair, and reasonable and the Transaction may not be avoided, and costs and damages may not be imposed, under section 363(n) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# **<u>Assumption of the TSA</u>** 

19. The Debtor is authorized to assume and directed to perform under the TSA, including the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, pursuant to sections 363 and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code. The TSA may be modified, amended, or supplemented by the parties thereto in accordance with the terms thereof without further order of this Court. The Debtor is not in default of any obligations under the TSA such that it owes no cure obligations to the Buyer under the TSA. The assumption of the TSA is made in good faith under sections 363(b) and (m) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

# <u>[</u> **<u>Assumption and Assignment of Assigned Contracts</u>** 

20. The Debtor is authorized and directed at the Closing, and subject to payment of applicable Cure Amounts, to assume and assign each of the Closing Date Assigned Contracts to the Buyer and to have such Closing Date Assigned Contracts assumed by the Buyer, pursuant to sections 105(a) and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, and to execute and deliver to the Buyer such documents or other instruments as may be necessary to assign and transfer the Closing Date

Assigned Contracts to the Buyer. The payment of the applicable Cure Amounts (if any) for the Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall (a) effect a cure of all defaults existing thereunder as of the Closing and (b) compensate for any actual pecuniary loss to such counterparty resulting from such default. Upon the payment of Cure Amounts, if any, by the Buyer for the Closing Date Assigned Contracts, such Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall remain in full force and effect, and no default shall exist under the Closing Date Assigned Contract, nor shall there exist any event or condition which, with the passage of time or giving of notice, or both, would constitute such default. The Cure Amounts with respect to such Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall not be subject to further dispute or audit, including, without limitation, any dispute or audit based on performance prior to the Closing. After payment of the Cure Amounts by the Buyer for the Closing Date Assigned Contracts, neither the Debtor nor the Buyer shall have any further liabilities to the counterparties to the Closing Date Assigned Contracts [other than the Buyer’s obligations under the Closing Date Assigned Contracts that accrue and become due and payable on or after Closing]. 

21. For the avoidance of doubt and notwithstanding anything to the contrary in this 363 Free and Clear Order or the documents related to the Transaction, as applicable, all contract counterparties’ rights with respect to the assumption and assignment of their respective contracts, and any Cure Amount proposed with respect thereto, are reserved through the Contract Objection Deadline or a supplemental Contract Objection Deadline (as applicable), and any such timely objections that are not resolved prior to an Assumption/Assignment/Cure Hearing shall be subject to adjudication at an Assumption/Assignment/Cure Hearing or such later hearing as may be agreed to by the Debtor and the contract counterparty, or as otherwise determined by the Court. Except with respect to the TSA, which shall be assumed pursuant to this 363 Free and Clear Order as of Closing, and notwithstanding anything herein to the contrary, no contract shall be assumed or

assigned to the Buyer on the Closing or otherwise unless Buyer has requested that such contract be treated as a [“Transferred Contract”] under the Stalking Horse Agreement and the Debtor shall file on the docket and serve a notice upon the applicable counterparties identifying the Closing Date Assigned Contracts that the Buyer has requested be treated as a [“Transferred Contract”] as of the Closing. 

22. Any non-Debtor counterparty to an Assigned Contract that does not file with the Court an objection to such assumption and assignment by the Contract Objection Deadline (as applicable) is deemed to have consented to such assumption and assignment. 

23. Pursuant to section 365(f) of the Bankruptcy Code, subject to the payment of the applicable Cure Amounts, the Closing Date Assigned Contracts that the Buyer designates to be assumed and assigned under the Stalking Horse Agreement shall be assigned and transferred to, and remain in full force and effect for the benefit of, the Buyer notwithstanding any provision in the contracts or other restrictions prohibiting their assignment or transfer. Any provisions in any Closing Date Assigned Contract that prohibit or condition the assignment of such Closing Date Assigned Contract to the Buyer or allow the counterparty to such Closing Date Assigned Contract to terminate, recapture, impose any penalty, condition on renewal or extension, or modify any term or condition upon the assignment of such Closing Date Assigned Contract to the Buyer, constitute unenforceable anti-assignment provisions that are void and of no force and effect. All other requirements and conditions under sections 363 and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code for the assumption by the Debtor and assignment to the Buyer of the Assigned Contracts have been satisfied. Upon the Closing, in accordance with sections 363 and 365 of the Bankruptcy Code, the Buyer shall be fully and irrevocably vested with all right, title, and interest of the Debtor under the Closing Date Assigned Contracts, and such Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall remain in full

force and effect for the benefit of the Buyer. Each counterparty to the Closing Date Assigned Contracts shall be forever barred, estopped, and permanently enjoined from (a) asserting against the Debtor or any Buyer Party or their respective property any assignment fee, acceleration, default, breach, or claim or pecuniary loss, or condition to assignment existing, arising, or accruing as of the Closing or arising by reason of the Closing, including, without limitation, any breach related to or arising out of change-in-control provisions in such Assigned Contracts, or any purported written or oral modification to the Closing Date Assigned Contracts and (b) asserting against any Buyer Party (or its respective property, including, without limitation, the Transferred Property) any claim, counterclaim, defense, breach, condition, or setoff asserted, or assertable against, the Debtor existing as of the Closing or arising by reason of the Closing. 

24. Upon the Closing and the payment of the relevant Cure Amounts, if any, the Buyer shall be deemed to be substituted for the Debtor as a party to the applicable Closing Date Assigned Contracts and the Debtor shall be released, pursuant to section 365(k) of the Bankruptcy Code, from any liability under the Closing Date Assigned Contracts. There shall be no accelerations, assignment fees, increases, or any other fees charged to the Buyer or the Debtor as a result of the assumption and assignment of the Closing Date Assigned Contracts. The failure of the Debtor or the Buyer to enforce at any time one or more terms or conditions of any Closing Date Assigned Contract shall not be a waiver of such terms or conditions or of the right of the Debtor or the Buyer, as the case may be, to enforce every term and condition of such Closing Date Assigned Contract. The validity of the assumption and assignment of any Closing Date Assigned Contract to the Buyer shall not be affected by any existing dispute between the Debtor and any counterparty to such Closing Date Assigned Contract. Any party that may have had the right to consent to the assignment of any Closing Date Assigned Contract and did not timely object prior to the passage

of the Contract Objection Deadline will be deemed to have consented for the purposes of section 365(e)(2)(A) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

25. All defaults or other obligations of the Debtor under the Assigned Contracts arising or accruing after the Contract Objection Deadline and prior to the Closing (without giving effect to any acceleration clauses or any default provisions of the kind specified in section 365(b)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code) shall be cured on the Closing, or as soon thereafter as reasonably practicable. 

26. Notwithstanding any term of any Closing Date Assigned Contract to the contrary, any extension or renewal options or other rights contained in such Closing Date Assigned Contract that purport to be personal only to, or exercisable only by, the Debtor, a named entity, or an entity operating under a specific trade name, may, in each case, be freely exercised to their full extent by the Buyer subject to the other applicable terms of the Closing Date Assigned Contracts. Any extension or renewal options in connection with all Closing Date Assigned Contracts that the Debtor validly exercised prior to the entry of this 363 Free and Clear Order, and all terms of the Closing Date Assigned Contracts, are in full force and effect and have not been previously rejected, and the Debtor’s time to assume or reject the Closing Date Assigned Contracts has not otherwise expired. 

27. The Debtor’s obligations to counterparties of any unexpired leases pursuant to section 365(d)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code through the effective date of assumption or assignment or rejection of any such unexpired leases shall not be affected or modified by this 363 Free and Clear Order. 

28. The assignments of each of the Closing Date Assigned Contracts are made in good faith under sections 363(b) and (m) of the Bankruptcy Code.

29. Nothing in this 363 Free and Clear Order, the Motion, or in any notice or any other document is or shall be deemed an admission by the Debtor that any Assigned Contract is an executory contract or unexpired lease under Section 365 of the Bankruptcy Code. 

30. The failure of the Debtor or the Buyer to enforce at any time one or more terms or conditions of any Assigned Contract shall not be a waiver of such terms or conditions or of their respective rights to enforce every term and condition of the Assigned Contracts.] 

# **<u>Other Provisions</u>** 

31. To the maximum extent permitted by applicable law, and in accordance with the Transaction, the Buyer shall be authorized, as of the Closing, to operate under any license, permit, registration, and governmental authorization or approval (collectively, the “Permits”) of the Debtor with respect to the Transferred Property. To the extent the Buyer cannot operate under any Permits in accordance with the previous sentence, such Permits shall be in effect while the Buyer, with assistance from the Debtor, works promptly and diligently to apply for and secure all necessary government approvals for new issuance of Permits to the Buyer. The Debtor shall maintain the Permits in good standing to the fullest extent allowed by applicable law for the Buyer’s benefit until equivalent new Permits are issued to the Buyer. 

32. To the extent provided by section 525 of the Bankruptcy Code, no governmental 

unit may revoke or suspend any Permit relating to the Transferred Property sold, transferred, or conveyed to the Buyer on account of the filing or pendency of this Chapter 11 Case or the consummation of the Transaction. 

33. Upon or after the Closing, the Buyer shall not be required to seek or obtain relief from the automatic stay under section 362 of the Bankruptcy Code to enforce any of its remedies and/or to take any and all actions permitted in connection with Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, the TSA, this 363 Free and Clear Order, or any other Transaction-related

document with respect to the Transaction. The automatic stay imposed by section 362 of the Bankruptcy Code is modified solely to the extent necessary to implement the preceding sentence, _provided_ , _however_ , that this Court shall retain exclusive jurisdiction over any and all disputes with respect thereto. 

34. Neither the Buyer nor the Debtor shall have an obligation to consummate the Transaction until all conditions precedent in any Transaction-related document, as applicable, and each of their respective obligations to consummate the Transaction, have been met, satisfied, or waived in accordance with the Transaction. 

35. The terms and provisions of the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA and this 363 Free and Clear Order shall be binding in all respects upon the Debtor, its affiliates, its estate, all creditors of (whether known or unknown) and holders of equity interests in the Debtor, any statutory committee appointed in this Chapter 11 Case, any holders of claims against or on all or any portion of the Transferred Property, [all counterparties to the Assigned Contracts,] the Buyer, and all of their respective successors and assigns including, but not limited to, any subsequent trustee(s), examiner(s), or receiver(s) appointed in the Debtor’s Chapter 11 Case or upon conversion to Chapter 7 under the Bankruptcy Code, as to which trustee(s), examiner(s), or receiver(s) such terms and provisions likewise shall be binding.  The Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA and any other Transaction-related documents shall not be subject to rejection or avoidance by the Debtor, its estate, its creditors, its shareholders, or any trustee(s), examiner(s), or receiver(s). 

36. The terms and provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order, all other Transactionrelated documents, and any actions taken pursuant hereto shall survive entry of an order which may be entered: (a) confirming any chapter 11 plan in this Chapter 11 Case; (b) converting this

Chapter 11 Case to a case under chapter 7 of the Bankruptcy Code; (c) dismissing this Chapter 11 Case; or (d) pursuant to which this Court abstains from hearing this Chapter 11 Case. The terms and provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order, notwithstanding the entry of any such orders described in (a)–(d) above, shall continue in this Chapter 11 Case, or following dismissal of this Chapter 11 Case. 

37. Each and every federal, state, and local governmental agency, department, or official is hereby directed to accept any and all documents and instruments necessary and appropriate to consummate the Transaction, including the terms and transactions contemplated by the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the TSA. 

38. The Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA and the Transaction contemplated hereunder, shall not be subject to any bulk sales laws or any similar law of any state or jurisdiction. 

39. The IP License shall have the protections of section 365(n) of the Bankruptcy Code. 

40. The Stalking Horse Agreement may be modified, amended, or supplemented by the parties thereto in accordance with the terms thereof, without further order of the Court, provided that any such modification, amendment, or supplement does not, based on the Debtor’s business judgment, have a material adverse effect on the Debtor’s estate or its creditors. For the avoidance of doubt, all other modifications, amendments, or supplements that have a material adverse effect on the Debtor’s estate or its creditors shall require notice to the party or parties materially and adversely affected and Court approval. 

41. All time periods set forth in this 363 Free and Clear Order shall be calculated in accordance with Bankruptcy Rule 9006(a).

42. Notwithstanding the possible applicability of Bankruptcy Rules 6004(h), 6006(d), 7062, and 9014 or otherwise, the terms and conditions of this 363 Free and Clear Order shall be effective immediately upon entry and the Debtor and the Buyer are authorized to consummate the Transaction immediately upon entry of this 363 Free and Clear Order and to take any action and perform any act authorized under this Order. 

43. To the extent there is any explicit conflict between the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order and the Stalking Horse Agreement, the TSA, or the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the terms of the 363 Free and Clear Order shall control. Nothing contained in any chapter 11 plan hereinafter confirmed in this Chapter 11 Case or any order confirming such chapter 11 plan, or any other order of the Court, shall conflict with or derogate from the provisions of the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA, or the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order. To the extent this 363 Free and Clear Order is inconsistent with any prior order or pleading filed in this Chapter 11 Case, the terms of this 363 Free and Clear Order shall govern. 

44. The provisions of this 363 Free and Clear Order are nonseverable and mutually 

dependent.

45. The Court shall retain exclusive jurisdiction with respect to all matters arising from 

or related to the implementation, interpretation, or enforcement of this 363 Free and Clear Order, including matters related to the terms, provisions, and enforcement of this 363 Free and Clear Order and the Transaction, including the Stalking Horse Agreement, the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the TSA, any amendments thereto, any waiver and any related or ancillary agreement.
