# **<u>SCHEDULE D</u>** 

# **Data Processing Addendum** 

1. <u>Definitions.</u> 

   - (a) “Data Protection Law” means any Law (including any and all legislative and/or regulatory amendments or successors thereto) regarding privacy, data protection, information security, and/or the Processing of Personal Data. 

   - (b) “Personal Data” means any Service Recipient Data that relates to, or can be used to identify, contact, or precisely locate an identified or identifiable individual, and information considered to be personal data or personal information as defined under, or otherwise governed by, applicable Data Protection Law. 

   - (c) “Process,” “Processing” or “Processed” means any operation or set of operations which is performed upon Personal Data irrespective of the purposes and means applied, including access, collection, recording, organization, structuring, adaptation, alteration, retrieval, consultation, retention, storage, transfer, disclosure (including disclosure by transmission), dissemination or otherwise making available, alignment, combination, use, blocking, erasure and destruction. 

   - (d) “Security Incident” means any unlawful or unauthorized Processing, destruction, alteration, disclosure, loss, acquisition or access to Personal Data or any other compromise of Personal Data in the possession or control of Service Provider. For the avoidance of doubt, Service Recipient Systems are not in the possession, custody or control of Service Provider. 

   - (e) “Service Recipient Systems” means any system in the possession, custody or control of Service Recipient. 

   - (f) Unless otherwise defined, the capitalized terms in this Schedule D shall have the same meaning as such terms in the Agreement. 

2. <u>Data Processing.  The Parties acknowledge that Service Provider and its personnel may</u> provide limited professional services as a courtesy to Service Recipient in connection with the Services, and that such Services will be provided by Service Provider using only Service Recipient Systems.  Service Recipient remains solely responsible for determining the purposes and means of Processing, providing lawful and documented instructions, ensuring that such instructions comply with applicable Data Protection Laws, and satisfying all controller obligations with respect to Personal Data.  Service Provider will Process Personal Data only as reasonably necessary to provide the Services and only on Service Recipient’s documented instructions, except as otherwise required by Law.  To the extent Service Provider’s personnel access Personal Data, such access will be conducted directly through Service Recipient Systems using Service Recipient-managed laptops or devices, and Service Provider will not be required to receive, host, copy, retain, or maintain Personal Data outside of Service Recipient Systems unless expressly agreed in writing.

Service Provider’s obligations as processor are limited to the obligations required by Data Protection Laws and those expressly set forth in this Schedule D, and Service Provider will otherwise have no liability for Service Recipient Systems or Service Recipient’s instructions, data, notices, consents, legal bases, or other controller obligations.  Service Provider agrees that it will: 

   - (a) follow Service Recipient’s lawful, documented instructions for Processing Personal Data, provided that Service Provider shall not be responsible for determining whether such instructions comply with Data Protection Laws; 

   - (b) promptly notify Service Recipient if Service Provider determines an instruction of Service Recipient with respect to Personal Data is unlawful; 

   - (c) promptly notify Service Recipient if Service Provider determines that it can no longer comply with Service Recipient’s documented instructions or applicable processor obligations under this Schedule D; 

   - (d) ensure that Service Provider’s personnel who access Personal Data are subject to appropriate confidentiality obligations and receive privacy and security training appropriate to their role; 

   - (e) provide reasonable information in Service Provider’s possession or control necessary to demonstrate compliance with this <u>Schedule D, provided that unless otherwise</u> required by Data Protection Law, any review or audit will be limited to information reasonably necessary to assess Service Provider’s processor obligations and will not require access to Service Provider’s systems, networks, or any confidential information; and 

   - (f) provide reasonable assistance to Service Recipient to the extent such assistance is reasonably necessary for Service Recipient to comply with its controller obligations under Data Protection Laws and relates to Personal Data Processed by Service Provider under this Agreement. 

3. <u>Data Security.  Service Provider shall maintain reasonable and appropriate administrative,</u> technical, and organizational measures designed to protect Personal Data Processed by Service Provider against unauthorized Processing, access, disclosure, or loss, taking into account the nature of the limited access provided and the fact that access will occur through Service Recipient Systems on Service Recipient-managed laptops or devices. 

4. <u>Security Incident.  In the event Service Provider becomes aware of a Security Incident, it</u> shall notify Service Recipient promptly and without undue delay and in any event within forty-eight (48) hours of becoming aware of the Security Incident.  Unless otherwise required by applicable Law, Service Provider shall not notify any third party of, or make any public announcement concerning, the Security Incident where such notification or announcement expressly identifies Service Recipient without Service Recipient’s prior written consent.  Service Provider shall promptly investigate the Security Incident to the

extent possible within its reasonable capacity.  In the event of legal action brought by either Party against a third party in connection with a Security Incident, Service Provider and Service Recipient each agree that it shall provide such assistance as may be reasonably necessary to enable the other Party to successfully prosecute such legal action. 

5. <u>Data Subject Requests and Regulatory Requests.  Service Provider will provide reasonable</u> assistance to the extent Service Provider is legally required to assist and such assistance relates to Personal Data Processed by Service Provider under this Agreement.  Because access to Personal Data is expected to occur through Service Recipient Systems and Service Provider is not expected to separately host or retain Personal Data, Service Provider’s data subject and regulatory requests obligations shall apply only to Personal Data, if any, in Service Provider’s possession or control. 

6. <u>Controller Obligations.  Service Recipient will comply with all obligations of a controller</u> with respect to the Processing of Personal Data pursuant to this Agreement, including providing all required notices, obtaining all required consents or other legal bases, responding to data subject and regulatory requests, and ensuring that its instructions to Service Provider are lawful, complete, accurate, and documented. 

7. <u>Termination of Processing.  Upon termination or expiration of the Agreement, Service</u> Provider shall cease Processing Personal Data as soon as reasonably practicable, except to the extent continued Processing is required by Data Protection Law or necessary to complete wind-down activities.  Because access to Personal Data is expected to occur through Service Recipient Systems and Service Provider is not expected to separately host or retain Personal Data, Service Provider’s return or deletion obligations shall apply only to Personal Data, if any, in Service Provider’s possession or control. 

8. <u>Cross-Border Transfer.  In the event that Data Protection Laws impose restrictions on the</u> cross-border transfer of Personal Data that are not contemplated herein, the Parties agree to meet in good faith to complete any formalities and enter any documents as may be required by such Data Protection Laws. 

9. For the avoidance of doubt, the terms of this Schedule D shall not apply where both Parties agree that they act as independent controllers with regard to applicable Personal Data.  In the event that both Parties act as independent controllers with regard to any Services, the Parties shall negotiate in good faith the terms under which such Services shall be provided.

# **<u>EXHIBIT A</u>** 

# **Exclusivity Agreement** 

[ _See attached._ ]

Character Technologies, Inc. 700 El Camino Real, Suite 120, #1152 Menlo Park, CA 94025 

September 18, 2026 Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. 500 South Buena Vista Street Burbank, CA 91521-1245 Attention: Justin Radell 

Ladies and Gentlemen: 

WHEREAS, in connection with a proposed transaction involving Character Technologies, Inc. (the “ **Company** ”) and Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. (“ **Disney** ”) or one of its affiliates, the Company and Disney desire to enter into this letter agreement (the “ **Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement** ”), which provides for, among other things, that the parties hereto will enter into the agreements contemplated by the term sheet attached hereto as Annex A (the “ **Term Sheet** ”); 

AND WHEREAS, the Term Sheet contemplates two interrelated and integrated transactions, namely, the “ **Initial Transaction** ” and the “ **License Transaction** ” (collectively, the “ **Proposed Transaction** ”); 

AND WHEREAS, in an effort to assist the Company in its efforts to maximize value and minimize employee attrition risk, and with the cooperation and assistance of the Company, Disney intends to make offers of employment to certain Company employees and to provide transition services to the Company for up to five months thereafter; 

AND WHEREAS, subject to and following the Initial Transaction, and subject to the process set forth herein, the Term Sheet contemplates the Company will grant to Disney and/or one of its designated affiliates a perpetual, worldwide, non-exclusive license to certain Company intellectual property and related Technology (the “ **IP License** ”) and enter into a release, waiver and covenant not to sue agreement with respect to such intellectual property, which shall irrevocably encumber the intellectual property subject to the IP License with respect to the rights granted under such license; 

AND WHEREAS, the License Transaction will be subject to a competitive process and will be implemented free and clear of all claims (including successor liability), liabilities, interests and encumbrances, other than permitted encumbrances, pursuant to Sections 105 and 363 of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code; 

AND WHEREAS, the Term Sheet contemplates that the parties shall negotiate and enter into, on or prior to the closing of the Proposed Transaction, definitive agreements to give effect to the Proposed Transaction;

NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual promises, covenants and agreements of the parties hereto, and for other good and valuable consideration, the receipt and sufficiency of which are hereby acknowledged, the Company and Disney hereby agree as follows: 

# 1. <u>Exclusivity.</u> 

(a) The Company agrees that, from the date of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement through 5:00 p.m. (Eastern Time) on the earlier of (i) the date upon which the Company commences a chapter 11 case in accordance with this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the Term Sheet and (ii) fourteen (14) days following execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement (as such period may be extended pursuant to this Section 1(a), the “ **Exclusive Period** ”), the Company will not, directly or indirectly, and the Company will cause its officers, directors, employees, consultants, advisors, agents, affiliates (companies which the Company controls), controlling persons and other representatives (“ **Representatives** ”) to not, directly or indirectly, solicit, knowingly encourage, initiate, engage or participate in discussions with any third party (other than Disney or its Representatives), or provide any non-public information to any third party (other than Disney or its Representatives), regarding (i) any license, sale, assignment or other disposition of any intellectual property of the Company outside of the ordinary course of business, (ii) any financing, sale of equity, merger, recapitalization, liquidation or similar transaction involving the Company or (iii) any hiring, engagement or transfer of any employees of the Company (each a “ **Strategic Transaction** ”); _provided_ , _however_ , if a chapter 11 case is commenced, the Exclusive Period obligations in this paragraph (including in any Definitive Agreement) will cease (the “ **Chapter 11 Exception** ”). Notwithstanding the foregoing, the Exclusive Period shall automatically be extended for successive seven (7) day periods if, at the expiration of the then-current Exclusive Period, the parties are working diligently and in good faith toward the commencement of a chapter 11 case and the consummation of the Proposed Transaction; _provided_ , _however_ , that in no event shall the Exclusive Period extend beyond thirty (30) days following execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement without the written mutual agreement of the Company and Disney. The Company will promptly notify Disney of any _bona fide_ inquiry, offer or proposal relating to a potential Strategic Transaction received during the Exclusive Period. Upon the commencement of the Exclusive Period, but subject to the Chapter 11 Exception, the Company shall, and shall cause its Representatives to, immediately cease and cause to be terminated all existing discussions, conversations, negotiations and other communications with all other third parties (other than Disney and its Representatives) conducted heretofore with respect to any potential Strategic Transaction, and shall terminate any access to any virtual or physical data room to which access was previously furnished to any such person in connection with such a potential transaction. 

(b) Upon the expiration of the later of (i) the thirtieth (30<sup>th</sup> ) day following execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, unless such time is otherwise extended by mutual agreement of the parties and (ii) the expiration of the Exclusive Period, if Disney is ready, willing and able to execute the Definitive Agreements (as defined in the Term Sheet) in forms reasonably acceptable to the Company and Disney and progress the Proposed Transaction but the Company does not commence a chapter 11 case in the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware on the terms set forth in the Term Sheet (including, but not limited to, having Disney serve as stalking horse bidder for the intellectual property subject to the IP License

referenced in the Term Sheet), then the Company shall promptly and fully refund to Disney the Refundable Deposit (as defined below) and Disney shall have no further obligation to the Company with respect to the Proposed Transaction or the subject matter of the Term Sheet. 

(c) The terms of the Mutual Non-Disclosure Agreement, by and between Disney and the Company, dated August 19, 2026 (the “ **NDA** ”), shall remain in full force and effect and apply to this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and, for the avoidance of doubt, this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall be considered Transaction Information (as defined in the NDA).  During the Exclusive Period, the Company will use commercially reasonable efforts to provide Disney with reasonable access to information and management to facilitate Disney’s due diligence.  Each party will be responsible for its own costs and expenses, including, without limitation, all fees and expenses of such party’s Representatives, incurred in connection with a potential transaction, including the negotiation of a term sheet and the negotiation, delivery and execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the definitive agreements underlying the Proposed Transaction, including the Definitive Agreements (as defined in the Term Sheet). 

(d) All communications relating to the Proposed Transaction by the Company or its Representatives shall be directed exclusively to the designated members of the Disney deal team (collectively, the “ **Disney Deal Team** ”) and its legal, financial and other professional advisors.  Disney shall designate the members of the Disney Deal Team in writing to the Company. The Company and its Representatives shall not initiate or engage in communications relating to the Proposed Transaction with any other employees, officers, directors or shareholders of Disney without the prior written consent of the Disney Deal Team. 

(e) All communications relating to the Proposed Transaction by Disney or its Representatives shall be directed exclusively to the designated members of the Company deal team (collectively, the “ **Company Deal Team** ”) and its legal, financial and other professional advisors. The Company shall designate the members of the Company Deal Team in writing to Disney. Disney and its Representatives shall not initiate or engage in communications relating to the Proposed Transaction with any other employees, officers, directors or shareholders of the Company without the prior written consent of the Company Deal Team. 

2. <u>Definitive Agreements. Upon execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment</u> Agreement, the parties shall use reasonable best efforts and shall work in good faith and expeditiously to execute the Definitive Agreements (as defined in the Term Sheet) on terms consistent with, and not deviating from, the terms set forth in the Term Sheet and implement the Proposed Transaction. 

# 3. <u>Prepayment.</u> 

(a) The parties agree that the Company shall be entitled to receive a total of US$20 million in the aggregate as cash consideration for entering into this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the TSA and consummating the Proposed Transaction (the “ **Cash Consideration** ”). US$5.5 million of the Cash Consideration shall be paid to the Company by Disney on the date hereof to an account specified in writing (email being sufficient) by the Company (the “ **Up-Front Payment** ”), which shall be non-refundable in all circumstances. In addition, US$2.0 million of the Cash Consideration shall be paid to the Company by Disney on

the date hereof to an account specified in writing (email being sufficient) by the Company, which amount shall be a deposit (the “ **Refundable Deposit** ”) against the Cash Consideration that is promptly and fully refundable to Disney if (i) Disney is not selected as the winning bidder (to the extent there is an auction), (ii) the Proposed Transaction is not consummated prior to the Outside Date (as may be extended by mutual agreement) or (iii) Disney is entitled to a refund under Section 1(b). To the extent the Refundable Deposit is payable to Disney in accordance with the terms of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the Term Sheet, Disney shall provide a notice to the Company specifying the account to which the Company shall wire funds in the amount of the Refundable Deposit (the “ **Refundable Deposit Notice** ”), and the Company shall wire such funds to Disney no later than three business days following the date of the Refundable Deposit Notice. 

(b) At the closing of the License Transaction (the “ **Closing** ”), the remaining US$12.5 million of the Cash Consideration shall be paid to the Company by Disney to an account specified in writing (email being sufficient) by the Company (the “ **Closing Payment** ”). Employee compensation, retention payments and equity incentives will be payable directly by Disney to employees and will not constitute consideration payable to the Company. Each of the Up-Front Payment, the Refundable Deposit and the Closing Payment will be subject to applicable withholding. The Company will deliver to Disney a duly executed IRS Form W-9 on the date hereof. 

# 4. <u>Release and Waiver.</u> 

(a) The Company, for itself and on behalf of its affiliates and each of its and their Representatives, and any successors and assigns of the foregoing (individually and collectively, the “ **Releasing Parties** ”), hereby irrevocably, unconditionally and forever acquits, releases, waives and discharges, and further covenants and agrees that it will not assert, and completely waives and relinquishes, any claims against Disney and its affiliates and any of its or their Representatives (including any Company Service Provider hired by Disney or any of its affiliates (“ **Hired Employees** ”)), divisions, affiliated corporations, affiliated non-corporation entities and any successors, predecessors and assigns of the foregoing (individually and collectively, the “ **Disney Parties** ”) from any and all past, present and future debts, losses, costs, bonds, suits, actions, causes of action, liabilities, contributions, attorneys’ fees, interest, damages, punitive damages, expenses, claims, potential claims, counterclaims, cross-claims or demands, in law or in equity, asserted or unasserted, express or implied, actual or potential, known or unknown, matured or unmatured, contingent or vested, liquidated or unliquidated, of any kind or nature or description whatsoever, that a Releasing Party had, presently has or may hereafter have or claim or assert to have against any of the Disney Parties by reason of any act, omission, transaction, occurrence, conduct, circumstance, condition, harm, matter, cause or thing that has occurred or existed at any time from the beginning of time up to and through the date hereof (collectively, “ **Claims** ”), including any Claims that in any way arise from or out of, are based upon or indirectly or directly relate to the following: 

(i) the negotiation or execution of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the Term Sheet or any of the transactions contemplated hereby, other than any claim arising out of a breach of Disney’s obligations under the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or, for the avoidance of doubt, if entered into, the other Definitive Agreements;

- (ii) the recruitment, solicitation, hiring, employment or engagement by Disney or any of its affiliates of, or the offer of employment by Disney or any of its affiliates to, the Offered Employees or any other current or former employee, consultant or other service provider of the Company or any of its affiliates (“ **Company Service Providers** ”), and any restriction applicable to any of the Company Service Providers with respect to entering into any and all arrangements with, and providing any and all services and information to, the Disney Parties; 

- (iii) any wind-up, liquidation, dissolution or cessation of the Company’s and its affiliates’ business operations or the sale or license of the Company’s and its affiliates’ assets, including the termination of any Company Service Provider; and 

- (iv) the provision of any services by (x) any Company Service Provider to Disney or the other Disney Parties and (y) any Hired Employee to the Company or any of its affiliates (in each case, including the use of any Technology and Intellectual Property Rights developed by such employees, consultants or other service providers during their employment with, or provision of services to, the Company or any of its affiliates, or otherwise developed or owned by the Company or its affiliates, or the use of any ideas, know-how and techniques) (all Claims, including the foregoing (i)-(iv), collectively, the “ **Released Matters** ”); 

_provided_ , _however_ , that such Releasing Party is not releasing and does not acquit, waive, relinquish or discharge, and Released Matters does not include, any rights or claims (x) available pursuant to this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the Term Sheet or any other related agreement to which the Releasing Party is a party or the transactions contemplated hereby or thereby (including any rights to payment hereby or thereby), including the other Definitive Agreements, and any employment, compensation or other arrangements between a Hired Employee and Disney or its affiliates, (y) under any other agreement entered into between such Releasing Party and any Disney Party, or (z) any matter that cannot be released as a matter of applicable law. The Company, for itself and on behalf of the Releasing Parties, hereby covenants not to sue or otherwise seek to enforce or assert any rights against Disney or any of the Disney Parties in connection with any of the Released Matters. Further, without limiting the foregoing, the Company, for itself and on behalf of the Releasing Parties, hereby covenants not to sue or otherwise seek to enforce or assert any rights against Disney or any of the Disney Parties in connection with the recruitment, solicitation, hiring, employment or engagement by Disney or any of its affiliates of, or the offer of employment by Disney or any of its affiliates to, a Company Service Provider (in each case, whether arising prior to, concurrently with or following the Closing). 

(b) As a condition to Disney entering into this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the Company agrees and acknowledges that Disney and its affiliates are express thirdparty beneficiaries of the provisions of Section 4 of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement with respect to Released Matters as such Released Matters pertain to the Hired Employees and shall have the right, exercisable in the sole discretion of Disney and its affiliates, to enforce the terms and conditions of this Section 4, or prevent the breach thereof, or to exercise any other right,

or seek any other remedy, which may be available to Disney and its affiliates as third-party beneficiaries of Section 4 of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, in each case, in respect of the Released Matters. 

(c) The releases contained herein are intended to be complete, global and allencompassing and specifically include claims that are known, unknown, fixed, contingent or conditional with respect to the matters described herein as of the date hereof. The Company hereby expressly waives any and all rights conferred upon it by any statute or rule of law which provides that a release does not extend to claims which the claimant does not know or suspect to exist in its favor at the time of executing the release, which if known by it must have materially affected its settlement with the released party, including the following provisions of California Civil Code Section 1542: “A GENERAL RELEASE DOES NOT EXTEND TO CLAIMS THAT THE CREDITOR OR RELEASING PARTY DOES NOT KNOW OR SUSPECT TO EXIST IN HIS OR HER FAVOR AT THE TIME OF EXECUTING THE RELEASE AND THAT, IF KNOWN BY HIM OR HER, WOULD HAVE MATERIALLY AFFECTED HIS OR HER SETTLEMENT WITH THE DEBTOR OR RELEASED PARTY.” 

(d) The following definitions shall be for all purposes, unless otherwise clearly indicated to the contrary, applied to the terms used in this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the Term Sheet: 

- (i) “ **Derivative Work** ” means any “derivative work” as defined in the Copyright Law of the U.S., Title 17 U.S.C. § 101 _et seq._ (the “ **Copyright Law** ”). 

- (ii) “ **Intellectual Property Rights** ” means any and all tangible and intangible and now known or hereafter existing (a) rights associated with works of authorship throughout the universe, including copyrights (including the sole and exclusive right to prepare Derivative Works of the copyrighted work and to copy, manufacture, reproduce, distribute copies of, modify, publicly perform and publicly display the copyrighted work and all Derivative Works thereof), Moral Rights, author’s rights and mask-work rights; (b) rights in and relating to the protection of trade secrets and confidential information; (c) patents, designs, algorithms and other industrial property rights and rights associated therewith; (d) other intellectual and industrial property and proprietary rights (of every kind and nature throughout the universe and however designated) relating to intangible property that are analogous to any of the foregoing rights (including logos, character rights, “rental” rights and rights to remuneration), whether arising by operation of law, contract, license or otherwise; (e) if applicable, registrations, applications, renewals, extensions, continuations, divisions or reissues thereof now or hereafter in force in the U.S. and throughout the universe (including rights in any of the foregoing); and (f) rights in and relating to the sole and exclusive possession, ownership and use of any of the foregoing throughout the universe, including the right to license and sublicense, franchise, assign, pledge, mortgage, sell, transfer, convey, grant, gift over, divide, partition and use (or not use) in any way any of the foregoing now

or hereafter; provided that, for purposes of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, “Intellectual Property Rights” shall not include (x) domain names, uniform resource locators, other names and locators associated with the Internet, and applications or registrations therefor or social media accounts or handles, or (y) trade names, logos, common law trademarks and service marks, trademark and service mark registrations or any other brand elements, and applications therefor (including intent to use applications) throughout the world. 

- (iii) “ **Moral Rights** ” means rights of attribution, paternity and integrity, claims to authorship, rights to object to any distortion, mutilation or other modification of, or other derogatory actions in relation to, any Intellectual Property Rights, whether or not such would be prejudicial to a party’s honor or reputation, and any similar right, existing under applicable laws. 

- (iv) “ **Technology** ” means all technology, methods, techniques, processes, designs, design rules, inventions (whether or not patented or patentable), diagrams, plans, discoveries, ideas, concepts, prototypes, specifications, schematics, formulae, compositions, communication protocols, algorithms, routines, reports, layout designs, topographies, cores, blocks, libraries, technology business plans, technology proposals, technology financial information, technology pricing and cost information, bills of material, user interfaces, websites, network configurations and architectures, test vectors and procedures, protocols, works of authorship, artificial intelligence (including deep learning, machine learning, natural language processing, neural network and reinforcement learning technologies, foundation models, language models, multi-modal models, world models, robotics and embodied models and agentic and embedding models), including all components, artifacts, materials and information embodying, comprising or used to develop, train, fine-tune, evaluate, deploy or operate such technologies (such as architectures, weights, parameters and other model artifacts, training, fine-tuning, evaluation, inference and orchestration pipelines, code, recipes, configurations, prompts, safety controls, benchmarks, evaluation and experiment results and memory and retrieval systems), software, files, information, documentation, test data, design data, performance data, quality data, technical data, datasets, databases and data collections, metadata and other scientific or technical information, as well as any other forms of technology and embodiments of Intellectual Property Rights, in each case whether or not embodied in any tangible form. Notwithstanding the foregoing, for purposes of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, “Technology” shall not include (i) customer data or databases and data collections of customer data, or any personally identifiable information or (ii) Unprocessed Data. 

- (v) “ **Unprocessed Data** ” means any data collected by or on behalf of the Company or its affiliates from publicly available sources and in Company’s or its affiliates’ possession or control that has not undergone the Company’s

standard practices for onboarding such data into one or more Company database(s) of data available for training purposes, including preprocessing, de-identification, classification, reformatting, data cleaning or other similar processing activities, and that is identified to Disney reasonably in advance and in writing as “Unprocessed Data.” 

5. <u>Termination. This Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement will automatically</u> terminate, and be of no further force and effect, upon the earlier of (i) the date on which Disney and the Company mutually agree that this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall terminate; (ii) the occurrence of the repayment of the Refundable Deposit to Disney by the Company in accordance with Section 1(b) or 3(a) of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement; and (iii) the Outside Date (as defined in the Term Sheet); _provided_ , that Section 1(b), Section 3(a) and Section 4 of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall survive any termination of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the Closing indefinitely; _provided further_ that the termination of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement will not relieve either Disney or the Company from any liability for willful and material breach of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement. 

# 6. <u>Miscellaneous.</u> 

(a) To the extent that the terms of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and the Term Sheet conflict, the terms of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall govern. 

(b) All capitalized terms used and not otherwise defined in this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall have the respective meanings ascribed to them in the Term Sheet. 

(c) This Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement may be executed and delivered in counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original but all of which together shall constitute one and the same instrument. This Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall become effective when one or more counterparts have been signed by each of the parties hereto and delivered to the other party. Such delivery may be made by exchange of copies of the signature page by email transmission or other electronic means. 

(d) Each of the parties hereto agrees that no failure or delay by any party hereto in exercising any of its rights, powers or privileges hereunder shall operate as a waiver thereof, nor shall any single or partial exercise thereof preclude any other or further exercise thereof or the exercise of any other right, power or privilege hereunder. 

(e) This Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement (and any disputes arising in connection herewith, whether in contract, tort or otherwise) shall be governed by and construed in accordance with the laws of the State of Delaware, regardless of the laws that might otherwise govern under the applicable principles of conflicts of laws thereof. Each party hereto (i) irrevocably submits to the personal jurisdiction of the United States District Court for the District of Delaware, or if and only if the United States District Court does not have jurisdiction, then before the Court of Chancery of the State of Delaware, or if and only if neither such court shall have jurisdiction, then before any other state or federal court sitting in Delaware having subject matter jurisdiction, and (ii) agrees that any actions or proceedings arising in connection with this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, the Term Sheet or the transactions contemplated by this

Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or the Term Sheet shall be brought, tried and determined exclusively in the courts referred to in clause (i) of this paragraph 6(e) and consents to service of process outside the State of Delaware pursuant to the requirements of such court in any matter subject to it.  The parties irrevocably waive, to the fullest extent permitted by law, any objection that they may have to the laying of venue of any such action or proceeding in such courts and any claim that any such action or proceeding brought in any such court has been brought in an inconvenient forum. 

(f) EACH OF THE PARTIES HERETO KNOWINGLY, VOLUNTARILY AND IRREVOCABLY WAIVES, TO THE FULLEST EXTENT PERMITTED BY LAW, ALL RIGHT TO TRIAL BY JURY IN ANY ACTION, PROCEEDING OR COUNTERCLAIM (WHETHER BASED ON CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE) ARISING OUT OF OR RELATING TO THIS EXCLUSIVITY AND PREPAYMENT AGREEMENT OR THE ACTIONS OF ANY PARTY HERETO IN NEGOTIATION, ADMINISTRATION, PERFORMANCE OR ENFORCEMENT OF THIS EXCLUSIVITY AND PREPAYMENT AGREEMENT. 

(g) The parties agree that irreparable damage would occur in the event that any of the provisions of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement were not performed in accordance with their specific terms or were otherwise breached and that monetary damages, even if available, would not be an adequate remedy therefor. It is accordingly agreed that the parties shall be entitled to an injunction or injunctions to prevent breaches of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and to enforce specifically the terms and provisions of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, in each case without proof of damages or the posting of any bond or other security, this being in addition to any other remedy to which any party is entitled at law or in equity. 

(h) All notices, consents, waivers and other communications required or permitted by this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement shall be in writing and shall be deemed given to a party when (i) delivered to the appropriate address by hand or by nationally recognized overnight courier service (costs prepaid), (ii) sent by email if sent during the addressee’s normal business hours, and if not sent during normal business hours, then on the addressee’s next business day, or (iii) received or rejected by the addressee, if sent by certified mail, return receipt requested, in each case to the following addresses or email addresses and marked to the attention of the individual (by name or title) designated below (or to such other address, email address or individual as a party may designate by notice to the other party):

If any term or provision of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement is determined to be unenforceable by reason of its extent, duration, scope or otherwise, then the parties agree that the court making such determination shall reduce such extent, duration, scope or other provision in the minimum amount necessary to make such provision enforceable and enforce it in its reduced form for all purposes contemplated by this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement. 

(k) This Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, together with the Term Sheet and the NDA, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties hereto concerning the subject matter hereof, and no modification of this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement or waiver of the terms and conditions hereof shall be binding upon either party hereto, unless approved in writing by both such parties. 

(l) The words “including,” “includes” or “include” in this Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement are to be read as listing non-exclusive examples of the matters referred to, whether or not words such as “without limitation” or “but not limited to” are used in each instance.

# **<u>Annex A</u>** 

# Binding Term Sheet 

This binding term sheet (the “ **Term Sheet** ”) between Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. (“ **Disney** ”) and Character Technologies, Inc. (“ **CAI** ” or the “ **Company** ”) sets forth a commitment by Disney and the Company to negotiate in good faith the Proposed Transaction (as defined below) and enter into the Definitive Agreements (as defined below). The terms of the Mutual Non-Disclosure Agreement, by and between Disney and CAI, dated August 19, 2026 (the “ **NDA** ”), shall remain in full force and effect and apply to this Term Sheet and, for the avoidance of doubt, this Term Sheet shall be considered Transaction Information (as defined in the NDA). 

The terms and conditions of the Proposed Transaction are not limited to those set forth herein, and this Term Sheet is not intended to describe all of the terms, conditions and other provisions of the Proposed Transaction or that are to be contained in the Definitive Agreements. This Term Sheet does not purport to summarize all of the terms of the Definitive Agreements to be entered into by the parties hereto with respect to the Proposed Transaction. 

|**1. Proposed Transaction**|•<br>This Term Sheet contemplates two interrelated and integrated transactions:<br>the “**Initial Transaction**” and the “**License Transaction**” (collectively, the<br>“**Proposed Transaction**”).<br>“**Initial Transaction”**<br>•<br>In an effort to assist the Company in its efforts to maximize value and<br>minimize employee attrition risk, the Company will provide Disney with<br>the right to solicit and hire certain Company employees. Thereafter, Disney,<br>working together with the Company, will provide offer letters and related<br>employment documentation to Key Employees and the other Offered<br>Employees (each as defined below).  Disney and the Company will work<br>collaboratively and in good faith to transition the applicable employees to<br>Disney as soon as reasonably practicable.<br>•<br>During and following the onboarding of Key Employees and the other<br>Offered Employees, Disney will provide certain agreed upon transition<br>services to the Company pursuant to a transition services agreement<br>(the “**TSA**”).  In connection with these employee-related matters, including<br>the transition services, and in consideration for the same, the Company will<br>grant Disney releases that will be incorporated into an exclusivity and<br>prepayment agreement signed by the Company (the “**Exclusivity and**<br>**Prepayment Agreement**”).<br>•<br>The onboarding and transition of employees to Disney and the provision of<br>transition services will be documented through (i) offer letters and related<br>employment documentation with the Key Employees and the other Offered<br>Employees, (ii) the TSA, (iii) the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement<br>and (iv) such side letters, resignations, consents and waivers as the parties<br>agree.<br>**“License Transaction” **|
|---|---|
||•<br>Subject to and following the Initial Transaction, and subject to the process<br>set forth herein, the Company will grant to Disney and/or one of its<br>designated affiliates (the“**Licensee**”) a perpetual, worldwide, non-|

exclusive license to certain Company intellectual property and related Technology (the “ **IP License** ”) and enter into a release, waiver and covenant not to sue agreement (the “ **Release, Waiver and Covenant Not to Sue Agreement** ”), which shall irrevocably encumber the intellectual property subject to the IP License with respect to the rights granted under such license.  For the avoidance of doubt, references in this Term Sheet to the “License Transaction” refers solely to the IP License and the Release, Waiver and Covenant Not to Sue Agreement, and excludes all employee hiring, onboarding, transition, retention and related arrangements described in this Section 1.  The License Transaction will be subject to a competitive process and will be implemented free and clear of all claims (including successor liability), liabilities, interests, and encumbrances, other than permitted encumbrances (“ **Permitted Encumbrances** ”) pursuant to Sections 105 and 363 of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code (any Bankruptcy Court order approving the transaction, which shall be a final, non-appealable order and in form and substance acceptable to Disney in its sole discretion, a “ **363 Free and Clear Order** ”). The licensed IP that is the subject of the License Transaction shall have the protections of Section 365(n) of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code, and shall be memorialized in the IP License and in the 363 Free and Clear Order. • As part of the 363 sale process, Disney will purchase any claims that the Company has against Disney employees that accept offers of employment with Disney that have not previously been released by the Company (the “ **Purchased Employee Claims** ”). • The venue for the Company’s chapter 11 bankruptcy case shall be Delaware and the timeline related to any such 363 sale process shall be reasonably acceptable to Disney. • In connection with any such competitive process, (i) as an inducement for Disney to be used as the floor bid for the License Transaction, (A) Disney will be named as stalking horse bidder for the IP License and granted customary bid protections, including a break-up fee and reasonable expense reimbursement collectively capped at 3.5% of the cash purchase price (the “ **Bid Protections** ”), and (B) Disney and the Company will agree on bid procedures, including a timeline for a bid procedures hearing and entry of a court order, a deadline for bid submissions, the auction (if any) and an approval hearing; and (ii) if a competing bidder other than Disney is the winning bidder for the IP License, or if another bidder is the winning bidder for substantially all of the Company’s assets (including the intellectual property related to the IP License), properties, businesses, or operations, including through an acquisition of an exclusive license of the Company’s intellectual property, then Disney will provide to such winning bidder transition services reasonably comparable to the TSA for a limited but reasonable period of time no greater than the lesser of (a) 60 days postclosing and (b) 75 days after conclusion of the auction; provided, that if there are multiple winning bidders, Disney shall only be required to provide transition services to one winning bidder. In connection with any such competitive process, Disney and CAI will work together in good faith to identify five (5) Key Employees who shall be made reasonably available for diligence with potential bidders who have (w) signed a non-disclosure agreement with the Company, (x) submitted evidence to the Company demonstrating the party’s financial capability to consummate a proposed <u>transaction, (y) submitted to the Company a preliminary indication of</u>

|interest and (z) who the Company expects will be deemed to be qualified<br>bidders under the relevant bidding procedures approved by the U.S.<br>Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “**Qualified Bidders**”).<br>In addition, if Disney is not the winning bidder, then Disney will allow one<br>winning bidder to interview and make employment offers to former<br>Company employees who wish to consider opportunities with such winning<br>bidder and will work in good faith with such winning bidder to transition<br>those employees who accept an offer. Disney shall make reasonably<br>available the former Company employees for the purpose described in the<br>immediately preceding sentence and shall not interfere with, hinder or take<br>any other action intended to impede the interview or offer process, provided<br>that such employees can still perform their day-to-day work obligations in<br>the ordinary course.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, Disney may designate<br>up to 20% of the former Company employees as outside the scope of such<br>process and Disney need not make available or allow any party a right to<br>interview or make employment offers to such former Company employees.|
|---|
|•<br>Except for the rights granted under the IP License, the Company shall retain<br>all right, title and interest in and to its assets, properties, businesses and<br>operations. The Licensee shall not assume, and the Company and its<br>successors shall not take any position that the Licensee has assumed, any<br>liabilities or obligations of the Company or its past, current or future<br>affiliates, whether arising before, on or after the closing of the Initial<br>Transaction or the License Transaction.  The Company shall retain<br>responsibility for all such liabilities and obligations.|
|•<br>The 363 Free and Clear Order shall provide customary findings, including<br>that (i) the Proposed Transaction is not structured as, and is not intended to<br>be treated as, an acquisition of equity or of all or substantially all of the<br>assets or business of the Company, (ii) no employees will be deemed to<br>transfer to Licensee by operation of law and (iii) Licensee will not be, and<br>will not be deemed to be, a successor to the Company or its business. Any<br>363 Free and Clear Order shall provide for a release of Licensee by the<br>Company for all matters related to the Proposed Transaction. The 363 Free<br>and Clear Order shall provide for Disney’s purchase of any Purchased<br>Employee Claims.|
|•<br>The License Transaction will be documented through (i) the IP License, (ii)<br>the Release, Waiver and Covenant Not to Sue Agreement, (iii) a form of<br>transition services or services agreement available to a winning bidder, if<br>applicable, which will be reasonably comparable in terms to the TSA, and<br>(iv) a 363 Free and Clear Order.|
|•<br>Following the closing of the Proposed Transaction, the Company will<br>remain a separate legal entity and may, in its sole discretion, continue to<br>operate its business or wind down, dissolve or liquidate.  At no time shall<br>Disney have any responsibility for the operation, management or<br>continuation of the Company’s business.|
|•<br>The Proposed Transaction is not intended to and shall not constitute a<br>transfer of the Company’s business as a going concern.  Disney will not<br>acquire or operate the Company’s consumer-facing platform, products,<br>services, customer accounts, contracts, trademarks, trade names, websites,<br>domains or ongoing business operations.  Any employment of Company<br>personnel by Disney will result from separate employment arrangements<br>entered into directly between Disney and such individuals and will not|

|**2. Consideration**|•<br>The Company will receive US$20 million in the aggregate as cash<br>consideration for the Proposed Transaction (the “**Cash Consideration**”).<br>•<br>US$5.5 million of the Cash Consideration shall be paid upon the execution<br>of the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and TSA and shall not be<br>refundable (the “**Up-Front Payment**”), which will be non-refundable in all<br>circumstances.<br>•<br>In addition, another US$2.0 million shall be paid upon the execution of the<br>Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement and TSA, which amount shall be a<br>deposit (the “**Refundable Deposit**”) against the Cash Consideration that is<br>promptly and fully refundable to Disney should Disney not be selected as<br>the winning bidder (to the extent there is an auction) and the Proposed<br>Transaction not be consummated.<br>•<br>US$12.5 million of the Cash Consideration shall be paid upon the closing<br>of the License Transaction.<br>•<br>Employee compensation, retention payments and equity incentives will be<br>payable directly to employees and will not constitute consideration payable<br>to the Company. Disney and the Company will mutually agree on<br>appropriate scope and quantum of any retention program.<br>•<br>All payments will be subject to applicable withholding, and the Company<br>will deliver a duly executed IRS Form W-9 at or prior to the closing.|
|**3. Due Diligence**|•<br>Disney will engage in a customary and comprehensive due diligence<br>process that includes financial, business, Technology, HR, accounting,<br>legal, regulatory and tax review for the Proposed Transaction and all related<br>business, legal and financial data by internal and external experts.<br>•<br>If requested by Disney, the Company will use commercially reasonable<br>efforts to accommodate Disney and its external advisors to conduct due<br>diligence on-site to reduce the time needed to complete due diligence.<br>•<br>Diligence will include a customary review of intellectual property and<br>Technology matters, including ownership and chain of title, Permitted<br>Encumbrances (including disclosure and information related to any existing<br>licenses), employee and contractor invention assignment agreements, open<br>source usage and copyleft exposure, third-party licenses and dependencies,<br>model weights and the provenance of training data and datasets.<br>•<br>Diligence will include a customary review of employment matters relating<br>to the Key Employees and the other Offered Employees, including existing<br>restrictive covenants, confidentiality and invention assignment obligations,<br>equity, deferred compensation and severance arrangements, and visa and<br>work authorization status.<br>•<br>The Company will use commercially reasonable efforts to make the Key<br>Employees and its senior technical personnel reasonably available for<br>diligence sessions and technical reviews, subject to the NDA.<br>•<br>The Company will provide Disney with reasonable access to documents<br>relevant to the due diligence process.|

|**4. Licensed Intellectual**<br>**Property**|•<br>In connection with the IP License, the Company will license to Licensee<br>Company’s (and not a third party’s) training infrastructure, training<br>pipelines, know-how, evaluation frameworks, safety tools, documentation,<br>design-specifications and technical materials reasonably necessary to<br>utilize the licensed Technology and develop future models and any<br>embodiment of such intellectual property.  The IP License will include only<br>such intellectual property and Technology as is reasonably necessary for<br>Licensee’s approved use cases.<br>•<br>The Company shall not license to Licensee any finished models, user data,<br>training data, corporate trademarks, trade names, branding, website content,<br>domain names, social media accounts or consumer-facing applications or<br>any third party technology.<br>•<br>The IP License will be perpetual, irrevocable, non-terminable, worldwide,<br>royalty-free, fully paid-up, and non-exclusive and transferable, but only to<br>(a) a buyer of a business, asset or entity from Disney that is using the IP<br>License at the time of closing of such sale (a “**Disney Assignee**”), or (b) a<br>successor in connection with a sale of a business to which the IP License<br>relates, with the right to sublicense through multiple tiers as described<br>below, and will survive any termination of the Definitive Agreements, any<br>change of control, or assignment or exclusive licensing of the licensed<br>intellectual property occurring after the date of the IP License and any<br>bankruptcy or insolvency of the Company (including the protections<br>available under Section 365(n) of the U.S. Bankruptcy Code).  For the<br>avoidance of doubt, Licensee may not sublicense (other than as set forth in<br>the following bullet point), transfer or assign the IP License on a standalone<br>basis to any third party.<br>•<br>The IP License (a) will allow for the provision of products and services<br>only under the branding owned or controlled by Disney and its affiliates<br>or to a Disney Assignee, and (b) is not sublicensable to third parties other<br>than to (i) consumer end users of such Disney products and services, (ii)<br>service providers of Disney and its affiliates in connection with the<br>provision of services to Disney and its affiliates, (iii) affiliates of Disney<br>but only for so long as such entity is an affiliate, and (iv) to a Disney<br>Assignee in accordance with the immediately preceding bullet, but only to<br>conduct the acquired business as it was conducted as of such divestiture<br>and its natural evolutions.<br>•<br>The Company will retain ownership of the licensed intellectual property<br>and may continue to use, develop, license and otherwise exploit it.<br>Licensee by virtue of being the author or creator, will own all<br>modifications, improvements, enhancements, Derivative Works and<br>outputs (including any models, weights and synthetic data) created by or<br>for Licensee using the licensed intellectual property.<br>•<br>Following the closing of the License Transaction, the Company will have<br>no obligation to prosecute, maintain, enforce, support or update the licensed<br>intellectual property and related Technology.|
|---|---|

|**5. Employees**|•<br>Within ten (10) days of the date of this Exclusivity and Prepayment<br>Agreement, Disney will require not more than six (6) employees of the<br>Company, to be mutually-agreed by Disney and the Company, and one of<br>which shall be  the Company’s chief executive officer (the “**Key**<br>**Employees**”) to accept an offer letter or enter into an employment<br>agreement on a form substantially similar to Disney’s standard form and to<br>execute a resignation letter resigning from all positions with the Company<br>and its subsidiaries, in each case effective as of the closing of the Proposed<br>Transaction, with a subsidiary of Disney.<br>•<br>Disney will offer employment to at least sixty (60) other Company<br>employees (such employees, together with the Key Employees, the<br>“**Offered Employees**”), at each such employee’s appropriate salary, bonus<br>and Disney stock-based compensation levels according to Disney’s then-<br>current compensation practices.  Based on a preliminary review of<br>approximately 65 Offered Employees, Disney estimates the annual run rate<br>total compensation of the Offered Employees to be approximately US$60<br>million.<br>•<br>Disney will also provide an aggregate retention pool in an amount equal to<br>US$30 million to incentivize such employees to remain employed at<br>Disney.  A portion of the retention payment will be payable on each of the<br>first and second anniversaries of the closing of the Initial Transaction and<br>subject to continued employment through each such anniversary.  This<br>retention pool will supplement the compensation during years 1 and 2 until<br>the run rate is reached.  The estimated total compensation and retention over<br>years 1-3 is estimated to be approximately US$ 180 million.<br>•<br>Each Offered Employee shall independently determine whether to accept<br>employment with Disney, and no employment relationship with Disney will<br>arise until such employee’s employment with the Company has terminated.<br>•<br>The Company will waive any applicable notice periods and will not enforce<br>against any Offered Employee any non-competition, non-solicitation,<br>confidentiality or invention assignment covenant that would restrict such<br>employee from being employed by, or providing services to, Disney or its<br>affiliates.<br>•<br>The Company will remain responsible for all compensation, benefits,<br>accrued paid time off, employment taxes and severance, if any, owed to<br>each Offered Employee through such employee’s last day of employment<br>with the Company (including as and to the extent triggered by their<br>separation from the Company).<br>•<br>The Company will release all employees, including its chief executive<br>officer, that accept offers of employment with Disney from claims and<br>causes of action including, as applicable, fiduciary duty claims.|

|---|---|
|**8. Restrictive Covenants;**<br>**Non-Solicitation**|•<br>From the closing of the Initial Transaction until the third anniversary of the<br>closing of the Initial Transaction, the Company will not, and will cause its<br>subsidiaries not to, directly or indirectly solicit, recruit or encourage any<br>Offered Employee who becomes employed by Disney or any of its affiliates<br>to leave such employment, or assist any other person in doing so; provided<br>that general advertisements and searches by recruiters that are not targeted<br>at such employees, and hiring of any such employee following the date that<br>is six (6) months after such employee ceases to be employed by Disney and<br>its affiliates, will not violate this covenant.|
|**9. Transition Services**|•<br>To the extent the Company requires assistance from Offered Employees<br>hired by Disney in order to satisfy its existing customer obligations or to<br>wind down its operations, the parties will enter into a transition services<br>agreement providing for (i) a defined scope of services and identified<br>personnel, (ii) a 20% cap on the time each individual may devote to such<br>services, (iii) a term of up to five (5) months, (iv) no fees during the five<br>(5) month term, (v) ownership by the recipient of work product created in<br>performing the services and (vi) customary liability limitations and<br>indemnities.  Any and all documented out-of-pocket costs incurred by<br>Disney will be billed at Disney’s cost and reimbursed promptly by the<br>Company.|
|**10. Regulatory Matters**|•<br>The parties do not expect the Proposed Transaction to require any antitrust<br>or other regulatory clearance or the expiration of any waiting period, and<br>the definitive agreements will include representations to that effect; the<br>parties will confirm this position in diligence, including with respect to any<br>non-U.S. filing obligations that may be triggered by the license of<br>intellectual property or the hiring of personnel.<br>•<br>From and after the closing of the Initial Transaction and the License<br>Transaction, respectively, the Company will use commercially reasonable<br>efforts to cooperate with Disney in connection with any governmental<br>inquiry, investigation or proceeding relating to the Proposed Transaction.<br>The Company will not participate in any meeting with any governmental<br>authority regarding the Proposed Transaction without Disney’s prior<br>consent, expect as required by applicable law.|
|**11. Communications**|•<br>All communications relating to the Proposed Transaction shall be directed<br>exclusively to the designated members of the Disney deal team<br>(collectively, the “**Disney Deal Team**”) and its legal, financial and other<br>professional advisors.  Disney shall designate the members of the Disney<br>Deal Team in writing to the Company.  The Company and its officers,<br>directors, employees, consultants, advisors, agents, affiliates (companies<br>which the Company controls), controlling persons and other representatives<br>shall not initiate or engage in communications relating to the Proposed<br>Transaction with any other employees, officers, directors or shareholders|

||of Disney without the prior written consent of the Disney Deal Team.<br>•<br>Likewise, all communications relating to the Proposed Transaction shall be<br>directed exclusively to the designated members of the Company deal team<br>(collectively, the “**Company Deal Team**”) and its legal, financial and other<br>professional advisors.  The Company shall designate the members of the<br>Company Deal Team in writing to Disney. Disney and its officers,<br>directors, employees, consultants, advisors, agents, affiliates (companies<br>which Disney controls), controlling persons and other representatives shall<br>not initiate or engage in communications relating to the Proposed<br>Transaction with any other employees, officers, directors or shareholders<br>of the Company without the prior written consent of the Company Deal<br>Team.|
|---|---|
|**12. Fiduciary Out**|•<br>Nothing in this Term Sheet, nor any obligations of the Company hereunder<br>or in connection with the Initial Transaction or the License Transaction,<br>shall restrict or limit the abilities of the Company’s directors and officers to<br>comply with their applicable fiduciary duties.|
|**13. Governing Law**|•<br>This Term Sheet and any action or proceeding arising under this Term Sheet<br>shall be governed by, and construed in accordance with, the governing law<br>provisions set forth in the Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement.|
