# **<u>Exhibit B</u>** 

# **Form of Bidding Procedures Order**

# **IN THE UNITED STATES BANKRUPTCY COURT FOR THE DISTRICT OF DELAWARE** 

In re: Chapter 11 CHARACTER TECHNOLOGIES, INC.,<sup>1</sup> Case No. 26-11603 ([___]) Debtor. 

**ORDER (A) APPROVING BIDDING PROCEDURES AND BID PROTECTIONS, (B) SCHEDULING CERTAIN DATES AND DEADLINES WITH RESPECT THERETO, (C) APPROVING THE FORM AND MANNER OF NOTICE THEREOF, (D) APPROVING THE DESIGNATION OF DISNEY WORLDWIDE SERVICES, INC. AS STALKING HORSE PURCHASER WITH RESPECT TO THE DISNEY TARGET ASSETS, (E) AUTHORIZING THE DEBTOR TO DESIGNATE ADDITIONAL STALKING HORSE PURCHASERS FOR THE ASSETS; AND (F) ESTABLISHING NOTICE AND PROCEDURES FOR THE ASSUMPTION** **<u>AND ASSIGNMENT OF CONTRACTS AND LEASES</u>** 

Upon consideration of the motion (the “Motion”)<sup>2</sup> of the above-captioned debtor and debtor in possession (the “Debtor”) for, _inter alia_ , the entry of an order (a) authorizing and approving the bidding procedures (the “Bidding Procedures”) attached hereto as **<u>Exhibit 1</u>** for the sale, license or other disposition (the “Sale”) of all or substantially all of the Debtor’s assets (the “Assets”), or any subset thereof;  (b) authorizing and approving certain bid protections (the “Bid Protections”), including a break-up fee and expense reimbursement of $700,000 (together, the “Break-Up Fee”) for Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. (“Disney”), the stalking horse purchaser with respect to the Assets (the “Disney Target Assets”) included in that certain Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement dated as of October __, 2026 attached to the Motion as <u>Exhibit B</u> (the “Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement”); (c) authorizing the Debtor to designate additional 

> 1 The Debtor and the last four digits of its taxpayer identification number are: Character Technologies, Inc. (8842). The Debtor’s mailing address for purposes of the chapter 11 case is 700 El Camino Real, Suite 120, P.O. Box 1152, Menlo Park, CA 94025. 

> 2 Capitalized terms used but not defined herein shall have the meanings given to such terms in the Motion.

stalking horse purchasers (each, an “Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser” and together with Disney, the “Stalking Horse Purchasers”) and additional stalking horse agreements (each, an “Additional Stalking Horse Agreement”), with respect to all or any subset of the Assets, and to offer the Bid Protections to any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser; (d) scheduling a hearing (the “Sale Hearing”) for approval of the Sale and approval of other deadlines related to the same; (e) authorizing the assumption and assignment of certain executory contracts and unexpired leases (the “Assigned Contracts”) in connection therewith; (f) authorizing and approving certain assumption and assignment procedures for the Assigned Contracts provided for herein (collectively, the “Assumption and Assignment Procedures”); and (g) granting related relief; and due and proper notice of the Motion having been given; and it appearing that no other or further notice of the Motion is required except as set forth herein; and it appearing that the Court has jurisdiction to consider the Motion in accordance with 28 U.S.C. §§ 157 and 1334 and the _Amended Standing Order of Reference_ from the United States District Court for the District of Delaware dated February 29, 2012; and it appearing that this is a core proceeding pursuant to 28 U.S.C. § 157(b); and it appearing that venue of this proceeding and the Motion is proper in this district pursuant to 28 U.S.C. §§ 1408 and 1409; and the Court having reviewed the Motion; and a hearing on the Motion having been held; and the Court having found and determined that the relief set forth herein is in the best interests of the Debtor, its estate and creditors and all parties in interest, and that the legal and factual bases set forth in the Motion and at the hearing on the Motion establish just cause for the relief granted herein; and after due deliberation and sufficient cause appearing therefor, it is hereby

# **FOUND AND DETERMINED THAT:**<sup>**3**</sup> 

A. This Court has jurisdiction to consider the Motion in accordance with 28 U.S.C. §§ 157 and 1334 and the Amended Standing Order of Reference. 

B. Venue of this proceeding and the Motion is proper pursuant to 28 U.S.C. §§ 1408 and 1409. This is a core proceeding pursuant to 28 U.S.C. § 157(b). 

C. The statutory and legal predicates for the relief requested in the Motion and provided for herein are sections 105(a), 363, 365, 503 and 507 of title 11 of the United States Code, as amended (the “Bankruptcy Code”), Rules 2002, 6004, 6006, 9007 and 9014 of the Federal Rules of Bankruptcy Procedure (the “Bankruptcy Rules”), and Rule 6004-1 of the Local Rules of the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “Local Rules”). 

D. The Debtor has demonstrated that good and sufficient notice of the relief granted by this Order has been given and no further notice is required, except as otherwise provided for herein. A reasonable opportunity to object or to be heard regarding the relief granted by this Order has been afforded to those parties entitled to notice pursuant to Bankruptcy Rule 2002 and all other interested parties. 

E. The Transaction Notice (as defined below), substantially in the form attached hereto as **<u>Exhibit 2</u>** <u>, is appropriate and reasonably calculated to provide all interested parties with</u> timely and proper notice of this Order, the Bidding Procedures, the auction for the Assets (the “Auction”), the Sale, and the Sale Hearing, and any and all objection deadlines related thereto, and no other or further notice of the foregoing is required. 

> 3 The findings and conclusions set forth herein constitute the Court’s findings of fact and conclusions of law pursuant to Bankruptcy Rule 7052, made applicable to this proceeding pursuant to Bankruptcy Rule 9014. To the extent that any of the following findings of fact constitute conclusions of law, they are adopted as such. To the extent any of the following conclusions of law constitute findings of fact, they are adopted as such.

F. The Bidding Procedures are: (a) fair, reasonable, and appropriate; (b) negotiated in good faith and at arm’s length; and (c) designed to promote a competitive bidding process, so as to maximize recovery with respect to the Sale. 

G. The Debtor has demonstrated a compelling and sound business justification for the Court to approve the Bid Protections, including payment of the Break-Up Fee, if necessary, to Disney in accordance with the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement and, as applicable, to any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser in accordance with the terms of the applicable Additional Stalking Horse Agreement. The Bid Protections approved herein are fair, reasonable, and provide a benefit to the Debtor’s estate and stakeholders. If triggered in accordance with the terms of the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor, payment of the Break-Up Fee, under this Order and upon the conditions set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, or any other Additional Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor, and the Bidding Procedures are (a) an actual and necessary cost and expense of preserving the Debtor’s estate within the meaning of sections 503(b) and 507(a) of the Bankruptcy Code, (b) reasonably tailored to encourage, rather than hamper, bidding for the applicable Assets by providing a baseline of value, increasing the likelihood of competitive bidding at the Auction, and facilitating participation of potential bidders in the Sale process, thereby increasing the likelihood that the Debtor will receive the best possible price and terms for the Assets, (c) of substantial benefit to the Debtor’s estate and stakeholders, (d) reasonable and appropriate, (e) a material inducement for, and conditions necessary to, ensure that Disney and any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor will continue pursuit of the proposed agreement to purchase the Assets in accordance with the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement, and (f) reasonable in relation to the lost opportunities resulting from the time and

money spent pursuing such transaction of Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor. 

H. The Assumption and Assignment Procedures provided for herein and the Assumption Notice (as defined below), in substantially the form attached hereto as **<u>Exhibit 3</u>** <u>, are</u> reasonable and appropriate and consistent with the provisions of section 365 of the Bankruptcy Code and Bankruptcy Rule 6006. The Assumption and Assignment Procedures and the Assumption and Assignment Notice have been tailored to provide an adequate opportunity for all Counterparties (as defined below) to assert any Contract Objections (as defined below). 

I. Entry of this Order is in the best interests of the Debtor, its estate, its creditors, and all other interested parties. 

# **NOW, THEREFORE, IT IS HEREBY ORDERED THAT:** 

1. <mark>The portions of the Motion seeking approval of (a) the Debtor’s entry into the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement (subject to higher or otherwise better offers in accordance with this Order) and all of its terms, including the Bid Protections, (b) the Debtor’s ability to designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers and provide Bid Protections to such Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers, (c) the Assumption and Assignment Procedures, (d) the Bidding Procedures, (e) the date and time of the Sale Hearing, and (f) the noticing and objection procedures related to each of the foregoing, including, without limitation, the notice of the Sale and the entry of this Order, substantially in the form attached hereto as</mark> **<u><mark>Exhibit 2</mark></u>** <mark>(the “Transaction Notice”), and the notice of the Debtor’s potential assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts, substantially in the form attached hereto as</mark> **<u><mark>Exhibit 3</mark></u>** <mark>(the “Assumption and Assignment Notice”) (subclauses (a) – (f) above, collectively, the “Bidding and Auction Process”) are hereby GRANTED to the extent set forth herein.</mark>

2. <mark>Any objections to the Motion as it pertains to the Bidding and Auction Process or the relief granted by this Order that have not been withdrawn, waived, or settled, and all reservations of rights included therein, are hereby overruled and denied on the merits with prejudice.</mark> 

3. <mark>The Debtor is authorized to (a) designate Disney as the Stalking Horse Purchaser with respect to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, and (b) grant the Bid Protections to Disney and such designation and grant are hereby approved.</mark> 

4. <mark>The Debtor is further authorized to (a) designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers with respect to all or any subset of the Assets, other than the Assets proposed to be acquired by Disney, (b) to enter into a form of Additional Stalking Horse Agreement with such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser(s), subject to final Court approval at the Sale Hearing, and (c) to grant the Bid Protections to such Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers.</mark> 

5. <mark>The Bidding Procedures are hereby approved. The failure to specifically include or reference any particular provision of the Bidding Procedures in the Motion or this Order shall not diminish or otherwise impair the effectiveness of such procedures, it being this Court’s intent that the Bidding Procedures are approved in their entirety, as if fully set forth in this Order. The Debtor is hereby authorized to conduct the Auction pursuant to the terms of the Bidding Procedures and this Order.</mark> 

6. <mark>For all purposes under the Bidding Procedures: (a) Disney shall be considered a Qualifying Bidder and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement shall be considered a Qualifying Bid; (b) Disney is, and will be deemed to be a Qualifying Bidder for all purposes under the Bidding Procedures, without regard to any of the requirements or conditions set forth herein and without any other or further action by Disney; and (c) in determining whether the Potential Bidders</mark>

<mark>constitute Qualifying Bidders, the Debtor may consider a combination of bids for the Assets, including the Disney Target Assets.</mark> 

7. <mark>The Bidding Procedures shall apply to the Potential Bidders, the Qualifying Bidders,</mark> 

<mark>the submission, receipt, and analysis of all bids relating to the Sale, and the conduct of the Sale and the Auction.</mark> 

8. <mark>The Bid Protections set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement are approved, including, with respect to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, the Break-Up Fee comprised of a break-up fee and expense reimbursement equal to $700,000, or 3.5% of the cash purchase price.</mark> 

9. <mark>Further, if Disney is not selected as the Winning Bidder, consistent with the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, Disney will provide to one such Winning Bidder transition services reasonably comparable to those included in that certain form of transition services agreement, which is attached hereto as</mark> **<u><mark>Exhibit 4</mark></u>** <u><mark>,</mark></u> <mark>for a reasonable period of time no greater than the lesser of (a) 60 days following the closing of the Sale and (b) 75 days after conclusion of the Auction; provided, that, if there are multiple Winning Bidders, Disney shall only be required to provide such transition services to one Winning Bidder.</mark> 

10. <mark>No entity other than Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor in accordance with the Bidding Procedures shall be entitled to any expense reimbursement, break-up fee, “topping,” termination, contribution, or other similar fee or payment.</mark> 

11. <mark>The obligations of the Debtor to pay the Break-Up Fee: (a) shall be entitled to administrative expense status under sections 503(b) and 507(a)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code,</mark> _<mark>provided</mark>_ <mark>that the Break-Up Fee shall be payable directly out of proceeds, and as a precondition to, an Alternative Transaction (as defined in the Bidding Procedures); (b) shall survive the termination</mark>

<mark>of the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement; and (c) shall be payable in accordance with the terms set forth in the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement. Disney is not required to seek relief from the automatic stay to enforce any remedies available to Disney related to the Break-Up Fee under</mark> 

<mark>the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement.</mark> 

12. <mark>The following “Assumption and Assignment Procedures” are hereby approved:</mark> 

   - a. <mark>On or before November 4, 2026 (the “Assumption and Assignment Notice</mark> <u><mark>Deadline”), the Debtor shall file with the Court and serve on any non-Debtor</mark></u> <mark>counterparty (each, a “Counterparty,” and collectively, the “Counterparties”) to an Assigned Contract, an Assumption and Assignment Notice.</mark> 

   - b. <mark>Any Assumption and Assignment Notice shall include, without limitation, the cure amount (each, a “Cure Amount”), if any, that the Debtor believes is required to be paid to the applicable Counterparty under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code for each of the Assigned Contracts. If a Counterparty objects to (i) the assumption and assignment of the Counterparty’s Assigned Contract, (ii) the Cure Amount for its Assigned Contract or (iii) the provision of adequate assurance of future performance, the Counterparty must file with the Court and serve on the Objection Notice Parties (as defined below) a written objection (a “Contract</mark> <u><mark>Objection”) on or before the applicable objection deadline set forth in these</mark></u> <mark>Assumption and Assignment Procedures.</mark> 

   - c. <mark>Any Contract Objection shall: (i) be in writing, (ii) comply with the Bankruptcy Rules and the Local Rules; (iii) be filed with the Clerk of the Court, 824 N. Market Street, 3rd Floor, Wilmington, Delaware 19801 on or before</mark> **<u><mark>4:00 p.m. (ET) on November 18, 2026 (the “Contract Objection Deadline”)</mark></u>** <u><mark>, and proof of service</mark></u> <mark>of such Contract Objection upon the Objection Notice Parties shall be filed with the Court as and when required under the Local Rules; (iv) be served upon the Objection Notice Parties; and (v) state with specificity the grounds for such objection, including, without limitation, the fully liquidated cure amount and the legal and factual bases for any unliquidated cure amount that the Counterparty believes is required to be paid under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code for the Assigned Contract, along with the specific nature and dates of any alleged defaults, the pecuniary losses, if any, resulting therefrom, and the conditions giving rise thereto. Any objections to adequate assurance of future performance by Disney shall be filed by the Contract Objection Deadline. Any objections to adequate assurance of future performance by a Winning Bidder other than Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor shall be filed in accordance with subparagraph (g) below.</mark> 

   - d. <mark>The “Objection Notice Parties” are as follows: (i) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., 222 Delaware Avenue, Suite 800, Wilmington, Delaware 19801 (Attn: Erin R. Fay (efay@wsgr.com) and Shane M. Reil (sreil@wsgr.com); (ii) proposed investment banker to the Debtor, Houlihan</mark>

<mark>Lokey (Attn: Daniel Cullins (email: dcullins@hl.com)); (iii) counsel to Disney, Skadden, Arps, Slate, Meagher & Flom LLP, One Manhattan West, New York, New York 10001 (Attn: Brandon Van Dyke (brandon.vandyke@skadden.com), Ron E. Meisler (ron.meisler@skadden.com) Bryan Uelk (bryan.uelk@skadden.com), and Samuel J. Cammer (samuel.cammer@skadden.com)); (iv) counsel to any official committee of unsecured creditors appointed in the chapter 11 case; and (v) counsel to the Office of the United States Trustee for the District of Delaware, 844 N. King Street, Suite 2207, Lockbox 35, Wilmington, Delaware 19801 (Attn: Linda J. Casey (linda.casey@usdoj.gov)).</mark> 

- e. <mark>As soon as practicable after the completion of the Auction, the Debtor shall file with the Court a notice identifying the bidder(s) selected as the winning bidder(s) with respect to all or any subset of the Assets (each, a “Winning Bidder” and, if more than one, the “Winning Bidders”) (a “Notice of Winning Bidder”), which shall set forth, among other things, (i) the Winning Bidder and Back-Up Bidder (if any) with respect to all or any of the Assets and the amount of each of the Winning Bid and the Back-Up Bid (if any) with respect to all or any of the Assets; and (ii) the Selected Assigned Contracts,</mark> _<mark>provided, however,</mark>_ <mark>that if Disney is serving as the Back-Up Bidder, the protections that apply to Disney’s Back-Up Bid as set forth in this Section 14 of the Bidding Procedures shall apply.</mark> 

- f. <mark>As soon as reasonably practicable after completion of the Auction, the Debtor will cause to be served by electronic mail, and if unavailable, overnight mail, upon each affected Counterparty and its counsel (if known) the Notice of Winning Bidder.</mark> 

- g. <mark>At the Sale Hearing, the Debtor will seek Court approval of the assumption and assignment to Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser, or in the event that a Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then to the Winning Bidder(s), of only those Assigned Contracts that have been selected by Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser, or, in the event that any Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then the Winning Bidder, to be assumed and assigned (each, a “Selected Assigned Contract,” and collectively, the “Selected Assigned Contracts”). The Debtor and its estate reserve any and all rights with respect to any Assigned Contracts that are not ultimately designated as Selected Assigned Contracts.</mark> 

- h. <mark>If no Contract Objection is timely received with respect to a Selected Assigned Contract: (i) the Counterparty to such Selected Assigned Contract shall be deemed to have consented to the assumption by the Debtor and assignment to the applicable Winning Bidder of the Selected Assigned Contract, and be forever barred from asserting any objection with respect to such assumption and assignment (including, without limitation, with respect to adequate assurance of future performance); (ii) any and all defaults under the Selected Assigned Contract and any and all pecuniary losses related thereto shall be deemed cured and compensated pursuant to section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code; and (iii) the Cure Amount for such Selected Assigned Contract shall be controlling, notwithstanding anything to the contrary in such Selected Assigned Contract, or any other related document, and the Counterparty shall be deemed to have consented to the Cure Amount and shall</mark>

<mark>be forever barred from asserting any other claims related to such Selected Assigned Contract against the Debtor and its estate or the applicable Winning Bidder for such Selected Assigned Contract, or the property of any of them that existed prior to the entry of the Sale Order.</mark> 

- i. <mark>To the extent that the parties are unable to consensually resolve any Contract Objection prior to the commencement of the Sale Hearing, including, without limitation, any dispute with respect to the cure amount required to be paid to the applicable Counterparty under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code (any such dispute, a “Cure Dispute”), such Contract Objection will be adjudicated at the Sale Hearing or at such other date and time as may be determined by the Debtor, in consultation with the Winning Bidder, or fixed by the Court; provided, however, that if the Contract Objection relates solely to a Cure Dispute, the Selected Assigned Contract may be assumed by the Debtor and assigned to the Winning Bidder  for such Selected Assigned Contract, provided that the cure amount that the Counterparty asserts is required to be paid under section 365(b)(1)(A) and (B) of the Bankruptcy Code (or such lower amount as agreed to by the Counterparty) is deposited in a segregated account by the Debtor or the Winning Bidder for such Selected Assigned Contract, pending the Court’s adjudication of the Cure Dispute or the parties’ consensual resolution of the Cure Dispute.</mark> 

- j. <mark>Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, if after the Sale Hearing or the entry of the Sale Order additional executory contracts or unexpired leases of the Debtor are determined to be Assigned Contracts, as soon as practicable thereafter, the Debtor shall file with the Court and serve, by regular mail, on the Counterparties an Assumption and Assignment Notice, and such Counterparties shall be required to file any Contract Objections not later than fourteen (14) days thereafter. If no Contract Objection is timely received, the Debtor shall be authorized to assume and assign such Assigned Contracts to the applicable Winning Bidder without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest and without the need for further order of the Court, and such assumption and assignment shall be subject to the terms of the Sale Order.</mark> 

13. <mark>The Debtor’s decision to assume and assign any Assigned Contracts is subject to this Court’s approval and the closing of the applicable Sale(s). Accordingly, absent this Court’s approval and the closing of the Sale(s), the Assigned Contracts shall not be deemed assumed or assumed and assigned, and shall in all respects be subject to further administration by the Debtor and its estate under the Bankruptcy Code in connection with the chapter 11 case.</mark> 

14. <mark>The Assumption and Assignment Procedures are appropriate and fair to all</mark> 

<mark>Counterparties and comply in all respects with the Bankruptcy Code, the Bankruptcy Rules, and</mark>

<mark>the Local Rules. The Assumption and Assignment Notice is: (a) reasonably calculated to (i) provide sufficient, effective notice to all Counterparties and any other affected parties of the Debtor’s intent to assume and assign to the Winning Bidder, some or all of the Assigned Contracts, and (ii) afford the Counterparties the opportunity to exercise any rights affected by the Motion and the relief granted by this Order pursuant to Bankruptcy Rules 2002(a)(2), 6004 and 6006; and (b) hereby approved.</mark> 

15. <mark>The inclusion of a contract, lease or other agreement on an Assumption Notice shall not constitute or be deemed a determination or admission by the Debtor and its estate or any other party in interest that such contract, lease or other agreement is, in fact, an executory contract or unexpired lease within the meaning of the Bankruptcy Code, and any and all rights of the Debtor and its estate with respect thereto shall be reserved.</mark> 

16. <mark>The Transaction Notice, the Assumption and Assignment Notice, the Bidding Procedures, the Auction, the Sale Hearing, the Assumption and Assignment Procedures and the objection periods associated with each of the foregoing are reasonably calculated to provide notice to any affected party and afford the affected party the opportunity to exercise any rights affected by the Motion as it relates the Bidding Procedures, the Auction, the Sale, the Sale Hearing, and the assumption and assignment to the Winning Bidder or Winning Bidders of the Assigned Contracts pursuant to Bankruptcy Rules 2002(a)(2), 6004 and 6006, and such notice and objection periods are hereby approved.</mark> 

17. <mark>The Transaction Notice is approved. Within two (2) business days after entry of this Order, the Debtor shall serve the Transaction Notice by regular mail on: (a) the Office of the United States Trustee for the District of Delaware; (b) counsel to Disney; (c) counsel to any official committee of unsecured creditors appointed in the chapter 11 case; (d) all parties known by the</mark>

<mark>Debtor to assert a lien or encumbrance on any of the Assets; (e) all persons known or reasonably believed to have asserted an interest in or claim to any of the Assets; (f) all persons known or reasonably believed to have expressed an interest in acquiring all or a substantial portion of the Assets within the one (1) year prior to the Petition Date; (g) the Office of the United States Attorney for the District of Delaware; (h) the Office of the Attorney General in each state in which the Debtor has operated; (i) the Office of the Secretary of State in each state in which the Debtor has operated; (j) the Internal Revenue Service and all state and local taxing authorities in the states in which the Debtor has or may have any tax liability; (k) the Federal Trade Commission; (l) the United States Attorney General/Antitrust Division of Department of Justice; (m) all non-Debtor parties to any of the Assigned Contracts; (n) all of the Debtor’s other known creditors and equity security holders; and (o) all other parties that have filed a notice of appearance and demand for service of papers in this chapter 11 case as of the service date (collectively, the “Transaction Notice</mark> <u><mark>Parties”).</mark></u> 

18. <mark>The Debtor shall post the Transaction Notice, the Assumption and Assignment Notice, and this Order, on the website of the Debtor’s claims and noticing agent, Stretto, Inc. (“Stretto”) at https://cases.stretto.com/chartech. With respect to parties whose identities or addresses are not reasonably ascertained by the Debtor, publication of the Transaction Notice in</mark> _<mark>USA Today</mark>_ <mark>within seven (7) business days of the entry of this Order, or as soon as reasonably practicable thereafter, and shall also post the Transaction Notice on the Debtor’s blog and online community webpages (which are the online locations through which the Debtor regularly communicates with the public).  Publication of the Transaction Notice as described in this Order conforms to the requirements of Bankruptcy Rules 2002(l) and 9008, and is reasonably calculated to provide notice to any affected party, including any Potential Bidders; and afford the affected</mark>

<mark>party the opportunity to exercise any rights affected by the Motion and the relief granted by the Order.</mark> 

19. <mark>Any objections to the Sale or to the relief requested in connection with the Sale (a “Sale Objection”), other than a Contract Objection, which shall be governed by the Assumption and Assignment Procedures, must: (a) be in writing; (b) comply with the Bankruptcy Rules and the Local Rules; (c) set forth the specific basis for the Sale Objection; (d) be filed with the Clerk of this Court, 824 N. Market Street, 3rd Floor, Wilmington, Delaware 19801</mark> **<u><mark>on or before 4:00 p.m. (ET) on November 13, 2026</mark></u>** <mark>(</mark> **<mark>the “Sale Objection Deadline</mark>** <mark>”), and proof of service of such Sale Objection upon the Objection Notice Parties shall be filed with the Court as and when required by the Local Rules; and (e) be served upon the Objection Notice Parties. If a Sale Objection is not filed and served on or before the Sale Objection Deadline in accordance with the foregoing requirements, the objecting party may be barred from objecting to the Sale and being heard at the Sale Hearing, and this Court may enter the Sale Order without further notice to such party.</mark> 

20. <mark>Failure to file a Sale Objection on or before the Sale Objection Deadline (a) shall forever bar the assertion, whether at any Sale Hearing or thereafter, of any objection to the Motion, to the entry of the Sale Order, and/or to the consummation and performance of the Sale contemplated by the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any other Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement with a Winning Bidder other than any Stalking Horse Purchaser, and (b) for purposes of section 363(f)(2) of the Bankruptcy Code, shall be deemed to be “consent” to the entry of the Sale Order and consummation of the Sale and all transactions related thereto.</mark> 

21. <mark>As part of its bid, each Qualifying Bidder must provide to the Debtor and the other Bidding Procedures Notice Parties information supporting the Qualifying Bidder’s ability to</mark>

<mark>comply with the requirements of adequate assurance of future performance under section 365(f)(2)(B) and, if applicable, section 365(b)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code (the “Adequate</mark> <u><mark>Assurance Information”), including (a) the Qualifying Bidder’s financial wherewithal and</mark></u> <mark>willingness to perform under any contracts and leases that are assumed and assigned to such Qualifying Bidder, which information may include (i) financial statements and (ii) annual reports; and (b) a contact person for the proposed assignee that the applicable Counterparty may directly contact in connection with the adequate assurance of future performance. To the extent that a Qualifying Bidder is a newly formed acquisition entity or the like, the financial and other information supporting the Qualifying Bidder’s financial wherewithal shall include financial or other information supporting the financial wherewithal of the Qualifying Bidder’s parent company or sponsor.</mark> 

22. <mark>The Deposit of each Qualifying Bidder (including the Deposit Disney has already paid in accordance with that certain Exclusivity and Prepayment Agreement, dated September 18, 2026, between and among the Debtor and Disney, and any Deposit provided by any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser) shall be held in a separate and designated account by the Debtor or its agent, and shall not become part of the Debtor’s bankruptcy estate unless and until released from escrow to the Debtors pursuant to the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement or by order of this Court.</mark> 

23. <mark>Any party in receipt of Adequate Assurance Information under this Order shall review the Adequate Assurance Information received on a confidential basis and shall not disclose the Adequate Assurance Information except as previously provided in this Order and the Bidding Procedures. Such counterparty may not use or disclose, except to representatives, attorneys, advisors, and financing sources (collectively, “Representatives”), any confidential Adequate</mark>

<mark>Assurance Information for any purpose other than: (a) evaluating whether adequate assurance of future performance as required under section 365(f)(2)(B) and, if applicable, 365(b)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code has been provided; and (b) in support of any objection (the “Assignment</mark> <u><mark>Objection”) (subject to the limitations on disclosure set forth herein) by such Counterparty relating</mark></u> <mark>to adequate assurance of future performance. Any Assignment Objection that includes confidential, non-public Adequate Assurance Information must be filed under seal unless disclosure of such confidential, non-public information is authorized by the Debtor and the applicable assignee. The party filing an Assignment Objection under seal shall follow the procedures for the same set forth in Local Rule 9018-1(d). The unredacted versions of such Assignment Objections shall be served upon the Debtor, the other Bidding Procedures Notice Parties, and the U.S. Trustee, with a copy to the Court’s chambers; provided, further, that all rights of all parties in interest in the chapter 11 case are reserved to oppose the filing under seal of any such information and to seek any other relief from this Court with respect to such matter. Any Representative receiving Adequate Assurance Information shall be notified and shall agree to be bound by the restrictions set forth in this Order.</mark> 

24. <mark>If no timely Qualifying Bids other than the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement</mark> 

<mark>for the Disney Target Assets or any other Additional Stalking Horse Agreement for all or substantially all of the Assets or any subset thereof, are submitted on or before the Bid Deadline, the Debtor shall not hold the Auction and shall request at the Sale Hearing that this Court approve the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement and the transactions contemplated thereunder. If the Debtor timely receives one or more Qualifying Bids for competing sets of the Assets, including, without limitation, the Disney Target Assets, then the Debtor shall conduct the Auction on November 23, 2026 at 10:00 a.m. (ET) at a location to be determined by the Debtor or virtually by</mark>

<mark>videoconference pursuant to information to be timely provided to the Auction Participants (as defined below). Only the Debtor, the Consultation Parties, the Auction Bidders, and any creditors or parties in interest who submit a request to attend the Auction in accordance with the Bidding Procedures (collectively, the “Auction Participants”).</mark> 

25. <mark>Following service of a notice of Auction results, parties may raise objections solely with respect to the conduct of the Auction, the Winning Bidder (only if not a Stalking Horse Purchaser), modifications to any Stalking Horse Agreement, and adequate assurance of future performance by a Winning Bidder other than any Stalking Horse Purchaser (each such objection, a “Supplemental Sale Objection”) at the Sale Hearing.</mark> 

26. <mark>The Debtor shall have until November 23, 2026 at Noon (ET) to file and serve a reply to any objection filed in connection with the Sale, including any Sale Objection or Contract Objection.</mark> 

27. <mark>The Sale Hearing shall be held before this Court on November 24, 2026 at [●] (ET), unless otherwise determined by the Court.</mark> 

28. <mark>The Sale Hearing and any other dates related thereto, including any dates set forth in the Bidding Procedures, may be adjourned by the Debtor in consultation with the Consultation</mark> 

<mark>Parties and the Winning Bidder(s), from time to time without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest other than by announcement of the adjournment in open court on the date scheduled for the Sale Hearing, or by filing a hearing agenda or notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case.</mark> 

29. <mark>In the event there is a conflict between this Order or the Bidding Procedures, on the one hand, and the Motion, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, an Alternative Agreement, or any notice served in connection with the Motion</mark>

<mark>or this Order on the other hand, this Order and the Bidding Procedures shall control and govern. If there is a conflict between this Order and the Bidding Procedures, this Order shall control and govern.</mark> 

30. <mark>Prior to mailing the Assumption and Assignment Notice and the Transaction Notice, as applicable, the Debtor, in consultation with Disney and any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor, may fill in, or cause to be filled in, any missing dates or other information, correct any typographical errors, conform the provisions thereof to the provisions of this Order, and make other non-material changes as the Debtor deems necessary or appropriate.</mark> 

31. <mark>Nothing in this Order or the Bidding Procedures will require any director or officer of the Debtor to take any action, or to refrain from taking any action, to the extent such director or officer reasonably determines in good faith, in consultation with outside counsel, that taking such action, or refraining from taking such action, as applicable, would result in a violation of applicable law or be inconsistent with his or her fiduciary obligations.</mark> 

32. <mark>This Order shall be immediately effective upon entry, and any stay of orders provided for in Bankruptcy Rules 6004(h) or 6006(d) or any other provision of the Bankruptcy Code, the Bankruptcy Rules, or the Local Rules is expressly waived. The Debtor is not subject to any stay in the implementation, enforcement or realization of the relief granted in this Order and may, in its sole discretion and without further delay, take any action and perform any act authorized or approved under this Order.</mark> 

33. <mark>The requirements set forth in Local Rules 6004-1, 9006-1, and 9013-1 are hereby satisfied or waived.</mark> 

34. <mark>Attached hereto as</mark> **<u><mark>Schedule A</mark></u>** <mark>is a summary of the key dates established by this</mark> 

<mark>Order.</mark>

35. <mark>This Court shall retain exclusive jurisdiction with respect to all matters arising from</mark> 

<mark>or related to the implementation or interpretation of this Order.</mark>

# **<u>BIDDING PROCEDURES</u>** 

On October 12, 2026, the above-captioned debtor and debtor in possession (the “Debtor”) filed a voluntary petition for relief under chapter 11 of title 11 of the United States Code, as amended (the “Bankruptcy Code”).  The Debtor is maintaining its business and managing its property as a debtor in possession pursuant to section 1107(a) and 1108 of the Bankruptcy Code. 

On [●], 2026, the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware (the “Court”) entered an order [Docket No. [●]] (the “Bidding Procedures Order”), among other things, granting certain relief requested in the related motion [Docket No. [●]] (the “Bidding Procedures Motion”), including authorizing the Debtor to solicit bids and approving the procedures set forth herein (collectively, the “Bidding Procedures”) to be employed by the Debtor in connection with the proposed sale, license or other disposition (the “Sale”) of all or substantially all of the Debtor’s assets (the “Assets”) free and clear of all Encumbrances other than Assumed Liabilities,<sup>2</sup> including the Sale of certain of the Assets (the “Disney Target Assets”) to Disney Worldwide Services, Inc. ( “Disney”), pursuant to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement dated as of October __, 2026 (the “Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement”) between Disney and the Debtor, or any additional stalking horse agreement (each, an “Additional Stalking Horse Agreement”) between the Debtor and any additional Stalking Horse Purchaser (an “Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser”) designated by the Debtor or, in the event that any Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then an Alternative Agreement (as defined below) with the Winning Bidder(s). The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement is attached to the Bidding Procedures Motion as <u>Exhibit B.</u>

# **1.** **<u>The Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement</u>** 

On October __, 2026, the Debtor entered into the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement with Disney.  As set forth more fully in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, the price to be paid by Disney consists of $20 million in cash (the “Stalking Horse Purchase Price”). Disney is deemed to be a Qualifying Bidder (as defined below), and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement is deemed to be a Qualifying Bid (as defined below). 

> 3 The Sale Objection Deadline and Contract Objection Deadlines apply to all objections to the Sale of the Assets and the assumption and assignment of the Assigned Contracts (including adequate assurance of future performance by any Stalking Horse Purchaser) with the exception of objections related to adequate assurance of future performance by a Winning Bidder _other than_ any Stalking Horse Purchaser, which shall be due at or prior to the Sale Hearing.

# **2.** **<u>Authorization to Designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers</u>** 

These Bidding Procedures authorize but do not require the Debtor to designate Additional Stalking Horse Purchasers with respect to all or any subset of the Assets, other than the Assets proposed to be acquired by Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement entered into with such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser shall be deemed to be a Qualifying Bid (as defined below) and such Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser shall be deemed to be a Qualifying Bidder (as defined below) for all purposes under these Bidding Procedures. 

# **3.** **<u>Assets for Sale</u>** 

The Debtor shall offer for Sale all or substantially all of the Assets or any subset thereof, including the Disney Target Assets, _provided_ that the Debtor determines that the aggregate consideration offered by any bid, or combination of bids, for the Assets, satisfies the requirements set forth in these Bidding Procedures.  Potential Bidders may bid on all or any number or combination of the Assets. 

# **4.** **<u>Participation Requirements</u>** 

Any interested party that wishes to participate in the bidding process for the Assets (each, a “Potential Bidder”) must first become a “Qualifying Bidder.” To become a Qualifying Bidder, and thus be able to conduct due diligence and gain access to the Debtor’s confidential electronic data room concerning the assets (the “Data Room”), a Potential Bidder must submit to the Debtor and its advisors: 

- (a) documentation identifying the Potential Bidder, its principals, and the representatives thereof who are authorized to appear and act on their behalf for all purposes regarding the contemplated bidding and transaction process; 

- (b) an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance satisfactory to the Debtor; and 

- (c) sufficient information, as reasonably determined by the Debtor, to allow the Debtor to determine, in its reasonable business judgment, that the interested party (i) has the financial wherewithal to consummate a Sale, and (ii) intends to access the Data Room for a _bona fide_ purpose consistent with these Bidding Procedures; 

Each Potential Bidder shall comply with all reasonable requests for information and due diligence access by the Debtor or its advisors regarding the ability of such Potential Bidder to consummate its contemplated transaction. 

# **5.** **<u>Bankruptcy Court Jurisdiction</u>** 

Any Potential Bidders and Qualifying Bidders shall: (a) be deemed to have waived any right to a jury trial in connection with, and consented and submitted to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court over, any actions or proceedings arising from or relating the Bidding Procedures, the Sale, the Auction and the construction and enforcement of the contemplated transaction documents

of such parties; (b) bring any such action or proceeding in the Court; and (c) be deemed to have consented to the Court entering a final judgment determining any such action or proceeding and that such final judgment in any such action or proceeding, including all appeals, shall be conclusive and may be enforced in other jurisdictions (including any foreign jurisdictions) by suit on the judgment or in any other manner provided by applicable law. 

# **6.** **<u>Form of Agreement for Sale of the Assets; Including the Transferred Property</u>** 

Potential Bidders should reference the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement. As set forth below, Potential Bidders intending to submit bids that include the Disney Target Assetes, or any combination of Assets must include with their bids: 

- (a) a statement that such Potential Bidder offers to (i) purchase or license the Disney Target Assets, or a number or combination of the Assets, and (ii) assume liabilities, upon substantially the same terms as, or terms more favorable to the Debtor and its estate than, as applicable, the terms set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; and 

- (b) a clean and duly executed transaction agreement (an “Alternative Agreement”) and a marked copy of such agreement showing variations from the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement. 

# **7.** **<u>Due Diligence</u>** 

The Debtor will provide any Qualifying Bidder (including, for the avoidance of doubt), Disney with reasonable access to the Data Room and any other additional information that the Debtor believes to be reasonable and appropriate under the circumstances.  All additional due diligence requests shall be directed to: (a) proposed investment banker for the Debtor, Houlihan Lokey, Attn: Daniel Cullins (214-220-8473); (dcullins@hl.com) and (b) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., Attn: Heather Lambert (hlambert@wsgr.com). 

The due diligence period shall extend through and including the Bid Deadline.  The Debtor, in its business judgment, may, but shall not be obligated to, furnish any due diligence information after the Bid Deadline. 

The Debtor reserves the right, in its reasonable discretion, to withhold or limit access to any due diligence information that the Debtor determines is business-sensitive or otherwise not appropriate for disclosure to a Qualifying Bidder. Notwithstanding any prepetition limitations, including, without limitation, any non-disclosure, confidentiality or similar provisions relating to any due diligence information, the Debtor and its estate shall be authorized to provide due diligence information to Qualifying Bidders _provided_ that such Qualifying Bidders have delivered an executed confidentiality agreement in form and substance acceptable to the Debtor.

# **8.** **<u>Bid Requirements</u>** 

Other than in the case of Disney and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser and its Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, which shall be considered a Qualifying Bidder and a Qualifying Bid, respectively, for all purposes under these Bidding Procedures, without regard to any of the requirements or conditions set forth herein and without any other or further action by the Stalking Horse Purchaser, to be deemed a “Qualifying <u>Bid,” a bid must be received from a Qualifying Bidder on or before the Bid Deadline and satisfy</u> each of the following requirements (each, a “Bid Requirement”): 

- a. be in writing; 

- b. fully disclose the identity of the Qualifying Bidder (and to the extent that the Qualifying Bidder is a newly formed acquisition entity or the like, the identity of the Qualifying Bidder’s parent company or sponsor), and provide the contact information of the specific person(s) whom the Debtor or its advisors should contact in the event that the Debtor has any questions or wishes to discuss the bid submitted by the Qualifying Bidder; 

- c. set forth the purchase price to be paid by such Qualifying Bidder; 

- d. not propose payment in any form other than cash; 

- e. state the liabilities proposed to be paid or assumed by such Qualifying Bidder; 

- f. specify the Assets that are included in the bid and state that such Qualifying Bidder offers to (i) purchase or license the Assets, or a number or combination of the Assets, and (ii) assume liabilities, upon substantially the same terms as, or terms more favorable to the Debtor and its estate than, the terms set forth in, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- g. be accompanied by an alternative asset purchase agreement (an “Alternative Agreement”) that reflects any variations from, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any other Additional Stalking Horse Agreement that has been designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets included in the bid; 

- h. state that such Qualifying Bidder’s offer is formal, binding, and unconditional, and is irrevocable until two (2) business days after the closing of the Sale; 

- i. to the extent that a Bid is not accompanied by evidence of the Qualifying Bidder’s capacity to consummate the sale, license or

other disposition set forth in its Bid with cash on hand, each Bid must include unconditional committed financing from a reputable financing institution, documented to the satisfaction of the Debtor, that demonstrates that the Qualifying Bidder has: (i) received sufficient debt and/or equity funding commitments to satisfy the Qualifying Bidder’s purchase price and other obligations under its Bid; and (ii) adequate working capital financing or resources to finance going concern operations for the Assets and the proposed transactions. Such funding commitments or other financing must be unconditional and must not be subject to any internal approvals, syndication requirements, diligence, or credit committee approvals, and shall have covenants and conditions reasonably acceptable to the Debtor; 

j. contain such financial and other information to allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to the Qualifying Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement, including, without limitation, such financial and other information supporting the Qualifying Bidder’s ability to comply with the requirements of adequate assurance of future performance under section 365(f)(2)(B) and, if applicable, section 365(b)(3) of the Bankruptcy Code, including the Qualifying Bidder’s financial wherewithal and willingness to perform under any contracts and leases that are assumed and assigned to the Qualifying Bidder, in a form that allows the Debtor to serve such information on any counterparties to any contracts or leases potentially being assumed and assigned in connection with the Sale within one (1) business day after the Debtor’s receipt of such information; 

- k. (A) identify with particularity each and every executory contract and unexpired lease the assumption and assignment of which is a condition to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement; (B) provide for the payment of cure costs related to such executory contracts and unexpired leases, and (C) demonstrate that the Qualifying Bidder can provide adequate assurance of future performance under all such executory contracts and unexpired leases; 

- l. set forth the Qualifying Bidder’s intentions with respect to (i) hiring any of the Debtor’s current or former employees, and (ii) providing transition services to the Debtor; 

- m. include a commitment to close the transactions contemplated by the Alternative Agreement by no later than December 17, 2026;

- n. not request or entitle such Qualifying Bidder to any break-up fee, termination fee, expense reimbursement, or similar type of fee or payment unless such Qualifying Bidder is seeking to be designated as an Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser with respect to the Assets in its bid; 

- o. if the Alternative Agreement contemplates the exclusive license or purchase of the Disney Target Assets, or the purchase of Assets subject to any other Stalking Horse Agreement, the aggregate consideration proposed by the Qualifying Bidder must equal or exceed the sum of the amount of (1) the value offered by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement with respect to the Disney Target Assets or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement with respect to any Assets included in the bid, plus (2) any applicable Break-Up Fee, plus (3) $1,000,000 (collectively, the “Minimum Overbid Amount”); 

- p. not contain any contingencies of any kind, including, without limitation, contingencies related to financing, internal approval, or due diligence; 

- q. contain a written acknowledgement and representation that the Qualifying Bidder (i) has had an opportunity to conduct any and all due diligence regarding the Assets, (ii) has relied solely upon its own independent review, investigation or inspection of any documents and other information in making its Qualifying Bid, and (iii) did not rely upon any written or oral statements, representations, promises, warranties or guaranties whatsoever, whether express, implied, by operation of law or otherwise, regarding the Assets, or the completeness of any documents or other information provided in connection with the Bidding Procedures and the Sale. Such written acknowledgment and representation must include the following disclaimer: Buyer acknowledges that, should the Closing occur, Buyer shall acquire the Assets without any surviving representations or warranties, on an “as is” and “where is” basis, except as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement; 

- r. set forth each regulatory and third-party approval required for the Qualified Bidder to consummate the Sale, and the time within which the Qualified Bidder expects to receive such regulatory and thirdparty approvals, and the Debtor, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, may consider the timing of such approvals, and any actions the Qualified Bidder will take to ensure receipt of such approvals as promptly as possible, when considering the other Bid Requirements. Each Qualified Bidder must further agree that its legal counsel will coordinate in good faith with counsel to the

Debtor to provide pertinent information regarding the Qualified Bidder’s operations reasonably required to analyze issues arising with respect to any applicable regulatory requirements and to discuss and explain the Qualified Bidder’s regulatory analysis, strategy, and timeline for securing all applicable approvals as soon as reasonably practicable; 

- s. provide for the Qualifying Bidder to serve as a backup bidder (the “Back-Up Bidder”) if the Qualifying Bidder’s bid is the next highest and best bid (the “Back-Up Bid”) after the Winning Bid, in accordance with the terms of the Alternative Agreement; 

- t. include written evidence of authorization and approval from the Qualifying Bidder’s board of directors (or comparable governing body) with respect to the submission, execution, and delivery of the Alternative Agreement; 

- u. provide a good faith cash deposit (the “Deposit”) in an amount equal to ten percent (10%) of the aggregate purchase price provided for in the Alternative Agreement (or such additional amount as may be determined by the Debtor in its reasonable discretion); and 

- v. provide that in the event of the Qualifying Bidder’s breach of, or failure to perform under, the Alternative Agreement, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to pursue all available legal and equitable remedies, including, without limitation, retention of the Deposit as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform. 

A bid from a Qualifying Bidder satisfying all of the above requirements, as determined by the Debtor in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below), shall constitute a Qualifying Bid.  The Debtor reserves the right to work with any Qualifying Bidder in advance of the Auction to cure any deficiencies in a bid that is not initially deemed a Qualifying Bid. 

Each Qualifying Bidder submitting a bid shall be deemed to: (a) acknowledge and represent that it is bound by all of the terms and conditions of the Bidding Procedures; and (b) have waived the right to pursue a substantial contribution claim under section 503 of the Bankruptcy Code related in any way to the submission of its bid, the Bidding Procedures, and the Sale. 

# **9.** **<u>Bid Deadline</u>** 

A Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser that may be designated by the Debtor, that desires to make a bid shall deliver a written and electronic copy of its bid in both PDF and MS-WORD format to the Bidding Procedures Notice Parties so as to be received on or before **<u>November 20, 2026 at 5:00 p.m. (ET)</u>** <u>(the “Bid Deadline”);</u> _provided_ that the Debtor may extend the Bid Deadline in consultation with the Consultation Parties (defined below) without further order of the Court.  To the extent that the Bid Deadline is extended for all

parties, the Debtor shall file a notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case indicating the same. **Any party that does not submit a bid by the Bid Deadline will not be allowed to (a) submit any offer after the Bid Deadline, or (b) participate in the Auction.** 

# **10.** **<u>Evaluation of Qualifying Bids</u>** 

The Debtor shall make a determination regarding whether a timely submitted bid from a Qualifying Bidder is a Qualifying Bid. 

No later than **November 21, 2026** , the Debtor shall: (i) notify all Qualifying Bidders whether their respective bids have been determined to be Qualifying Bids; and (ii) determine which of the Qualifying Bids, at such time, is the highest or best bid for purposes of constituting the opening bid of the Auction (which must be at least equal to the Minimum Overbid Amount, the “Baseline Bid” and the Qualifying Bidder submitting the Baseline Bid, the “Baseline Bidder”), and shall promptly notify any Stalking Horse Purchaser and all Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids of the Baseline Bid. 

# **11.** **<u>No Qualifying Bids</u>** 

If no timely Qualifying Bids other than any Stalking Horse Purchaser’s Qualifying Bid are submitted on or before the Bid Deadline, the Debtor shall not hold an Auction with respect to the Assets covered by the applicable Stalking Horse Agreement and shall request at the Sale Hearing that the Court approve such Stalking Horse Agreement and the transactions contemplated thereunder. 

# **12.** **<u>Auction</u>** 

If the Debtor timely receives one or more Qualifying Bids for all or any subset of the Assets, including, without limitation, the Disney Target Assets, then the Debtor shall conduct an auction (the “Auction”).  Following the Auction, the Debtor will determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties, which Qualifying Bid is the highest or otherwise best bid for the Assets, which will be determined by considering, among other things, the following non-binding factors: (a) the amount and nature of the consideration, including any assumed liabilities; (b) the number, type and nature of any changes requested by the bidder in the form of Alternative Agreement as compared to any Stalking Horse Agreement designated by the Debtor with respect to the Assets subject to its bid; (c) the extent to which such modifications are likely to delay closing of the Sale and the cost to the Debtor and its estate of such modifications or delay; (d) the total consideration to be received by the Debtor and its estate; (e) the transaction structure and execution risk, including conditions to, timing of and certainty of closing, termination provisions, availability of financing and financial wherewithal to meet all commitments, and required governmental or other approval; (f) the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate; and (g) any other qualitative or quantitative factors the Debtor may reasonably deem reasonably appropriate under the circumstances. 

The Auction shall be governed by the following procedures: 

- (a) the Auction shall be held on **<u>November 23, 2026 at 10:00 a.m. (ET)</u>** at a location to be determined by the Debtor or virtually via video conference pursuant to

information to be timely provided by the Debtor to the Auction Participants (as defined below); 

- (b) only Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser(s) designated by the Debtor, and the other Qualifying Bidders with Qualifying Bids (together, the “Auction <u>Bidders”) shall be entitled to make any subsequent bids at the Auction;</u> 

- (c) the Auction Bidders shall appear at the Auction in-person (or via Zoom, if permitted) in the Debtor’s sole discretion, or through a duly authorized representative; 

- (d) only the Debtor, the Consultation Parties, and the Auction Bidders, together with the professional advisors to each of the foregoing parties, may attend the Auction (collectively, the “Auction Participants”); _provided_ that any creditors or parties in interest in the chapter 11 case may request permission in writing to attend the Auction no later than Noon (ET) two (2) days prior to the Auction and the Debtor, in its sole discretion, may reasonably determine to grant or deny such permission; 

- (e) the Debtor and its professional advisors shall direct and preside over the Auction, which shall be transcribed; 

- (f) prior to start of the Auction, each of the Auction Bidders shall confirm on the record of the proceedings that they have not engaged in any collusion with respect to the Bidding Procedures, the Auction, or the Sale; 

- (g) bidding shall commence at the amount of the Baseline Bid, and the Auction Bidders may submit successive bids in increments of $500,000, _provided_ that: (i) each such successive bid must be a Qualifying Bid; and (ii) the Debtor shall retain the right to modify the bid increment requirements at the Auction; 

- (h) in any successive bid by Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser designated by the Debtor, the amount of the applicable Break-Up Fee shall be included in the calculation of such bid; 

- (i) the Auction may include individual negotiations with any of the Auction Bidders, but all bids shall be made on the record and in the presence of all of the Auction Bidders; 

- (j) all material terms of the bid that is deemed to be the highest and best bid for each round of bidding shall be fully disclosed to the Auction Bidders, and the Debtor shall use reasonable efforts to clarify any and all questions that the Auction Bidders may have regarding the Debtor’s announcement of the then-current highest and best bid; 

- (k) the Debtor and its professional advisors may employ and announce at the Auction additional procedural rules that are reasonable under the circumstances ( _e.g._ , the amount of time allotted to make subsequent bids) for conducting the Auction, provided that such rules are (i) not inconsistent with the Bankruptcy Code, the

Federal Rules of Bankruptcy Procedure, the Local Rules of the United States Bankruptcy Court for the District of Delaware, or any applicable order of the Court, including, without limitation, the Bidding Procedures Order, and (ii) disclosed to the Auction Bidders in advance of their application; 

- (l) each Auction Bidder shall (i) be deemed to have waived any right to a jury trial in connection with, and consented and submitted to the exclusive jurisdiction of the Court over, any actions or proceedings arising from or relating the Bidding Procedures, the Sale, the Auction and the construction and enforcement of the contemplated transaction documents of the Auction Bidders, (ii) bring any such action or proceeding in the Court, and (iii) be deemed to have consented to the Court entering a final judgment determining any such action or proceeding and that such final judgment in any such action or proceeding, including all appeals, shall be conclusive and may be enforced in other jurisdictions (including any foreign jurisdictions) by suit on the judgment or in any other manner provided by applicable law; 

- (m) the Auction Bidders shall have the right to make additional modifications to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, in conjunction with each Qualifying Bid submitted in each round of bidding during the Auction, _provided_ that (i) any such modifications on an aggregate basis and viewed in whole, shall not, in the Debtor’s discretion be less favorable to the Debtor and its estate than the terms of, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, and (ii) each Qualifying Bid shall constitute an irrevocable offer and shall be binding on the Auction Bidder submitting such bid until such party shall have submitted a subsequent Qualifying Bid at the Auction or the conclusion of the Sale Hearing, whichever occurs sooner, unless such bid is selected as the Winning Bid or the Back-Up Bid, which shall remain binding as provided for herein; 

- (n) the Debtor shall have the right to request any additional financial information that will allow the Debtor to make a reasonable determination as to an Auction Bidder’s financial and other capabilities to consummate the transactions contemplated by, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement, as may be amended during the Auction, and any further information that the Debtor may believe is reasonably necessary to clarify and evaluate any bid made by an Auction Bidder during the Auction; 

- (o) upon the conclusion of the Auction, the Debtor shall determine, in consultation with the Consultation Parties and subject to Court approval, the offer or offers for the Assets that is or are the highest or otherwise best from among the Qualifying Bids submitted at the Auction for all or substantially all or any subset of the Assets (the “Winning Bids”).  In making this decision, the Debtor shall consider the amount of the purchase price, the assumption of liabilities, the likelihood of the bidder’s ability to close a transaction and the timing thereof, the number, type and

nature of any changes to the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or any Alternative Agreement submitted with the Winning Bid, as applicable, requested by each bidder, and the net benefit to the Debtor’s estate.  The bidder submitting such Winning Bid at the Auction shall become a “Winning Bidder,” and shall have such rights and responsibilities of the purchaser as set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement, as applicable.  The Debtor may, in its business judgment, designate a Back-Up Bid (and the corresponding Back-Up Bidder) to purchase the Assets in the event that the Winning Bidder does not close the Sale; 

- (p) if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall allow one (1) Winning Bidder to interview and make employment offers to any employees of Disney formerly employed by the Debtor who wish to consider opportunities with such Winning Bidder and shall work in good faith with such Winning Bidder to transition employees who accept an offer to the Winning Bidder; _provided_ that Disney may designate up to 20% of such employees as outside the scope of such process, and Disney need not make available or allow the Winning Bidder (or any other party) the right to interview or make employment offers to such employees; 

- (q) if the Winning Bidder is not Disney, Disney shall be entitled to the Break-Up Fee; 

- (r) as soon as reasonably practicable after completion of the Auction, the Debtor will file with the Court and serve on the Transaction Notice Parties a notice setting forth the results of the Auction (the “Notice of Winning Bidder”), which will (a) identify the Winning Bidder and the Back-Up Bidder, if applicable; (b) include a copy of the Winning Bid and the Back-Up Bid, if applicable, or a summary of the material terms of such bid, including any proposed assumption and assignment of Contracts contemplated thereby; and (c) set forth the Adequate Assurance Objection Deadline, the date, time, and location of the Sale Hearing and any other relevant dates or other information necessary to reasonably apprise the Transaction Notice Parties of the outcome of the Auction; and 

- (s) prior to the Sale Hearing, the Winning Bidder shall complete and execute all agreements, contracts, instruments and other documents evidencing and containing the terms and conditions upon which the Winning Bid was made; 

**THE WINNING BID AND ANY BACK-UP BID AND THEIR RELATED STALKING HORSE TRANSACTION AGREEMENT, ADDITIONAL STALKING HORSE AGREEMENT, OR ALTERNATIVE AGREEMENT, AS APPLICABLE, SHALL CONSTITUTE AN IRREVOCABLE OFFER AND BE BINDING ON THE WINNING BIDDER AND THE BACK-UP BIDDER, RESPECTIVELY, FROM THE TIME THE BID IS SUBMITTED UNTIL TWO (2) BUSINESS DAYS AFTER THE SALE HAS CLOSED. EACH QUALIFYING BID THAT IS NOT THE WINNING BID OR THE BACK-UP BID SHALL BE DEEMED WITHDRAWN AND TERMINATED AT THE CONCLUSION OF THE SALE HEARING.**

# **13.** **<u>Sale Hearing</u>** 

The Winning Bid and any Back-Up Bid and, as applicable, the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement, or the Alternative Agreement related to such bids will be subject to approval by the Court.  The Sale Hearing to approve the Winning Bid and any Back-Up and the applicable asset purchase agreements of such bids shall take place on **<u>November 24, 2026] at [●] (ET)</u>** . 

The Sale Hearing and any additional deadlines established in connection with these Bidding Procedures may be adjourned by the Debtor, in consultation with any Consultation Parties, Disney, and any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser, or in the event Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then in consultation with the Winning Bidder(s), from time to time without further notice to creditors or other parties in interest other than by announcement of the adjournment in open court on the date scheduled for the Sale Hearing, or by filing a hearing agenda or notice on the docket of the chapter 11 case. 

At the Sale Hearing, the Debtor will seek entry of an order, substantially in the form of the Sale Order that, among other things: (i) authorizes and approves the Sale to Disney, any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser or, in the event that Disney or any Additional Stalking Horse Purchaser is not designated as a Winning Bidder, then to the Winning Bidder(s), pursuant to the terms and conditions set forth in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement, any Additional Stalking Horse Agreement or the Alternative Agreement submitted by the Winning Bidder, as applicable. 

# **14.** **<u>Back-Up Bidder</u>** 

Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, in the event that the Winning Bidder fails to close the Sale on or before the date that is two weeks following entry of the Sale Order (or such date as may be extended by the Debtor), the Back-Up Bid will be deemed to be the Winning Bid, the Back-Up Bidder will be deemed to be the Winning Bidder, and the Debtor shall be authorized to close the Sale to the Back-Up Bidder subject to the terms of the Back-Up Bid without the need for further order of the Court and without the need for further notice to any interested parties; _provided, however,_ that Disney, if operating as the Back-Up Bidder, shall not be subject to any penalties, and, Disney shall be permitted to terminate the Sale unilaterally if the Sale does not close on or prior to December 17, 2026 and the failure to close is not on account of Disney’s material breach; _provided, further,_ that any duties and related requirements for Disney to serve as the Back-Up Bidder, or milestones in connection therewith, shall be subject to the terms of the last bid Disney submitted at the Auction. 

# **15.** **<u>Return of Deposits</u>** 

All Deposits shall be returned to each bidder not selected by the Debtor as the Winning Bidder no later than five (5) business days following the closing of the Sale, including that certain $2,000,000 fully refundable deposit to be paid to Disney to the extent Disney is not selected as the Winning Bidder.  The deposit of the Winning Bidder or, if the Sale is closed with the Back-Up Bidder, the deposit of the Back-Up Bidder, shall be applied to the purchase price for the Sale.  If the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) fails to consummate the Sale because of a breach or failure to perform on the part of such

bidder, then, subject to the terms of any Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or any Alternative Agreement, as applicable, the Debtor and its estate shall be entitled to retain the Deposit of the Winning Bidder (or, if the Sale is to be closed with the Back-Up Bidder, then the Back-Up Bidder) as part of the damages resulting to the Debtor and its estate for such breach or failure to perform.<sup>4</sup> 

# **16.** **<u>Bidding Procedures Notice Parties</u>** 

The term “Bidding Procedures Notice Parties” shall mean: (a) proposed counsel to the Debtor, Wilson Sonsini Goodrich & Rosati, P.C., 222 Delaware Avenue, Suite 800, Wilmington, Delaware 19801, Attn: Erin R. Fay (efay@wsgr.com) and Shane M. Meisler (ron.meisler@skadden.com), Bryan Uelk (bryan.uelk@skadden.com)_and Samuel J. Cammer (samuel.cammer@skadden.com)); and (d) counsel to any official committee of unsecured creditors that may be appointed in the chapter 11 case. 

# **17.** **<u>The Consultation Parties</u>** 

The Debtor shall consult with any official committee of unsecured creditors appointed by the Bankruptcy Court and counsel to such committee (the “Consultation Parties”) as explicitly provided for in the Bidding Procedures; provided, however, that the Debtor shall not be required to consult with any Consultation Party (and its advisors) that is a Qualified Bidder. For the avoidance of doubt, any consultation rights afforded to the Consultation Parties by these Bidding Procedures shall not limit the Debtor’s discretion in any way and shall not include the right to veto any decision made by the Debtor in the exercise of its reasonable business judgment. 

# **18.** **<u>Fiduciary Out</u>** 

Nothing in these Bidding Procedures will require any director or officer of the Debtor to take any action, or to refrain from taking any action, with respect to these Bidding Procedures, to the extent such director or officer reasonably determines in good faith, in consultation with outside counsel, that taking such action, or refraining from taking such action, as applicable, would result in a violation of applicable law or be inconsistent with his or her fiduciary obligations. 

# **19.** **<u>Reservation of Rights</u>** 

Except as otherwise provided in the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement or the Bidding Procedures Order, the Debtor may, and reserves the right to, modify the Bidding Procedures at or prior to the Auction, including, without limitation, to modify, extend, enlarge, revise, or otherwise alter any or all of the deadlines set forth herein or related hereto including, without limitation, with respect to the marketing process, the submission of bids, the designation of Additional Stalking 

> 4 For the avoidance of doubt, the Debtor’s retention of a Deposit shall not constitute a waiver of any of the Debtor’s legal or equitable rights relating to a Winning Bidder’s or Back-Up Bidder’s breach or failure to perform, and all such rights and remedies are reserved.

Horse Purchasers, Qualified Bidders, and Auction Participants, the Assumption and Assignment Procedures, the Auction, the Sale Hearing, and the closing of any Sale  to Winning Bidders or Back-Up Bidders, as applicable, as well as to modify bidding increments, waive terms and conditions set forth herein with respect to any or all Potential Bidders (including, without limitation, the Bid Requirements), impose additional terms and conditions with respect to any or all of the Potential Bidders, adjourn or cancel the Auction at or prior to the Auction, and adjourn the Sale Hearing. Nothing herein shall obligate the Debtors to consummate or pursue any transaction with a Qualifying Bidder, other than Disney subject to, and in accordance with the terms of these Bidding Procedures and the Stalking Horse Transaction Agreement.
